## E0001 TRAINER_BRENDAN_ROUTE_103_MUDKIP class=rival
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Pikachu uses Fake Out only on an interruptible threat or Electroweb when the speed drop enables its partner; the starter attacks or sets up only behind a genuinely safe turn. On turn two, exploit the created speed/position advantage rather than repeat Fake Out. Alternate deployment: Pikachu and Shroomish trade immediate starter pressure for disruption and recovery. Reserves & disruption: Taillow is an Orb-activation finisher, not a guaranteed turn-one Guts attacker. Preserve an attack line when sleep is blocked; respect the actual unchosen regional starter. Counterplay & audit: Grass/powder immunity, Electric immunity, priority denial, double targeting, and denying free setup. Disposition: Keep the printed Hoenn seeds; regional generated starter sets require the explicit expansion register, not a false claim that six printed aliases cover all generations.
crack: Grass/powder immunity, Electric immunity, priority denial, double targeting, and denying free setup. Disposition: Keep the printed Hoenn seeds; regional generated starter sets require the explicit expansion register, not a false claim that six printed aliases cover all generations.

## E0002 TRAINER_CALVIN_1 class=regular
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Poochyena uses Taunt or Helping Hand according to whether denying setup or securing damage matters more; Zigzagoon drums only when its post-cost HP and legal Gluttony/Figy recovery survive the opposing pair. On the next turn, cash in with Extreme Speed rather than continue setting up. Alternate deployment: Hoothoot Tailwind plus Starly gives an immediate attacking mode without Belly Drum. Reserves & disruption: If Drum is unsafe, attack with Zigzagoon or move to the bird pair. Figy is compatible with Adamant; Unnerve, item suppression or removal can deny recovery. Rattled is an opponent-dependent bonus, not an activation plan. Counterplay & audit: Priority immunity/denial, Fake Out on the support, physical mitigation, berry denial or focused damage before Drum resolves. Disposition: RETAIN; Figy replaces Sitrus to make Gluttony meaningful. Benchmark the first forced route fight against multiple starter pairs, not just a compliant setup fixture.
crack: Priority immunity/denial, Fake Out on the support, physical mitigation, berry denial or focused damage before Drum resolves. Disposition: RETAIN; Figy replaces Sitrus to make Gluttony meaningful. Benchmark the first forced route fight against multiple starter pairs, not just a compliant setup fixture.

ZIGZAGOON @FIGY_BERRY GLUTTONY ADAMANT 252/252/4/0/0/0 6 | BELLY_DRUM, EXTREME_SPEED, SEED_BOMB, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255

## E0003 TRAINER_RICK class=regular
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Scarf Spinarak chooses String Shot when the two-stage Speed drops let Dwebble or Karrablast act usefully before the player; Electroweb offers damaging one-stage control when that is enough. Turn two switches Spinarak when its locked support action no longer produces value. Alternate deployment: Fast control beside Karrablast creates stronger immediate offensive conversion. Reserves & disruption: Dewpider supplies the special Water lane when physical attacks are contained. Do not let a Choice-locked utility move become repeated empty turns. Re-evaluate both targets, turn order, protection, stat-loss immunity, Contrary and retaliatory abilities. Counterplay & audit: Ground immunity blocks Electroweb damage but does not inherently block String Shot. Taunt, Magic Bounce, Clear Amulet, White Herb, speed reversal and punishing switches offer counterplay. Disposition: RETAIN; String Shot replaces Toxic Thread for direct paired speed control, while the Scarf control-and-switch identity remains.
crack: Ground immunity blocks Electroweb damage but does not inherently block String Shot. Taunt, Magic Bounce, Clear Amulet, White Herb, speed reversal and punishing switches offer counterplay. Disposition: RETAIN; String Shot replaces Toxic Thread for direct paired speed control, while the Scarf control-and-switch identity remains.

## E0004 TRAINER_ALLEN class=regular
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Pikachu can buy Paras a Spore or use its own damage while Paras selects Wide Guard against real spread pressure. On turn two, attack or reposition; do not automatically shield both supports. Alternate deployment: Pikachu and Bunnelby create a faster damage-focused opening; Mienfoo can supply Fake Out while Paras acts or pressure Rock/Steel targets that resist Bunnelby's Normal attacks. Reserves & disruption: Mienfoo's Eviolite and Regenerator support repeatable U-turn positioning with Bunnelby. Choose a legal useful reserve, account for entry hazards and the incoming attack, and credit Regenerator only when a switch actually occurs. Drain Punch supplies an attacking recovery line; Knock Off disrupts equipment. Paras is not protected from single-target double targeting by Wide Guard. Counterplay & audit: Powder immunity, spread-versus-targeted mix-ups, Ghost immunity to Fake Out/Fighting attacks, Psychic/Fairy pressure on Mienfoo, trapping and punishing the incoming reserve. Disposition: RETAIN; Mienfoo replaces the unsupported Truant/Choice Band Slakoth role. Preserve Bunnelby's Frustration friendship zero. Do not force switches or Fake Out when an immediate attack is better.
crack: Powder immunity, spread-versus-targeted mix-ups, Ghost immunity to Fake Out/Fighting attacks, Psychic/Fairy pressure on Mienfoo, trapping and punishing the incoming reserve. Disposition: RETAIN; Mienfoo replaces the unsupported Truant/Choice Band Slakoth role. Preserve Bunnelby's Frustration friendship zero. Do not force switches or Fake Out when an immediate attack is better.

## E0005 TRAINER_TIANA class=casual
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Skitty chooses Fake Out when interruption is necessary, or timely Fake Tears on the same target Swirlix can exploit with special damage; Helping Hand remains the damage-support alternative when defense drops are blocked or less useful. Do not spend a turn lowering Special Defense after the relevant partner attack has already happened. Alternate deployment: Buneary threatens Klutz-assisted Flame Orb Switcheroo while Swirlix attacks. Reserves & disruption: Eevee can receive a Wish on a planned switch or keep itself alive for another attack. An unconsumed Sitrus does not grant Unburden, and switching removes the earned speed bonus. Fake Tears must respect protection, reflection, stat-loss immunity, Contrary, White Herb and retaliatory abilities; it does not help Eevee's physical attacks. Counterplay & audit: Taunt, Magic Bounce, Clear Amulet, status immunity, item protection where supported, and burst damage before Wish resolves. Disposition: RETAIN; Fake Tears replaces Sing to give the whimsical support core a reliable special-damage partnership. No automatic credit for Unburden or guaranteed Orb disruption.
crack: Taunt, Magic Bounce, Clear Amulet, status immunity, item protection where supported, and burst damage before Wish resolves. Disposition: RETAIN; Fake Tears replaces Sing to give the whimsical support core a reliable special-damage partnership. No automatic credit for Unburden or guaranteed Orb disruption.

## E0006 TRAINER_BILLY class=regular
strategy: RAIN
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Lotad chooses Rain Dance only when Ducklett's next attack and its own rain turns repay the support action; Ducklett can use Icy Wind or a safe attack while weather is established. Turn two exploits rain instead of resetting it unnecessarily. Alternate deployment: Lotad and Tandemaus combine weather pressure with Encore/Population Bomb rather than another Water attacker. Reserves & disruption: Ditto is a reactive reserve, not an arbitrary turn-one transformation. Ducklett Surf still hits Lotad despite its resistance; use a safe attack when ally damage loses the exchange. Counterplay & audit: Weather replacement, Wide Guard, Grass/Electric pressure, and denying a favorable Ditto target. Disposition: Preserve the rain lesson; attach an ally-damage decision to Surf rather than claiming rain partners are immune.
crack: Weather replacement, Wide Guard, Grass/Electric pressure, and denying a favorable Ditto target. Disposition: Preserve the rain lesson; attach an ally-damage decision to Surf rather than claiming rain partners are immune.

## E0007 TRAINER_DARIAN class=regular
strategy: SETUP,PRESSURE
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Chinchou's Discharge is a safe ally line beside Ground-type Barboach while Barboach either attacks or uses Dragon Dance behind adequate survival. Turn two evaluates the new speed order before spending another setup turn. Alternate deployment: Tentacool resists neither Ground nor Electric but takes Grass neutrally and threatens Grass attackers with Sludge Bomb. Acid Spray supports an actual special follow-up from Chinchou or Clamperl; value same-turn support only when it lands before the partner attack. Clamperl retains the special Shell Smash mode. Reserves & disruption: Eviolite gives Tentacool working bulk; Liquid Ooze punishes eligible draining attacks, not all Grass damage. Muddy Water attacks both opponents without allied damage. Discharge damages Tentacool and Clamperl: re-evaluate every switch and choose a safer action when warranted. Counterplay & audit: Ground/Electric pressure on Tentacool, Grass pressure on the other three, Taunt, immediate damage and revenge priority. Disposition: Approved Corphish replacement removes the universal Grass weakness while preserving the Chinchou/Barboach engine and marine theme. Shared support forecasts honor order and stat-drop blockers; no forced Acid Spray or Discharge script.
crack: Ground/Electric pressure on Tentacool, Grass pressure on the other three, Taunt, immediate damage and revenge priority. Disposition: Approved Corphish replacement removes the universal Grass weakness while preserving the Chinchou/Barboach engine and marine theme. Shared support forecasts honor order and stat-drop blockers; no forced Acid Spray or Discharge script.

BARBOACH @EVIOLITE OBLIVIOUS ADAMANT 252/252/4/0/0/0 4 | DRAGON_DANCE, WATERFALL, HIGH_HORSEPOWER, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255

## E0008 TRAINER_CINDY_1 class=regular
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Snubbull's Intimidate softens eligible attackers while Milcery selects Helping Hand or an active Fairy attack. Turn two should exploit a damage threshold or heal a relevant slot, not maintain two harmless supports. Alternate deployment: Snubbull and Furfrou offer a physical-pressure mode with U-turn positioning. Reserves & disruption: Slakoth is a one-hit reserve with an exit; Aroma Veil protects only its enumerated effects and is not a blanket anti-disruption shield. Counterplay & audit: Special offense, Defiant/Competitive, Taunt outside Aroma Veil's protected set, and targeting Milcery. Disposition: Keep the species identity; reject any AI trace that values pure durability above protecting an actual threat.
crack: Special offense, Defiant/Competitive, Taunt outside Aroma Veil's protected set, and targeting Milcery. Disposition: Keep the species identity; reject any AI trace that values pure durability above protecting an actual threat.

## E0009 TRAINER_LYLE class=casual
strategy: PRESSURE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Nymble uses First Impression only on its eligible entry turn and otherwise attacks or pivots; Sewaddle supplies Bug/Grass damage or Struggle Bug mitigation. ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Spritzee sets Trick Room only after checking survival and legal disruption; Gothita attacks or uses priority Helping Hand for a useful partner damage threshold. Zero-Speed Brave Trapinch uses Life Orb Earthquake while airborne Levitate Baltoy supplies special damage or necessary Trick Room renewal. Arena Trap restricts eligible grounded foes, not Flying/Levitate or other valid escape routes. Reserves & disruption: Earthquake must be reappraised beside Spritzee/Gothita or a grounded/ability-suppressed Baltoy; Protect is conditional, not an automatic ally-damage excuse. First Impression works only on Trapinch's entry turn. Helping Hand supports both Trapinch's physical attacks and the special partners, regardless of their relative Speed; it does not make Earthquake safe for Gothita. The old slower-than-Spritzee Fake Tears line under Trick Room is retired. Baltoy remains a second setter if Spritzee falls. Counterplay & audit: Concentrated attacks, Dark/Ghost coverage, slower opposing threats, grounding/suppression and disruption outside Aroma Veil. Disposition: Approved Trapinch replaces Fidough; preserve separate single-owner doubles and actual per-slot Speed IVs. Individual levels await native difficulty calibration.
crack: Concentrated attacks, Dark/Ghost coverage, slower opposing threats, grounding/suppression and disruption outside Aroma Veil. ## E0045 TRAINER_LOLA_1 class=regular
strategy: TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: A tuber's pool party on the Route 109 beach: four little Water Pokemon, one speed engine, and a Soul-Heart cleaner. First two useful turns: Focus Sash Ducklett is guaranteed to get Tailwind up, then uses Helping Hand on the partner's knockout hit or Scald for chip; it must not reset Tailwind while the first one is still running. Goldeen attacks beside it: Megahorn hits the Grass types that beat a Water team, Knock Off strips an item, and Lightning Rod pulls single-target Electric attacks away from its partners and absorbs them. Alternate deployment: Eviolite Seel is the physical reserve - Fake Out buys a turn for Tailwind or Luvdisc, Drill Run answers the Electric types, and Aqua Jet picks off a weakened target. Reserves & disruption: Luvdisc keeps the Soul-Heart cleanup mode - every knockout on either side raises its Special Attack - with Icy Wind speed control, Draining Kiss sustain and Charm against physical attackers. Stats: Timid HP/Speed Ducklett is a support setter, not an attacker; Goldeen and Seel are Eviolite physical attackers. Could it be cooler? Lola used to be a sun garden - Cherrim, Leafeon and Ponyta - with Luvdisc the only Water type on a Tuber. Each slot keeps its job in the water: Ducklett takes Cherrim's support-setter role with Tailwind in place of Sunny Day, Goldeen takes Leafeon's physical attacker slot, and Seel takes Ponyta's Eviolite reserve. Tailwind rather than rain keeps her a separate fight from Chandler's rain team next door. Level offsets carried by role: Ducklett takes Cherrim's +1 on the same Focus Sash, Goldeen +2 as an Eviolite pre-evolution in Leafeon's slot, Luvdisc +4 and Seel +3 unchanged.
crack: Electric and Grass attacks answer the team - but aim Electric at the side Goldeen is not on, or remove Goldeen first, because Lightning Rod redirects it. Electric hits Ducklett four times over and Rock double, and Fake Out or a multi-hit move breaks its Focus Sash. Taunt stops Tailwind and Helping Hand; outlast the four Tailwind turns behind Protect, and keep Luvdisc from collecting knockouts.

## E0063 TRAINER_JACLYN class=regular
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Jaclyn keeps the trapped-Encore, Simple Stored Power puzzle and now has a Psychic that Dark types cannot wall. First two useful turns: Wobbuffet uses Encore to lock in a completed support move, or Safeguard against a relevant status line, while Swoobat safely uses Calm Mind or attacks. Simple doubles Calm Mind to +2 Special Attack and Special Defense, and Stored Power then attacks; Shadow Tag traps non-Ghost foes but never redirects attacks. Counter and Mirror Coat punish the foe that hits Wobbuffet hardest. Alternate deployment: Kadabra gives immediate fast pressure, with Dazzling Gleam for Dark and Fighting foes. Reserves & disruption: Gallade replaces Musharna, the overlap with Brandi. Justified raises its Attack when a Dark move hits it, so the Knock Offs, Foul Plays and Sucker Punches that answer the other three feed it; Sacred Sword hits Dark and Steel foes for super-effective damage and ignores their stat boosts, Psycho Cut handles Fighting and Poison foes, and Knock Off punishes Ghosts. Lum Berry cures the Will-O-Wisp or sleep a Dark/Ghost support would use to neutralize it.
crack: Flying, Ghost and Fairy attacks hit Gallade for super-effective damage, and a Ghost type that never uses a Dark move gives Justified nothing. ## E0101 TRAINER_GEORGIA class=regular
strategy: ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: NONE
tactic: ACTIVATE DUSKULL SHADOW_SNEAK METANG

Duskull then spreads Will-O-Wisp onto the physical threat and Pain Splits back its HP. Duskull uses Eviolite, HP/Defense, Careful and zero Attack IV to soften the friendly hit. Reserves: Azumarill is the class's answer to the Fire and Dark attackers that hit Duskull, Metang and Hattrem super effectively: Water/Fairy resists both types, Huge Power Liquidation hits either one super effectively, and Aqua Jet picks off a faster Fire or Dark attacker that the boosted Metang has already weakened; Play Rough covers Fighting and Dragon answers. Hattrem is the Magic Bounce support that reflects status and hazards, heals the pair with Life Dew, and adds Dazzling Gleam as the second answer to Dark. Every member keeps Protect, so the kids can scout a doubled target on the turn Metang is boosting.
crack: Deny the activation (Fake Out or remove Duskull, or Knock Off the Weakness Policy first), then focus Metang before it moves at +2; Grass and Electric attacks remove Azumarill, which is slow and has no recovery beyond its Sitrus Berry; Intimidate and burns shrink both physical attackers; Ghost and Dark attacks still hit Duskull, Metang and Hattrem super effectively, so pairing a Dark attacker with a Grass or Electric partner leaves Azumarill unable to cover both; Poison attacks also hit Azumarill.

Dry Skin heals Heliolisk every turn in rain and Glare still works, because Electric Terrain blocks only sleep; Heatmor's Thunder Punch and Hisuian Arcanine's Wild Charge are grounded and gain the terrain's 30 percent, and both hit the Water types the rain favours. Heliolisk's Air Balloon keeps it off the ground, so its own Thunderbolt does not gain the terrain boost while the balloon holds. In clear weather Arcanine's Wild Charge is still the Water answer its Passho Berry used to buy time for.
crack: Fighting attacks such as Close Combat hit Heliolisk for super-effective damage and still hit it through Air Balloon; popping the balloon exposes it to Ground attacks. Volt Absorb, Lightning Rod and Ground types blank Thunderbolt. In a thunderstorm, Ground types blank all three Electric attacks and the Fire half is already halved - bring one. Chip Typhlosion before Eruption, exploit its Choice lock, Wide Guard the spread Fire attacks and pressure the Fire members with Ground or Rock attacks rather than a lone Water spread attack.

Stats: Volcarona shifts to HP/Speed as the setter, Magnezone remains HP/Defense, Darmanitan takes the lower level for Scarf, Marowak keeps Mamoswine's physical spread. Could it be cooler? Water and Ground attacks hit Darmanitan and Marowak once the sun is gone; Magnezone takes four times from Ground. Exploit Darmanitan's Scarf lock, and answer Magnezone with special or Ground attacks - Iron Defense does nothing against special damage.

## E0138 TRAINER_LUCY_LAVARIDGE class=brain
strategy: MEGA_REVEAL,SETUP,ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: 1
plan: The Pike Queen's serpents, rebuilt as six so her Mega actually fights. First two useful turns: Absol leads in the sole Mega slot and MEGA_REVEAL evolves it on its first turn; Magic Bounce then reflects Spore, Will-O-Wisp, Taunt, Encore, Thunder Wave and Parting Shot aimed at it. It uses Sucker Punch only when the target's likely action is an attack, Knock Off to strip Eviolite/Sitrus/Choice items and Superpower into Steel, Rock, Dark and Normal threats. Sandaconda is the new answer to her old counters: Ground typing ignores Electric attacks, High Horsepower hits Electric/Steel/Fire/Poison/Rock foes for super-effective damage, Rock Slide pressures Flying and Fire pairs, and Coil (+1 Attack/Defense/accuracy) is taken only on a turn Absol is drawing the fire. Passho Berry blunts the first Water hit aimed at it. Alternate deployment: Milotic replaces either lead as the bulky special pivot; Competitive turns an Intimidate, Icy Wind or Parting Shot drop into +2 Special Attack; Scald's burn blunts physical attackers, Icy Wind is spread speed control, Ice Beam covers Grass/Flying/Dragon/Ground and Recover keeps it cycling. Seviper's Life Orb Sludge Bomb, Flamethrower and Giga Drain cover Fairy, Steel and Water/Ground; Glare paralyzes a fast non-Electric threat so Drapion and Sandaconda can move first. Reserves & disruption: Drapion's Scope Lens plus Cross Poison or Night Slash is a 50% critical rate and Sniper makes each critical hit 2.25x; it has no setup and needs no ally turn. Shuckle is the shell game: Guard Split onto Mega Absol averages their Defense and Special Defense, turning the glass Mega into a wall that still hits; Power Split on the strongest foe averages its Attack and Special Attack with Shuckle's tiny ones; Encore locks a foe into Protect or a wasted setup move and Helping Hand boosts a partner's finishing hit. Glare cannot paralyze Electric types, Sucker Punch fails into Protect and status, and Sturdy does not survive Mold Breaker.
crack: Target Absol on turn one with priority, Steel or Fairy damage before its Mega bulk from Guard Split exists, and never feed it status to bounce. Spread Water, Grass or Ice attacks remove Sandaconda once Passho is spent; Ground attacks punish Seviper and Drapion; Electric attacks punish Milotic. Taunt shuts Shuckle out completely because it carries no attack, and Mold Breaker ignores its Sturdy. Lucario-style Steel/Fighting attackers resist Absol's Dark hits and Drapion's Night Slash. Burn- and paralysis-immune leads blunt Scald and Glare, and Battle Armor or Shell Armor denies Drapion's critical hits.

## E0152 TRAINER_HEIDI class=ace
strategy: SUN,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: A Picnicker who refuses to eat in a sandstorm: she brings her own sunshine. First two useful turns: Tropius uses Sunny Day, which replaces the Route 111 sandstorm, while Lurantis uses Superpower, whose stat drops Contrary turns into Attack and Defense gains. Turn two attacks through the real boosts under the sun rather than setting up again. Roles: Tropius is the picnic basket - under sun Harvest regrows its eaten Sitrus Berry every turn, and Solar Blade fires without its charge turn; Dual Wingbeat is its answer to the Bug and Fighting attackers that threaten the Grass half. Sun also makes Lurantis's Synthesis restore two-thirds of its HP instead of a quarter in sand, so the sustain the team was missing in the storm comes back. Alternate deployment: Solrock is the sun she carries with her and the answer to the Fire that her own sunshine feeds. It resists Fire and Flying, so a sun-boosted Heat Wave aimed at the Grass pair barely marks it, and it punishes the Fire lead with single-target Stone Edge, which Wide Guard cannot stop and which hits a Fire and Flying attacker four times over. Zen Headbutt takes out the Fighting partner that brings Wide Guard, Levitate keeps it clear of Ground coverage, and Morning Sun restores two-thirds of its HP under the team's sun. Reserves & disruption: Stamina Mudsdale is the Ground-type anchor that the sandstorm never touched, with High Horsepower, Heavy Slam, Rock Slide and Close Combat as physical coverage; it has no Body Press in this build. Without the sun the team still fights, but it takes sand chip and heals less.
crack: Replace the sun with your own weather, or remove Tropius before Sunny Day; take Solrock out first with Water, Grass, Dark, Ghost or Steel attacks, and then Fire and Flying pressure on the Grass members works again, boosted by her own sun; Intimidate weakens a team that attacks only physically; Stone Edge can miss; Rock Slide and Ice attacks hit Tropius; Wide Guard blocks Mudsdale's Rock Slide; Water, Grass and Fighting answer Mudsdale; Haze or Clear Smog undoes the Contrary gains.

Turn two cashes the boost or the speed drop rather than setting up again. Turn two keeps Sceptile attacking under Unburden; Protect only against a committed double target. ## E0159 TRAINER_ROMAN class=regular
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: A Hiker's mountain crew: three Rock and Ground bodies and a Clodsire dug out of the wet riverbank, built to punish the Water, Grass and Fairy attackers the player brings for rocks. Water Absorb makes Clodsire immune to Water and heals it for a quarter of its HP when Water hits it, so a Water spread move only lands on its partner; Poison and Ground typing resists Fairy and shrugs off Electric; Poison Jab hits Fairy and Grass attackers super-effectively. First two useful turns: Donphan leads with High Horsepower or Knock Off while Clodsire Poison Jabs the Fairy or Grass attacker, or High Horsepowers the Steel or Electric one, and Recovers when chip adds up. Turn two swings Donphan's Ice Spinner into the Grass or Flying attacker while Clodsire keeps soaking the Water. Reserves: Garganacl lands Salt Cure on the Water or Steel answer, which then loses a quarter of its HP every turn it stays in, Body Presses off its Defense and Recovers when the pressure is on it; Purifying Salt makes it immune to status and halves Ghost damage. Golem's Weakness Policy is reactive: a super-effective Water or Grass hit it survives through Sturdy doubles its attacking stats, and Heavy Slam hits Fairies. Donphan's Sturdy is spent the first time Life Orb recoil lands, so treat it as the immediate physical pressure, not a wall.
crack: Grass is the clean answer: it hits Donphan, Golem and Garganacl super-effectively and Clodsire only neutrally, and Clodsire's Poison Jab is its only reply. Psychic hits Clodsire super-effectively. Keep Water attacks off Clodsire and spread them only after it falls, and do not hand Golem its Weakness Policy with a single weak hit. Fighting and Steel pressure on Garganacl once Salt Cure is known; Ice and Water on Donphan. Taunt stops both Recovers, and Garganacl's Body Press is only as strong as its Defense.

## E0163 TRAINER_ALAN class=regular
strategy: SAND,REDIRECTION
mega_slots: 3
plan: A desert ruin room that owns its weather and eats the rain. First two useful turns: Tyranitar and Cradily lead. Pelipper is faster than Tyranitar, so Drizzle lands first and Sand Stream overwrites it on entry - Swift Swim never switches on, Water attacks never get the rain boost, and both Rock types take special hits with 50% more Special Defence. Cradily's Storm Drain pulls every single-target Water move on the player's side into itself, cancels it and raises its Special Attack, so Scald and Hydro Pump stop reaching Tyranitar, Aggron and Palossand at all. Turn one Tyranitar throws Thunder Punch into Pelipper (four times) or Rock Slide when two Flying bodies stand in front of it; Cradily answers with Power Gem into Pelipper, Sludge Bomb into Ludicolo (the Water/Grass that shrugs off Thunder Punch and Giga Drain) or Giga Drain into a Water/Ground, healing off it. Turn two repeats with Storm Drain's boost banked. Smooth Rock keeps the sand up for eight turns, so a Pelipper that switches out and back in has to spend two turns to win the weather back. Alternate deployment: Mega Aggron is pure Steel after the transformation, so Water hits it only neutrally, resists the Ice attacks aimed at Cradily and Tyranitar, and answers them with Heavy Slam; Body Press uses its Defence into Rock, Dark and Ice bodies. Reserves & disruption: Palossand's Shore Up heals two thirds in sand and its Giga Drain punishes a Water attacker; sand chips every non Rock/Ground/Steel body on the player's side while all four of Alan's are immune. Why the last version still lost: the sand plan was sound but never got to work. Tyranitar sat fourth, so the rain ran unopposed on turn zero; it came in on a super-effective Giga Drain aimed at Palossand's slot and was then removed by an unboosted Scald before it ever used Thunder Punch. Scald was the real problem - with or without rain it was super-effective on three of four bodies and burned the physical Rock types - and the Conservative trait spent five of the room's first eight actions on Protect or switches. Now Tyranitar leads, Cradily takes the Water moves instead of the Rock types, Assault Vest gives Cradily no Protect to hide behind, and the trait is gone. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and this is it: Rhydon took four times from Water and was Alan's third Rock/Ground body; the Root Fossil from the desert's Mirage Tower gives a Hiker digging in the ruins a Rock type that wants the rain player to attack it. Offsets individual: Cradily keeps Rhydon's -1 - Storm Drain and sand-boosted Special Defence make it the bulkiest body here; Tyranitar keeps +1 as the weather that must land.
crack: Blaziken-style Fighting and Fire pressure: Close Combat hits Cradily and Aggron super-effectively and Tyranitar four times, and Cradily's Earth Power is its only answer. Ice attacks hit Cradily super-effectively even in sand. Spread Water moves (Surf, Muddy Water) are not redirected and still hit everything around Cradily. Remove Tyranitar first, then win the weather back when Smooth Rock expires; Taunt stops Palossand's Shore Up; double-target the slow members before Mega Aggron's bulk comes online.

strategy: REDIRECTION,ALLY_COMBO

Level offsets are individual: Dunsparce +6 as an Eviolite pre-evolution, the evolved swarm +1 to +2. Counterplay & audit: Ghost immunity, Rocky Helmet/contact punishment, priority denial and physical mitigation.
crack: Lead a Ghost so Follow Me redirects nothing worth having, or a Rocky Helmet/Iron Barbs body so every multi-hit turn costs Randall more than it gains. Wide Guard blanks nothing here - the damage is single-target - so the real answers are contact punishment, physical mitigation and removing Maushold before Population Bomb resolves.

tactic: INSTRUCT ORANGURU INSTRUCT GIRAFARIG
tactic: INSTRUCT ORANGURU INSTRUCT SPINDA
tactic: INSTRUCT ORANGURU INSTRUCT WYRDEER
tactic: INSTRUCT ORANGURU INSTRUCT FARIGIRAF
tactic: INSTRUCT ORANGURU INSTRUCT LICKILICKY

It is now: the old version let Fake Out delete the room turn for free and had a Speed-invested Girafarig sitting in a Trick Room. Leading Armor Tail beside the setter turns Parker's room into a puzzle the player has to solve without priority, and the Telepathy Earthquake loop is the interaction to remember. Keeping the Girafarig/Farigiraf pair is deliberate - the gym shows both halves of one line doing opposite jobs - and the honest counterplay is Taunt, a second Trick Room, or simply killing Oranguru before Lickilicky lands its first Earthquake. Level offsets are individual: Girafarig +6 and Spinda +4 as fragile members, the evolved room core +2.
crack: Taunt or Encore Oranguru before the room lands, or set your own Trick Room to cancel it. Armor Tail stops priority, not speed control - Icy Wind, Electroweb or a Tailwind of your own inverts the room's maths. Levitate, Flying typing or a switch denies the Instructed Earthquake, and Farigiraf's Assault Vest means it can never Protect a losing turn.

## E0166 TRAINER_GEORGE class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 1
ai: Conservative
plan: Room concept - the fortress with a clock. First two useful turns: Mega Audino's Healer/Heal Pulse and Encore keep the partner alive and lock a committed player move while Dubwool either attacks or takes a Cotton Guard behind Fluffy. Turn two must convert - Body Press off the raised Defense, not another free guard turn. Alternate deployment: Audino beside Choice Band Miltank when the player leads Taunt, so the room still has immediate damage. Reserves & disruption: Chansey is the Eviolite special sponge with Thunder Wave as real speed control, Komala is status-proof through Comatose, and Snorlax is now the win condition rather than a sixth wall - Curse behind Heal Pulse gives the room an actual clock the player has to beat. AI executability: SETUP is backed by Cotton Guard and Curse; Conservative suits a room that wins on attrition, but it must not be allowed to guard indefinitely - the plan requires a damage conversion by turn three. Could this team be cooler? The old six-wall version was passive attrition with no way to win; it is cooler now because Curse Snorlax turns the fortress into a race - the player is not waiting for George to run out of ideas, they are watching a +2/+2 Snorlax grow behind Heal Pulse and Friend-Guard-grade mitigation. The memorable interaction is Encore trapping a player's setup move while Snorlax Curses through it. Honest counterplay: Taunt shuts Cotton Guard, Curse and Heal Pulse at once; Unaware, Haze, Roar or a special attacker with Fighting coverage beats the whole room. Level offsets are individual: Chansey +6 as an Eviolite pre-evolution, Mega Audino -1 because the stone is already a tier of power, the rest +2.
crack: Taunt is the single best answer - it blanks Heal Pulse, Encore, Cotton Guard, Soft-Boiled and Curse. Failing that, special Fighting damage ignores every Defense boost in the room, Haze erases the Snorlax clock, and Encore cuts both ways: give George a turn where his only legal repeat is a guard.

## E0167 TRAINER_BERKE class=gym
strategy: TAILWIND,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Room concept - the Normal gym's answer room: every member is a Normal type, and the room is built to beat the Fighting type every Normal gym invites. First two useful turns: Pidgeot sets Tailwind while Hisuian Zoroark opens under Illusion, disguised as Mega Kangaskhan until it takes damage; Normal/Ghost typing makes it immune to both Fighting and Normal attacks, so the Close Combat aimed at the "Kangaskhan" hits nothing, and Nasty Plot on that free turn makes its Shadow Ball the room's first threat. Turn two is the payoff: under Tailwind Gumshoos' Adaptability Double-Edge moves first, and Pidgeot's No Guard Hurricane cannot miss and hits Fighting types super-effectively. Alternate deployment: Exploud beside Mega Kangaskhan is the pure-Normal mode - Throat Spray fires on the first Hyper Voice, Scrappy lets both of them hit Ghosts, and Parental Bond makes every Kangaskhan attack land twice. Reserves & disruption: Stoutland is the Choice Band Intimidate breaker. AI executability: TAILWIND is backed by Pidgeot; SETUP is backed by Nasty Plot, Throat Spray and the Mega transition, so it must not be treated as a free boost turn. Could this team be cooler? The old room was a cold room - Snow Warning Aurorus, Mega Glalie and Slush Rush Cetitan - with three Ice types in the Normal gym, and none of them helped against the Fighting type that beats a Normal gym. Each slot keeps its job on a Normal type: Pidgeot's Tailwind replaces the snow as the speed engine, Zoroark replaces Aurorus as the special attacker that opens the fight, Gumshoos replaces Cetitan as the heavy hitter the speed engine carries, and the Mega moves to Kangaskhan, the most Normal Mega there is. The memorable line is a player's Close Combat passing straight through what they thought was Kangaskhan. Level offsets carried by role: Mega Kangaskhan takes Mega Glalie's -1, Pidgeot takes Kangaskhan's old +2, Zoroark +3, the rest +2.
crack: Break the Illusion with any cheap hit before committing a Fighting attack, then hit Zoroark with Dark damage, its only weakness, or any strong neutral attack - it is frail and Ghost attacks cannot touch it. Rock, Electric and Ice answer Pidgeot; Taunt it or outlast Tailwind behind Protect. Fighting still hits Gumshoos, Exploud, Stoutland and Mega Kangaskhan super-effectively once Zoroark and Pidgeot are gone, and Intimidate halves Gumshoos and Kangaskhan's Parental Bond hits alike. A Ghost type of your own gains nothing here: Scrappy and Shadow Ball both reach it.

## E0168 TRAINER_MARY class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Room concept - the price of power. Every member of Mary's six pays for its damage with a drawback: Hustle's misses, a Choice lock, a self-inflicted burn, Fluffy's doubled Fire damage, Berserk's requirement to be hurt first, and recoil. Mega Lopunny's Scrappy is the deliberate exception - the clean blade in a room full of costs. First two useful turns: Drampa fires the best Choice Specs option while Rufflet pressures or U-turns; Drampa's Berserk is reactive, so the opening attack is not pre-boosted and the AI must not plan as though it were. Turn two respects the lock and Hustle's 80% accuracy instead of assuming both attacks connect. Alternate deployment: Mega Lopunny's Fake Out buys Drampa a clean Specs turn and Scrappy means Ghost leads do not blank it. Reserves & disruption: Obstagoon converts its own Flame Orb into Guts Facade, Dudunsparce's Serene Grace Coil endgame doubles Body Slam paralysis and Rock Slide flinch, and Bewear's Fluffy halves every contact move aimed at it. AI executability: SETUP is backed by Coil; there is no weather, terrain or room flag claimed that the loadout cannot deliver. Could this team be cooler? Yes - and the change is exactly the member that had no price and no payoff. Silvally with a bare RKS System was a Normal-typed Multi-Attack and nothing else; Bewear replaces it with a rare, genuinely scary Normal/Fighting body whose ability is a double-edged trade (contact halved, Fire doubled), which is the room's whole thesis. The interaction to remember is Obstagoon's burn turning into a Guts Facade the same turn Lopunny's Fake Out denies the answer. Honest counterplay: Fairy and Fighting hit most of the room, Fire melts Bewear, Wide Guard is useless here but Rocky Helmet is excellent, and every Choice lock and Hustle miss is a free turn the player can bank. Level offsets are individual: Rufflet +6 as an Eviolite pre-evolution, Mega Lopunny -1, the rest +2.
crack: Punish the drawbacks rather than out-damaging them. Stall a Hustle miss, Encore or Taunt the Choice user, use a Fire move on Bewear where Fluffy doubles it, and keep Drampa below the Berserk threshold or kill it through the threshold in one hit. Fairy/Fighting coverage and contact punishment handle the rest.

## E0169 TRAINER_ALEXIA class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room concept - the berserkers, every one of whom poisons or burns itself on purpose. First two useful turns: Zangoose and Ursaring let their Orbs land at end of turn and attack or take one justified guard; the AI must not Protect a harmless slot while the partner is removed. Turn two runs on real Toxic Boost and Guts numbers, not anticipated ones - before the Orb activates these are ordinary attackers and should be played that way. Alternate deployment: Swellow's Toxic Orb Guts Facade is the fast third orb, giving Alexia an immediate-speed mode when the slower bears are a bad matchup; it also pivots with U-turn to bring a bear in safely. Reserves & disruption: Linoone takes a benchmarked Belly Drum only when Extreme Speed can actually close, Bibarel stacks Simple Curse (+2 Attack, +2 Defense per use) and now finishes with priority Aqua Jet, and Ursaluna's Bulletproof is the room's answer to Focus Blast, Sludge Bomb and Shadow Ball. AI executability: SETUP is backed by Belly Drum, Curse and Swords Dance. Stats/legality fixes applied: Bibarel's Superpower was actively anti-synergistic - Simple doubles the Attack and Defense drops as well as the Curse boosts - so it becomes Aqua Jet, which is what a slow +2 sweeper actually needs. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and it is: Delcatty with Wonder Skin and a Choice Scarf was the one member that neither hurt itself nor benefited from anyone who did. Swellow replaces it with the Hoenn Guts icon and completes the thesis - three self-inflicted status conditions on one team, each converting a "bad" status into the room's best damage. The interaction to remember is a Toxic Orb ticking on turn one while the player is still deciding whether it was a mistake. Honest counterplay: burst them before the Orbs pay, deny priority, Taunt the setup and remember that a Guts or Toxic Boost attacker is on a clock its own poison is running. Level offsets are individual: the fragile Orb users +2 to +3, Ursaluna +1 because Bulletproof plus Headlong Rush is already the room's strongest body.
crack: Race the Orbs. Every one of these six is weaker on turn one than on turn two, so the first exchange is the player's cheapest. Priority denial, physical mitigation and Taunt on Linoone/Bibarel/Ursaluna all work; Rocky Helmet and Intimidate tax a room built entirely on physical contact; and the poison damage finishes what the player starts.

## E0171 TRAINER_NORMAN_1 class=leader
strategy: ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon, Conservative
tactic: SUPPRESS WEEZING_GALAR NONE SLAKING
plan: Leader concept - Norman answers his own gym's thesis. Six rooms have each shown a Normal-type paying a price for its power; Norman brings the Pokemon with the worst price in the game and simply switches it off. First two useful turns: Galarian Weezing's Neutralizing Gas suppresses every ability on the field, Truant included, so Choice Band Slaking attacks every single turn while Weezing burns or attacks a real threat. Turn two preserves the Gas only while it is actually protecting Slaking's output - if Weezing is about to die anyway, it should spend its last turn on damage or a burn rather than a pointless guard. Alternate deployment: once Weezing is gone, Indeedee-F and Meloetta form a completely different battle - Psychic Surge under a Terrain Extender turns Expanding Force into a spread attack and Follow Me protects the pair, and both the Surge and Porygon2's Download only come online because the Gas has left. Reserves & disruption: Porygon2's Eviolite bulk plus Download and Recover is the long game; Mega Staraptor is the Reckless closer, and its ability too is suppressed while Weezing is out - the AI must read the live suppression state rather than count all six abilities at once. AI executability: ALLY_COMBO is backed by the printed SUPPRESS tactic, which is a genuine bespoke consumer, and by Follow Me; no weather or room flag is claimed. Could this team be cooler? This is the strongest concept in either gym and it is kept intact - a leader whose signature is a support Pokemon that disables the whole field so that his ace can exist is exactly the kind of interaction the player remembers. The one thing raised is pressure: the gym trainers now sit around +2 and a leader must be a clear step beyond, so Slaking and Porygon2 go to +4 and the support core to +3, with Mega Staraptor held at +1 because the stone is already a tier. Honest counterplay is unusually rich: remove or suppress Weezing and Truant returns mid-battle, Ghost typing blanks Slaking's entire Choice-locked Normal option, and the post-Gas Psychic mode has to be beaten a second, different way.
crack: Kill Weezing and the ace breaks itself - Truant comes back and Slaking gives you a free turn every other turn while Choice-locked. A Ghost type walls Double-Edge outright, redirection or Intimidate blunts whatever Slaking is locked into, and the second half of the fight is a Psychic Terrain battle that wants Dark, Steel and your own terrain rather than the answers that beat the first half.

## E0173 TRAINER_YUJI class=regular
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The dojo's guard discipline. First two useful turns: Scrafty uses Fake Out or Quick Guard and Hitmontop uses Wide Guard, so on the same turn the dojo can blank the player's priority and spread attacks together; Hitmontop otherwise Helping Hands the partner with the best target or throws Triple Axel into the Flying attacker that threatens the whole dojo. Hitmontop is the one member whose job is not to punch hardest: Steadfast turns a flinch into Speed, and Triple Axel's three hits are its Flying answer. Lucario's mixed EV investment is intentional and must not be corrected to a purely physical set. Cadence: two Fake Out users should be spread across turns, not both spent on turn one.
crack: Flying attacks hit three of the four super effectively and Psychic still hits Hitmontop and Hariyama; a fast special Fairy can remove Scrafty and Hitmontop before slow Hariyama Heavy Slams; Ghost types ignore Fake Out and most of the punches; Taunt the guards before the protected turn; special burst beats the physical-heavy dojo; Wide Guard does not stop single-target moves; burn or Intimidate blunts Hariyama's Heavy Slam, and heavy targets shrink it.

## E0175 TRAINER_FOSTER class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 3
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Omastar attacks or takes a safe Shell Smash while Runerigus burns or attacks the relevant threat. Turn two evaluates actual White Herb use, Weak Armor and any Wandering Spirit ability exchange. Alternate deployment: Runerigus supports Choice Band Relicanth's immediate physical pressure. Reserves & disruption: Mega Slowbro has a separate Calm Mind endgame. A contact-triggered ability exchange can invalidate presumed immunities or recovery, so use the current ability state. Counterplay & audit: Grass pressure, Taunt, priority after Smash and special attacks avoiding beneficial contact interactions. Disposition: Keep; no automatic claim that Runerigus's ability only harms opponents. Could this team be cooler? The fossil-diver theme is strong and stays: Omastar pairs White Herb with Shell Smash so the Defense and Special Defense drops are erased the moment they happen, leaving a clean +2/+2/+2, and Runerigus's Wandering Spirit swaps abilities with whatever makes contact with it - a rare, memorable and genuinely disruptive trigger. Mega Slowbro's Calm Mind plus Slack Off is the long game and Choice Band Rock Head Relicanth throws recoil-free Head Smashes. The improvement worth naming is that nothing protects Omastar's Shell Smash turn; a Fake Out or redirection partner would turn a good setup piece into a great one. Flagged. Offsets individual: Mega Slowbro 0, the rest +1 to +2.
crack: Grass pressure, Taunt, priority after Smash and special attacks avoiding beneficial contact interactions. Disposition: Keep; no automatic claim that Runerigus's ability only harms opponents.

crack: Wide Guard, spread damage, physical offense, Taunt and removing the actual attacker before two supports prolong the ending.

## E0227 TRAINER_MAGIKARP_GUY class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon, Conservative
plan: The Magikarp lover's exam, and the only fight in the campaign the player must enter with six Magikarp. Every member is a fish the rest of the world throws back, and every member is a lock that one of Magikarp's own moves opens - Tackle, Dragon Rage, Focus Sash plus Flail, Rattled, even Bounce - so the player leaves believing Magikarp are secretly great. Nothing here carries Electric or Grass coverage, so almost every attack the fisherman owns is a resisted Water move. First two useful turns: his own Magikarp and Feebas lead. His Magikarp holds the same Focus Sash and Flail the player is likely to bring - it is harmless at high HP and dangerous only once it sits at one HP - so the lesson is to chip it with Dragon Rage, whose fixed 40 breaks the Sash and never leaves it in Flail range for long. Feebas is the mirror image: Eviolite bulk plus Mirror Coat returns double the damage of any special hit, and Dragon Rage is special, so the right answer to Feebas is the humble Tackle. Alternate deployment: Wishiwashi arrives in School Form with 130-plus Defence and Special Defence that makes Flail bounce off, but Dragon Rage ignores defences; two Dragon Rages a turn bring its 131 HP down inside two turns, and without HP investment a full-health Magikarp survives two of its resisted Liquidations. Pyukumuku is the inverse lock: Counter doubles any physical hit back and Innards Out deals its remaining HP to whatever finishes it, so it is also a Dragon Rage target - chip it low first and the final hit costs only the last sliver. Its Toxic even feeds a Facade Magikarp. Reserves & disruption: Gyarados is the ace and the promise - what the player's Magikarp become. Its Intimidate lowers the Attack Flail needs but triggers Rattled, so a Rattled Magikarp gains a Speed stage and outruns even a Dragon Danced Gyarados. Waterfall is resisted and a full-HP Magikarp survives one, a Focus Sash Magikarp survives anything once and answers at one HP with a 200-power Flail, and the turn Gyarados spends in the air on Bounce is a free turn of Dragon Rage. The Mega, the Choice Scarf Imposter Ditto and Milotic are gone: they made the fight unwinnable rather than hard. Levels sit at the cap so the player's own cap-level Magikarp are even with the fish, and Gyarados at +1 is the one step up.
crack: Dragon Rage his Magikarp so its Sash breaks early and it never Flails from one HP; Tackle Feebas and never feed it a special move for Mirror Coat. Double Dragon Rage Wishiwashi and Pyukumuku - fixed damage walks through School Form's defences, ignores Counter, and chips Pyukumuku low before the Innards Out finish. Save the Focus Sash Flail Magikarp and the Rattled Magikarp for Gyarados: let it swing first, answer from one HP, and spend its Bounce turns on Dragon Rage.

Priority and Taunt against Vespiquen, contact and physical mitigation (Intimidate, burns) against Mega Heracross.

Counterplay & audit: Rock/Electric pressure, priority denial, special mitigation and room reversal. Disposition: Keep; explicitly pair Feint's target with the ally's chosen attack target. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and the fix belongs to the campaign rather than to Phil: Unfezant here is the same Scope Lens Super Luck body that Gabby and Ty field at E0228, and the Fortree gym now differentiates its own copy with Focus Energy for guaranteed crits. Phil's own concept is sound - Intimidate Staraptor setting Tailwind, Defiant Farfetch'd turning an opposing Intimidate into an Attack boost, and Volt Absorb Kilowattrel as the Electric switch-in - and Defiant beside Intimidate is a genuinely nice bit of internal design. Flagged for a later differentiation pass rather than churned now. Offsets stay low and individual for a route regular.
crack: Rock/Electric pressure, priority denial, special mitigation and room reversal. Disposition: Keep; explicitly pair Feint's target with the ally's chosen attack target.
STARAPTOR @LIFE_ORB INTIMIDATE JOLLY 4/252/0/0/0/252 4 | BRAVE_BIRD, CLOSE_COMBAT, TAILWIND, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
FARFETCHD @SITRUS_BERRY DEFIANT JOLLY 4/252/0/0/0/252 5 | BRAVE_BIRD, LEAF_BLADE, FEINT, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
KILOWATTREL @WISE_GLASSES VOLT_ABSORB TIMID 4/0/0/252/0/252 4 | THUNDERBOLT, HURRICANE, VOLT_SWITCH, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255

## E0243 TRAINER_GRUNT_WEATHER_INST_1 class=grunt
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Raid stage 1 - the lobby. These are the grunts who got through the door before anyone touched the machines, so this is the one room in the Institute with no weather at all: pure piratical disruption and burst. First two useful turns: Golbat Taunts a support or U-turns out while Crawdaunt swings Adaptability Crabhammer/Knock Off - Knock Off stripping a Focus Sash or Eviolite on turn one is what makes the rest of the raid harder. Turn two keeps pressure rather than guarding the only threatening slot with no partner payoff. Alternate deployment: Liepard's Prankster Fake Out and Encore support Crawdaunt more directly, locking a player into a support move while Adaptability does the work. Reserves & disruption: Tentacruel is the black-flag anchor - Liquid Ooze punishes every Drain/Leech recovery the player has, and Acid Spray now drops a target's Special Defense by two stages to set up Sludge Bomb or Muddy Water. AI executability: no strategy flag is claimed and none is needed; this grunt is disruption-plus-burst, not an incomplete rain team. Could this team be cooler? It is now, marginally but concretely: Tentacruel's Rapid Spin did nothing in a hazard-free doubles format - a genuinely empty slot on an otherwise sharp Aqua opener - and Acid Spray turns it into the room's setup move, which is the memorable interaction here (a two-stage special drop immediately followed by a partner's spread Water attack). The Dark/Poison/Water palette is exactly the faction identity the raid should open with. Honest counterplay: priority denial, Grass and Electric coverage, and refusing to rely on drain recovery while Liquid Ooze is on the field. Level offsets are individual: Golbat +5 as an Eviolite pre-evolution, the evolved crew +1 as the raid's first and easiest room.
crack: Electric and Grass coverage answer three of the four. Deny Crawdaunt its Knock Off turn and your items survive the whole raid; play around Prankster by leading a Dark type or a Taunt of your own; and never plan a Giga Drain or Drain Punch turn while Tentacruel's Liquid Ooze is out.

## E0244 TRAINER_GRUNT_WEATHER_INST_4 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Raid stage 2 - the cold storage. The crew here has the Institute's Ice data and a Contrary squid that gets stronger every time it hits something. First two useful turns: Walrein opens with Icy Wind to reverse the speed order, or Encore to lock a player into a move that has already stopped being useful, so that Malamar's Superpower lands under Aqua's terms. Turn two runs on real Contrary boosts: Superpower raises Malamar's Attack and Defense instead of dropping them, so the second Superpower is strictly stronger than the first and the AI must value repetition rather than treat it as a one-shot. Alternate deployment: Gourgeist-Super's Flare Boost special pressure beside Speed Boost Sharpedo is the faster, frailer mode when Walrein's bulk is the wrong tool. Reserves & disruption: Walrein is support, not the damage engine - Thick Fat makes it the crew's answer to Fire and Ice, not a win condition. Stats/legality fix applied: Malamar's Clear Amulet was an active anti-synergy - Contrary wants opposing Intimidate, Snarl and Icy Wind to land, because it converts every one of them into a boost, and Clear Amulet was blocking exactly that. Lum Berry replaces it and covers the burn or paralysis that actually stops a physical Contrary sweeper. AI executability: SETUP is backed by Contrary Superpower accumulating real boosts; no weather flag is claimed because nothing on this team can set one. Could this team be cooler? The concept is good - a squid that grows off the player's own debuffs is a genuinely memorable Aqua idea - and it is now mechanically honest rather than quietly self-defeating. The interaction to remember is a player's Intimidate lead making Malamar stronger. Gourgeist-Super's form alias and its configured Flare Boost remain a catalogue permission to confirm against the resolved form tables rather than a tactical problem. Honest counterplay: Bug and Fairy hit Malamar, Haze erases the whole Contrary curve, and Taunt shuts Walrein's support kit. Level offsets are individual and flat at +1 - this is the raid's second room and all four are evolved.
crack: Haze or Clear Smog undoes every Contrary boost at once and is the single cleanest answer. Otherwise refuse to hand Malamar free stat drops (no Intimidate lead, no Snarl, no Icy Wind), hit it with Bug or Fairy damage, and remember Sharpedo needs turns it does not have before Speed Boost matters.

## E0245 TRAINER_GRUNT_WEATHER_INST_5 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Raid stage 3 - the armoury. This crew fights with priority and artillery rather than weather. First two useful turns: Toxicroak's Fake Out buys Scizor either a safe Swords Dance or an immediate Technician Bullet Punch - Technician boosts Bullet Punch and Bug Bite, so Scizor's priority is genuinely threatening rather than chip. Turn two attacks through the boost, or abandons setup outright once Fire pressure appears, because Scizor and a Dry Skin Toxicroak both fold to it. Alternate deployment: Levitate Eelektross beside Clawitzer is the artillery mode - Eelektross has no type weakness at all while Levitate holds, and Clawitzer's Mega Launcher multiplies Water Pulse, Dark Pulse and Aura Sphere by 1.5, which is the crew's answer to a Steel or Dark wall. Reserves & disruption: Dry Skin is a Water-matchup tool and a Fire liability in the same ability; Scizor's Metal Coat is a loadout item and carries no implication about player item access. AI executability: SETUP has a real Swords Dance user protected by a real Fake Out; nothing else is claimed. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and the change removes the raid's clearest duplicate: this grunt fielded the same Adaptability Crawdaunt as the lobby grunt two rooms earlier, with the same three moves. Clawitzer replaces it with a Water-type whose whole identity is a cannon - which is both better flavour for a pirate boarding party and a completely different threat vector (special, long-range, Aura Sphere for the Steel and Dark bodies Crawdaunt could not break). The moment to remember is a 1.5x Aura Sphere from a shrimp answering the Steel type that just walled Bullet Punch. Honest counterplay: Fire ruins half the crew, and Toxicroak's Dry Skin makes that worse rather than better. Level offsets are individual: Toxicroak +1 as the fragile Fake Out lead, the rest +2 as the raid escalates.
crack: One Fire move threatens Scizor, Toxicroak (Dry Skin doubles it) and Clawitzer's usefulness in the same turn. Ground damage answers Eelektross the moment Levitate is suppressed or ignored; Psychic coverage handles Toxicroak; and denying the Fake Out turn means Scizor never gets its Swords Dance.

## E0246 TRAINER_GRUNT_WEATHER_INST_2 class=grunt
strategy: RAIN,SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Raid stage 4 - the machine room, and the first grunt who has actually turned something on. Pelipper's Drizzle is Aqua getting what it came for, and the rest of the raid escalates from here. First two useful turns: Pelipper establishes rain and chooses Tailwind, or Wide Guard when that is what protects Ferrothorn's turn; Ferrothorn attacks or takes a survivable Iron Defense behind Iron Barbs, which taxes every contact move aimed at it. Turn two converts Defense into Body Press, or abandons the slow plan for a special reserve if the rain matters more than the wall. Alternate deployment: Pelipper and Swift Swim Ludicolo are the straightforward fast-rain pressure mode, and under rain Ludicolo's Hydro Pump is the crew's biggest single number. Reserves & disruption: Inteleon's Snipe Shot ignores redirection entirely, which is the answer to a Follow Me or Rage Powder player, and Sniper multiplies a critical hit rather than guaranteeing one - without Focus Energy it is a chance, not a plan. Rain weakens Fire damage; it does not create immunity. AI executability: RAIN has an automatic setter, TAILWIND a real user, SETUP an Iron Defense; all three flags are backed. Could this team be cooler? The composition earns retention and the contrast is the point - a defensive Steel mode and a fast Water mode in one grunt, chosen against what the player leads, is more interesting than a fourth interchangeable rain sweeper, and the Snipe Shot answer to redirection is a genuinely uncommon tool at this stage. What it lacked was raid position: as the room where Aqua first seizes the weather it should read as a step up from the lobby, so its offsets move to +2. The moment to remember is Snipe Shot going straight through a Follow Me. Honest counterplay: replace the weather and Ludicolo, Pelipper and the Fire-resistance all weaken at once.
crack: Take the weather back - one Drought or Sand Stream turn undoes the room's whole reason for existing. Grass and Fighting coverage answer Ferrothorn, Electric answers the Water half, and Taunt on Pelipper denies Tailwind, Wide Guard and the rain-support plan in one move.

## E0247 TRAINER_GRUNT_WEATHER_INST_3 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Raid stage 5 - the last door before Shelly, and the ugliest crew in the building. First two useful turns: Mightyena's Intimidate lands on entry and Howl raises the Attack of both Mightyena and its partner, which is what makes this a doubles support move rather than a selfish boost; Golisopod uses First Impression only on its genuine entry turn, because the move is dead from turn two onward. Turn two must account for Emergency Exit - if Golisopod drops below half it is pulled out whether Aqua wanted it or not, which can end a planned boosted attack before it happens. Alternate deployment: Alolan Muk and Qwilfish are the slower poison-control mode - Muk's Poison/Dark typing resists most of what a player brings to a Ghost-free room, and Qwilfish's second Intimidate stacks with Mightyena's. Reserves & disruption: Spikes and Curse should not both burn a turn in the same battle; one of them is the plan and the other is a concession. Sucker Punch fails against any non-attacking turn. AI executability: SETUP is backed by Howl and Curse. Could this team be cooler? The composition is retained and deliberately so - Mightyena, Golisopod, Alolan Muk and Qwilfish is the strongest pure Aqua-flavour roster in the raid (a wrecked beach's worth of Dark, Poison and Water bruisers) and the Emergency Exit risk is a real, readable drawback the player can force rather than a hidden coin flip. The honest answer to "could it be cooler" is that its weakness was presentation, not species: a raid boss's antechamber was running the same flat offsets as the lobby. It now sits at +2 to +3, the highest of the grunts, and the moment to remember is deliberately damaging Golisopod to force it out of a winning position. Honest counterplay: special damage bypasses two Intimidates, priority denial blanks First Impression and Sucker Punch, and controlled chip damage weaponises Emergency Exit against its owner.
crack: Chip Golisopod below half on purpose - Emergency Exit will remove it from a board it was winning. Special attackers ignore both Intimidates entirely; Ground and Psychic coverage answer the Poison half; and every Sucker Punch fails on a turn you spend setting up or guarding instead of attacking.

## E0248 TRAINER_SHELLY_WEATHER_INSTITUTE class=admin
strategy: SNOW,REDIRECTION,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Raid boss - Shelly does not steal the weather machine, she reprograms it. Five grunts escalate from no weather, to a stolen rain switch, and then the admin turns the whole Institute to snow and fights the player inside a building that is now working for Aqua. That is the reason this boss is not a second rain team, and it is also why two of her six are the Institute's own Pokemon, commandeered rather than caught. First two useful turns: Alolan Ninetales sets Snow Warning on entry and takes Aurora Veil behind Light Clay - eight turns of halved damage on both sides is the single biggest number in the fight - while Empoleon applies Hydro Pump/Flash Cannon pressure and punishes only genuine opposing stat drops through Competitive. Turn two exploits the Veil: Clefable's Follow Me absorbs a double-target so Mega Feraligatr can Dragon Dance safely, and Sheer Force Liquidation and Ice Punch lose their secondary effects but gain 1.3x damage in exchange. Alternate deployment: Castform beside Ninetales is the Institute's own research Pokemon turned against its keepers - under Shelly's snow Forecast makes Castform an Ice type, so Weather Ball is a 100-power same-type attack and Blizzard never misses; it is the thematic heart of the fight. Reserves & disruption: Rotom-Wash is the Levitate Electric pivot, and Clefable's Unaware means a player who answered the grunts by setting up finds those boosts simply ignored. AI executability: SNOW has an automatic setter, REDIRECTION a real Follow Me user, SETUP a real Dragon Dance; the custom Mega Feraligatr transition remains a source and native gate to confirm. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and one slot was quietly dead: Kingdra's Damp does nothing outside Explosion and Aftermath, so the boss was carrying a 540-BST body with a blank ability and a generic special kit. Castform replaces it with the one Pokemon that belongs in this building more than any other, and its ability is not flavour - Forecast is the mechanic that ties the boss to the room she is standing in. Clefable is likewise re-read as the Institute's own, which resolves the only other thematic loose end. The moment to remember is a Castform turning white as Shelly's snow lands and immediately throwing a perfect-accuracy Blizzard. Honest counterplay: take the weather back and four of her six get worse at once. Level offsets are individual and boss-grade: Castform +5 because it is a frail 420-BST body carrying a key role, Mega Feraligatr +3, the support core +3 to +4.
crack: Replace the snow. Drought, Drizzle or Sand Stream simultaneously ends Aurora Veil's reason for existing, Castform's typing and Blizzard's accuracy. Failing that, remove screens with Brick Break or Defog, attack Clefable rather than routing around Follow Me, bring Grass and Electric for the Water core, and keep your own stat boosts off the table while Unaware and Competitive are on the field.

## E0249 TRAINER_BRENDAN_ROUTE_119_MUDKIP class=rival
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Rival concept - the Hoenn team the player could have built, assembled better. All six branch aliases are one design: the first five slots are fixed and only the fourth member and the ace change, so the rival always covers the starter the player chose and always leads the same way. First two useful turns: Swellow's Flame Orb lands at the end of turn one and from turn two its Guts Facade is a 140-power attack from a burned body that can no longer be burned again, while Manectric decides between Thunderbolt pressure and a Volt Switch pivot - Lightning Rod is the reason this pair can stand together at all, because it draws the Electric attacks that would otherwise delete Swellow. Turn two exploits whatever the Orb and the pivot bought; before the burn lands Swellow is an ordinary bird and must be played as one. Alternate deployment: Breloom's Toxic Orb Poison Heal is the second, opposite orb - the same self-inflicted status converted into recovery instead of damage - and a 100%-accurate Spore plus priority Mach Punch is the disruption mode when the bird lead is a bad matchup. Reserves & disruption: the branch-specific fourth member exists to threaten the regional starter the player did NOT pick (Shiftry and Sceptile against a Mudkip player, Ludicolo and Swampert against Torchic, Camerupt and Blaziken against Treecko), and Mimikyu's Disguise guarantees it one free turn for Will-O-Wisp or Taunt while Mental Herb covers a Taunt of the player's own. Shiftry's Pickpocket cannot trigger while it still holds its Sitrus Berry - it comes online only after the berry is eaten, which is a sequencing note rather than a dead ability. AI executability: no strategy flag is claimed and none is backed; this is a disruption-and-orbs team, not a weather or room team, and Ace Pokemon correctly points the AI at the starter. Could this team be cooler? The composition earns retention and should not be churned: two orbs paying off in opposite directions, a redirection ability protecting the member that needs it, guaranteed sleep, a guaranteed free turn, and a starter that always answers the player's own - that is a genuinely tight six-slot Hoenn design and it reads instantly as a rival rather than a route trainer. The one change worth considering later is giving the ace a Mega stone so the rival escalates visibly across the campaign; it is deferred here because Blazikenite and Sceptilite both already have homes earlier in the game and duplicating them at Route 119 would flatten those showcases. The moment to remember is Lightning Rod eating a Thunderbolt aimed at a burned Swellow that then hits back for 140. What did need fixing was the levels: a blanket zero made the campaign's mid-game rival the same difficulty as the regulars around it. Every member goes to +2 and the ace to +4. Counterplay & audit: powder immunity, priority, Ground pressure and denying repeated pivots.
crack: Kill Swellow on turn one, before the Flame Orb resolves - it is the whole team's tempo and it is a 60/60/50 body until the burn lands. A Grass type or Safety Goggles blanks Spore and leaves Breloom a slow Mach Punch user with no Poison Heal trigger. Ground damage answers Manectric and removes the Lightning Rod umbrella at once; a Steel or Fire type walls Mimikyu once Disguise is broken, and breaking Disguise with any cheap attack rather than your strongest is the correct line. The ace is always the starter that beats yours, so bring the coverage move your own starter lacks.
SWELLOW @FLAME_ORB GUTS JOLLY 4/252/0/0/0/252 2 | FACADE, BRAVE_BIRD, U_TURN, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
MANECTRIC @FOCUS_SASH LIGHTNING_ROD TIMID 4/0/0/252/0/252 2 | THUNDERBOLT, VOLT_SWITCH, SNARL, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
BRELOOM @TOXIC_ORB POISON_HEAL JOLLY 4/252/0/0/0/252 3 | SPORE, MACH_PUNCH, SEED_BOMB, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
SHIFTRY @SITRUS_BERRY PICKPOCKET JOLLY 4/252/0/0/0/252 2 | FAKE_OUT, LEAF_BLADE, KNOCK_OFF, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
MIMIKYU @MENTAL_HERB DISGUISE JOLLY 4/252/0/0/0/252 3 | PLAY_ROUGH, SHADOW_SNEAK, WILL_O_WISP, TAUNT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
SCEPTILE @LIFE_ORB OVERGROW JOLLY 4/252/0/0/0/252 4 | LEAF_BLADE, DRAGON_CLAW, X_SCISSOR, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255

crack: Kill Swellow on turn one, before the Flame Orb resolves - it is the whole team's tempo and it is a 60/60/50 body until the burn lands. Ground damage answers Manectric and removes the Lightning Rod umbrella at once; a Steel or Fire type walls Mimikyu once Disguise is broken, and breaking Disguise with any cheap attack rather than your strongest is the correct line. The ace is always the starter that beats yours, so bring the coverage move your own starter lacks.

## E0249 TRAINER_BRENDAN_ROUTE_119_TREECKO class=rival
strategy: SUN
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Rival concept - the Hoenn team the player could have built, assembled better. All six branch aliases are one design: the first five slots are fixed and only the fourth member and the ace change, so the rival always covers the starter the player chose and always leads the same way. First two useful turns: Swellow's Flame Orb lands at the end of turn one and from turn two its Guts Facade is a 140-power attack from a burned body that can no longer be burned again, while Manectric decides between Thunderbolt pressure and a Volt Switch pivot - Lightning Rod is the reason this pair can stand together at all, because it draws the Electric attacks that would otherwise delete Swellow. Turn two exploits whatever the Orb and the pivot bought; before the burn lands Swellow is an ordinary bird and must be played as one. Alternate deployment: Breloom's Toxic Orb Poison Heal is the second, opposite orb - the same self-inflicted status converted into recovery instead of damage - and a 100%-accurate Spore plus priority Mach Punch is the disruption mode when the bird lead is a bad matchup. Reserves & disruption: the branch-specific fourth member exists to threaten the regional starter the player did NOT pick (Shiftry and Sceptile against a Mudkip player, Ludicolo and Swampert against Torchic, Camerupt and Blaziken against Treecko), and Mimikyu's Disguise guarantees it one free turn for Will-O-Wisp or Taunt while Mental Herb covers a Taunt of the player's own. Shiftry's Pickpocket cannot trigger while it still holds its Sitrus Berry - it comes online only after the berry is eaten, which is a sequencing note rather than a dead ability. AI executability: this Treecko branch alone claims SUN, backed by Camerupt's Sunny Day. Route 119 rains three days in four, and rain halves every Fire attack this branch exists to land on the player's Grass starter, so Camerupt carries Sunny Day in place of Rock Slide: when the rain is up and Blaziken or Heat Wave has a Grass target, one Sunny Day replaces the rain for five turns and doubles the Fire damage back. On a dry day it simply attacks. Blaziken keeps Rock Slide for Flying and Bug coverage, and Ace Pokemon still points the AI at the starter. Could this team be cooler? The composition earns retention and should not be churned: two orbs paying off in opposite directions, a redirection ability protecting the member that needs it, guaranteed sleep, a guaranteed free turn, and a starter that always answers the player's own - that is a genuinely tight six-slot Hoenn design and it reads instantly as a rival rather than a route trainer. The one change worth considering later is giving the ace a Mega stone so the rival escalates visibly across the campaign; it is deferred here because Blazikenite and Sceptilite both already have homes earlier in the game and duplicating them at Route 119 would flatten those showcases. The moment to remember is Lightning Rod eating a Thunderbolt aimed at a burned Swellow that then hits back for 140. What did need fixing was the levels: a blanket zero made the campaign's mid-game rival the same difficulty as the regulars around it. Every member goes to +2 and the ace to +4. Counterplay & audit: powder immunity, priority, Ground pressure and denying repeated pivots.
crack: Kill Swellow on turn one, before the Flame Orb resolves - it is the whole team's tempo and it is a 60/60/50 body until the burn lands. A Grass type or Safety Goggles blanks Spore and leaves Breloom a slow Mach Punch user with no Poison Heal trigger. Ground damage answers Manectric and removes the Lightning Rod umbrella at once; a Steel or Fire type walls Mimikyu once Disguise is broken, and breaking Disguise with any cheap attack rather than your strongest is the correct line. The ace is always the starter that beats yours, so bring the coverage move your own starter lacks. Against this branch, Taunt or knock out Camerupt before Sunny Day, or bring your own Drizzle to take the rain back.

MANECTRIC @FOCUS_SASH LIGHTNING_ROD TIMID 4/0/0/252/0/252 2 | THUNDERBOLT, VOLT_SWITCH, SNARL, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255

## E0251 TRAINER_HIDEO class=ace
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: 4
plan: First two useful turns: Bisharp attacks while Muk uses Helping Hand or its own Poison damage at a real threshold. Turn two brings Salazzle or Mega Gengar when the physical matchup is unfavorable. Alternate deployment: Salazzle's Fake Out supports Gengar's special pressure and Icy Wind. Reserves & disruption: Knock Off and Poltergeist do not coexist here, so don't import that issue; Defiant triggers only from the supported opposing effects. Counterplay & audit: Ground/Fighting pressure, priority denial and special mitigation. Disposition: Keep; Mega Gengar's trapping does not make this a Perish team without a song. Could this team be cooler? Kept: Salazzle's Corrosion lets it badly poison Steel and Poison types that are normally immune, which is the single most distinctive thing a Poison specialist can do, and Mega Gengar's trapping plus Icy Wind speed control turns the fight into a cage. Bisharp's Defiant punishes the Intimidate or Snarl that a player naturally brings against a physical Dark type. Muk's Poison Touch plus Helping Hand is the support anchor. The improvement worth naming is that Salazzle's Toxic is not on the set, so Corrosion currently has nothing to corrode - it is the ability's entire point and the slot carries Fake Out instead. Flagged as a build note for the next pass. Offsets individual: Mega Gengar +2 as the ace.
crack: Ground/Fighting pressure, priority denial and special mitigation. Disposition: Keep; Mega Gengar's trapping does not make this a Perish team without a song.

## E0253 TRAINER_COLIN class=regular
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Togedemaru Fake Outs the foe most able to stop Xatu, and Xatu sets Tailwind only when it improves the race; turn two uses the speed or pivots to the special attacker, and Fake Out is not repeated. Togedemaru is the flock's lightning rod: Lightning Rod pulls single-target Electric attacks away from the birds and absorbs them, Electric/Steel resists Rock, Flying and Ice, Iron Head hits Rock and Ice attackers super-effectively, and Zing Zap punishes Water and Flying foes. Shuca Berry softens the 4x Ground hit that is its one real weakness. Magic Bounce Xatu reflects Taunt, Will-O-Wisp, Thunder Wave, Leech Seed and hazards back at the sender. Alternate deployment: Xatu and Pa'u Oricorio support a Quiver Dance special mode; Oricorio's Dancer also copies a player's Quiver Dance, Dragon Dance or Swords Dance. Reserves: Sheer Force Hisuian Braviary is the Choice Specs breaker with Esper Wing.
crack: Ground and Fire attacks on Togedemaru, spread Electric moves (Discharge, Electroweb) that Lightning Rod does not redirect, Rock Slide into the three birds once Togedemaru is gone, Taunt where Magic Bounce does not protect the action, and room reversal.

## E0255 TRAINER_LORENZO class=ace
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: First two useful turns: Ogerpon and Zangoose choose complementary physical targets; Zangoose's Toxic Boost is credited only after Orb activation. Route 120 can sit under a Calyrex fog anomaly, and fog opens the battle with Misty Terrain, which stops the Toxic Orb poisoning a grounded Zangoose. Zangoose therefore attacks with Double-Edge rather than Facade: in clear weather or rain the Orb still lands and Toxic Boost raises Double-Edge by half, and in fog Double-Edge is still a 120-power hit instead of a 70-power Facade. Turn two pivots or uses the appropriate priority line. Alternate deployment: Nidoqueen and Starmie give a fully special alternative against physical mitigation. Reserves & disruption: Base Ogerpon's held Life Orb does not create a mask transformation. Spiky Shield's contact payoff is conditional. Counterplay & audit: Physical mitigation, priority denial, status timing and special walls against the reserve mode. Disposition: Keep; Ogerpon is the plain masked form in this Mega-focused ruleset. Could this team be cooler? Kept, and Ogerpon carries it: Defiant plus Ivy Cudgel is a legendary-grade lead that punishes Intimidate, and Spiky Shield is a Protect that damages whoever tried to hit through it - a guard with a real payoff rather than a free read, which is exactly the Protect standard this campaign wants. Toxic Boost Zangoose beside it is the second self-harm attacker when the Orb can land and Sheer Force Nidoqueen the special breaker. Starmie's Natural Cure means status never sticks to it. The improvement worth naming is that four attackers share no support turn between them; Lorenzo wins on raw quality rather than on a plan. Offsets individual: Starmie +2 as the fast closer.
crack: Physical mitigation, priority denial, status timing and special walls against the reserve mode. Rock and Steel types resist Zangoose's Double-Edge, and its recoil wears it down. Disposition: Keep; Ogerpon is the plain masked form in this Mega-focused ruleset.
OGERPON @LIFE_ORB DEFIANT JOLLY 4/252/0/0/0/252 4 | IVY_CUDGEL, HORN_LEECH, SPIKY_SHIELD, U_TURN | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/31 | friendship=255
