## E0258 TRAINER_JENNIFER class=ace
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 1
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: First two useful turns: Sableye chooses its useful Prankster action or Mega transformation while Corviknight attacks or takes a safe Bulk Up. Turn two evaluates the post-Mega ability and whether the setup still matters. Roles: Corviknight replaces Kommo-o as the Fairy answer. Kommo-o and Hydreigon were both four-times weak to Fairy and Kommo-o's Flash Cannon was chip off a Bold set; Corviknight is Steel/Flying, so Fairy moves - Pixilate Hyper Voice and Moonblast included - hit it for half, and its Iron Head hits Fairy types super-effectively. Bulk Up raises both Attack and Defense, which powers Iron Head and Body Press together, Roost and Leftovers keep it standing, and Mirror Armor returns Intimidate and other stat drops to the sender. Alternate deployment: Whimsicott's Tailwind and Helping Hand support Hydreigon's immediate special pressure. Reserves & disruption: Mega Sableye loses its base Prankster role when it transforms; do not credit priority support and the Mega ability simultaneously. How the pieces combine: two Prankster users with completely different jobs - Mega Sableye's priority Will-O-Wisp and Recover behind Magic Bounce make it nearly unkillable by status, while Whimsicott passes Tailwind, Encore and Helping Hand at priority - and Corviknight holds the board against the Fairy that would otherwise sweep both Dragons. Jennifer stands on Route 120, where the rain that covers much of the route can follow the player into her fight, so no member leans on Fire. Hydreigon's Earth Power hits the Steel types that resist Draco Meteor and Dark Pulse. Could this team be cooler? A Fairy lead used to beat three of four members; now it has to go through a Steel bird that resists it and hits back first. Honest counterplay is unusually specific: a Dark-type lead ignores both Pranksters at once, and Mega Sableye's Magic Bounce only exists after the transition, so the pre-Mega turn is the window. Offsets individual: Mega Sableye 0, Hydreigon +2.
crack: Electric and Fire remove Corviknight, after which Fairy pressure beats Hydreigon and Whimsicott's partners again; Taunt/ability-bypassing interactions, special damage into Corviknight's lower Special Defense, and priority denial. Disposition: exact transformation timing is an intent decision, not automatic first-turn Mega.

## E0272 TRAINER_MIU_AND_YUKI class=regular
strategy: SUN,SNOW
mega_slots: NONE
plan: First two useful turns: Ninetales establishes sun while Weepinbell attacks or uses a legal Sleep Powder; turn two exploits Chlorophyll before considering a switch. Alternate deployment: Alolan Ninetales and Torterra form the separate snow/Veil matchup mode. Reserves & disruption: Switching to Alolan Ninetales replaces sun and removes Weepinbell's Chlorophyll advantage. Veil is not an additional benefit layered on permanent sun. Counterplay & audit: Weather control, Taunt, Fire/Flying pressure and exploiting the setter switch. Disposition: Keep as a genuine two-weather puzzle only with explicit mutually exclusive modes and a reason to change them. Could this team be cooler? It is kept exactly because of what looks like a bug: two sisters, two Ninetales, two incompatible weathers. Kantonian Ninetales's Drought and Alolan Ninetales's Snow Warning overwrite each other, and the deployment order is the design - the lead pair is sun (Drought plus Chlorophyll Weepinbell), and the Alolan half with Aurora Veil is the second mode after the first falls. The player therefore fights the same trainer under two different skies, and the memorable moment is the weather flipping when the second sister's Ninetales comes out. Torterra's Solid Rock is the grounded body that does not care either way. The honest caution is that the AI must never send both Ninetales out together, which would waste one of them. Offsets individual: the Eviolite Weepinbell at +1, the rest low.
crack: Weather control, Taunt, Fire/Flying pressure and exploiting the setter switch. Disposition: Keep as a genuine two-weather puzzle only with explicit mutually exclusive modes and a reason to change them.

## E0283 TRAINER_JARED class=gym
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: Room concept - the treetop's opening room: setup that actually gets a turn, and every member a bird. First two useful turns: Murkrow uses Prankster Tailwind before anything else on the field moves, and Noctowl attacks under it, its Tinted Lens turning the resists that normally wall Air Slash and Hyper Voice into neutral damage. Turn two is Oricorio's: Quiver Dance raises its Special Attack, Special Defense and Speed at once, and Dancer copies any dance move the player uses - a Dragon Dance or Swords Dance becomes Oricorio's too. Alternate deployment: Hisuian Decidueye beside Eviolite Trumbeak is the answer lead for the Electric or Rock type every Flying gym invites. Grass/Fighting resists both Electric and Rock, Triple Arrows and Leaf Blade hit Rock, Steel and Ice types super-effectively, Scrappy makes it immune to Intimidate, and Triple Arrows' Defense drop sets up Trumbeak's Skill Link Rock Blast and Bullet Seed, which always hit five times. Yache Berry halves the first Ice attack aimed at the owl, and Swords Dance is the room's second SETUP payoff. Reserves & disruption: Bombirdier is the room's inversion - a Flying type whose Rocky Payload boosts Rock Slide, so Jared answers the player's own Flying and Ice switch-ins from inside a Flying gym. Prankster Haze on Murkrow erases a player's boosts before Tailwind expires. AI executability: TAILWIND has a priority user, and SETUP is backed by Quiver Dance, Swords Dance and Skill Link payoff. Could this team be cooler? The previous room held Swoobat, Scyther and Flapple - a bat, a mantis and an apple dragon under a Bird Keeper. Each is now a bird doing the same job: Oricorio is the special setup sweeper Swoobat was, Trumbeak is the Eviolite multi-hit attacker Technician Scyther was, and Hisuian Decidueye - an archer owl that is not Flying-type - is the one counter to Electric and Rock that Flapple was, now resisting both instead of only one. Level offsets unchanged: Murkrow +6 and Trumbeak +5 as Eviolite pre-evolutions, Oricorio and Bombirdier +3, Noctowl and Decidueye +2.
crack: Taunt or a faster priority user removes Tailwind and Haze before they matter; Oricorio and Murkrow both fold to a single neutral hit. Decidueye is the room's only answer to Electric and Rock, so remove it first - Flying attacks hit it four times over, and Fire, Ice, Psychic and Fairy attacks hit it double once the Yache Berry is gone. With Decidueye gone the room is five Rock-weak, Electric-weak birds; bring a Steel type that Bombirdier's Rocky Payload cannot punch through, and never use a dance move in front of Oricorio.

## E0284 TRAINER_EDWARDO class=gym
strategy: TAILWIND,REDIRECTION
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room concept - the carrion flock: nothing here fights fair. First two useful turns: Jumpluff is now a dedicated support - Rage Powder pulls both player attacks onto a Focus Sash body while Honchkrow takes a free Brave Bird or Sucker Punch, and Sleep Powder or Encore replaces the redirection turn when the player has a powder-immune lead. Turn two is the Moxie turn: a single knockout behind redirection snowballs Honchkrow's Attack, and Life Orb Brave Bird from +1 is what actually ends the room. Alternate deployment: Mandibuzz Tailwind supporting Choice Band Braviary is the honest plan when sleep and powder are off the table entirely - Overcoat also means Mandibuzz never eats an opposing Spore. Reserves & disruption: Vespiquen is the Defend Order pivot that heals the pair, and Skill Link Toucannon breaks Focus Sash and Substitute with Bullet Seed and Rock Blast while Beak Blast punishes contact. Defiant on Braviary triggers on opposing stat drops only - friendly Intimidate or a self-inflicted drop must never be credited. AI executability: TAILWIND has a real user; REDIRECTION is now genuinely backed by Rage Powder rather than implied by the theme. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and the fix is small and exact: Jumpluff was carrying Acrobatics, a fourth mediocre attack on a Pokemon with 55 Attack, in a room whose whole win condition is giving Honchkrow one safe turn. Rage Powder converts it into the enabler the Moxie plan needs, and the interaction to remember is a Focus Sash Jumpluff eating a double-target so a Life Orb Honchkrow can start snowballing. Honest counterplay: powder immunity (Grass types, Overcoat, Safety Goggles) blanks both of Jumpluff's key moves at once, Rock and Electric punish the whole flock, and Sucker Punch fails against any non-attacking turn. Level offsets are individual and flat at +2 across an evolved, well-rounded six - nothing here is a fragile pre-evolution needing compensation.
crack: Bring powder immunity and the support half of the room stops existing. After that, deny Honchkrow its first knockout - Moxie never starts - punish Sucker Punch by setting up or guarding instead of attacking, and remember Braviary's Choice Band locks it the moment it commits.

## E0285 TRAINER_FLINT class=gym
strategy: SETUP,ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Room concept - the room that uses Earthquake in a Flying gym. First two useful turns: Yanmega attacks or takes a Speed Boost turn behind Focus Sash while Archeops fires Earthquake beside it - both of Flint's leads are airborne, so the spread Ground move hits only the player's side. Turn two rechecks the state rather than the species list: a Roost, a Gravity, an Ingrain or an ability suppression grounds the partner and the same Earthquake becomes a mistake. Alternate deployment: Choice Scarf Flygon and Mega Salamence are the fastest version of the same idea, Levitate and Aerilate respectively, with Salamence's Double-Edge converting to Flying. Reserves & disruption: Tropius is the Harvest/Sitrus recovery body that keeps recycling its berry and draining with Leech Seed, and Dragonite is the Multiscale Dragon Dance option - note that Dragonite is grounded, so it is the one member that must never be on the field for an ally Earthquake. Archeops deliberately carries no item so its Acrobatics is at full 110 power, and its Defeatist is the honest price. AI executability: ALLY_COMBO is backed by a real, mechanical airborne-Earthquake relationship rather than a flavour claim, and SETUP by Dragon Dance. Could this team be cooler? This is already the best-conceived room in the gym and it keeps its composition - the itemless Acrobatics Archeops is a genuinely clever build and the Ground/Flying spread engine is the most distinctive thing in Fortree. What it lacked was individual levels: a blanket zero offset made a room with two legend-tier Dragons read the same as the filler rooms. The interaction to remember is a spread Earthquake that the player's team eats twice while Flint's pair floats above it. Honest counterplay: Ice and Rock hit almost everything, Wide Guard blanks both Earthquake and Rock Slide, and grounding effects turn the room's engine against itself.
crack: Wide Guard is unusually strong here - it blanks the spread Ground and Rock moves that are the room's whole plan. Otherwise Ice coverage sweeps five of six, Rock handles the rest, and anything that grounds Flint's own pair (Gravity, Smack Down, Mold Breaker) converts his Earthquake into a friendly-fire turn.

## E0286 TRAINER_ASHLEY class=gym
strategy: RAIN,SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
plan: Room concept - the storm room, and the only one in Fortree where the sky is wet. First two useful turns: Pelipper's Drizzle lands on entry and it chooses between Tailwind and immediate Hurricane/Muddy Water pressure - under its own rain Hurricane never misses, which is the reason this room exists. Gyarados leads beside it, drops Intimidate on both player slots and either attacks or takes one Dragon Dance behind Lum Berry; its Earthquake is a free spread move because every partner flies. The room's Electric answer is Emolga, the storm's own lightning. Motor Drive makes it immune to Electric moves and turns every one aimed at it into +1 Speed, so the Electric sweeper every Fortree challenger brings cannot touch it. Its job is to disarm that sweeper: Eerie Impulse cuts the Electric attacker's Special Attack by two stages, halving the spread Electric damage the four-times-weak flock would take, and Encore locks a terrain setter, Protect or setup move into repeating itself. Focus Sash guarantees it that action against the non-Electric hit. Under the rain Thunder never misses, so Emolga's own STAB punishes the Water and Flying types the player brings. Turn two converts: Mantine's Swift Swim comes online, Swanna's Hydration scrubs any status at end of turn and its Weather Ball is a 100-power Water move. Alternate deployment: Pelipper and Mantine are the bulky special rain pair when the player's lead punishes physical setup. Reserves & disruption: Mega Pidgeot's No Guard-grade Hurricane accuracy is redundant in rain but its Heat Wave is actively weakened by it - the AI must not pick the Fire option under its own weather. AI executability: RAIN has an automatic setter, TAILWIND a real user, SETUP a genuine Dragon Dance. Could this team be cooler? Yes. Five of six members took four-times Electric damage and one Earthquake on Gyarados was not enough to stop a terrain sweep. Cramorant goes: its Surf was the one move that hit partners, and it was another four-times Electric weakness. Emolga keeps the room a flock of storm birds and answers lightning with lightning rather than borrowing a ground-bound counter.
crack: Remove Emolga before it acts - it is frail once the Sash is broken, so a multi-hit or chip-then-hit works, and Ice or Rock hits it super-effectively - then Electric pressure works again. Physical Electric attackers ignore Eerie Impulse; switching resets it and Encore. Drought or Sand Stream removes Hurricane and Thunder accuracy, Mantine's Speed and Swanna's Weather Ball in one turn; Rock handles Pelipper and Pidgeot; Gyarados folds to a single Electric move if it spends its first turn on Dragon Dance instead of Earthquake.

## E0287 TRAINER_HUMBERTO class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room concept - six flyers that refuse to pay the usual price. Poison Heal turns poison into recovery, Magic Guard turns Life Orb recoil into nothing, Mirror Armor returns stat drops to the sender, Unburden turns a consumed item into doubled Speed, Motor Drive turns an Electric attack into Speed, and Magic Bounce sends every status move back where it came from. First two useful turns: Gliscor lets its Toxic Orb land - after that it is healing 12.5% a turn - and either attacks or takes a Swords Dance beside Corviknight, whose Mirror Armor punishes an Intimidate or Icy Wind lead by sending the drop straight back. Gliscor's Earthquake is the room's signature: every other member flies, so the spread attack hits only the player's side. Turn two accounts for the real state: Roost grounds Corviknight for the turn, and Poison Heal only exists after the Orb has actually activated. Alternate deployment: Hawlucha's White Herb is the room's cleverest line - Close Combat's own Defense drops consume the Herb, which triggers Unburden, which doubles its Speed; Sigilyph is the special counterpart, taking Life Orb recoil for free through Magic Guard. Reserves & disruption: Rotom-Fan is the Motor Drive pivot with Will-O-Wisp, and Xatu is the room's thesis turned against the player's support - Taunt, Spore, Will-O-Wisp, Thunder Wave, Encore and Stealth Rock aimed at it all bounce back, so the moves a player uses to beat a setup room are the ones that cannot touch it. Night Shade deals fixed damage and U-turn pivots it back out before it takes a real hit. AI executability: SETUP is backed by two Swords Dances; nothing claims a weather or terrain the room cannot make. Could this team be cooler? Shedinja was the old sixth - a brilliant drawback-denial piece, but a Bug/Ghost that cannot fly under a Bird Keeper in the Flying gym, and it survived Gliscor's Earthquake only through Wonder Guard. Xatu is a Hoenn bird, it is immune to the Earthquake like everyone else here, and Magic Bounce keeps the one-slot puzzle: the player must stop using status moves on it and answer it with damage. The moment to remember is a Taunt thrown at Xatu coming back to silence the player's own setter while Gliscor heals through its poison beside it. Honest counterplay: every one of these abilities has a named answer, and a player who brings two of them wins. Level offsets are +2 across the room; Shedinja's +4 existed only to buy a 1 HP body Attack and does not carry to Xatu.
crack: Answer the abilities one at a time. Attack Xatu rather than status it - Rock, Electric, Ice, Ghost and Dark all hit it super-effectively. Mold Breaker, Taunt or a simple knockout handles Gliscor before Poison Heal matters, and a Flying or Levitate partner ignores its Earthquake; attacking Corviknight beats Mirror Armor, which only reflects stat changes; Hawlucha is fast but frail - remove it before the White Herb is consumed. Ice and Rock coverage sweep the rest.

## E0288 TRAINER_DARIUS class=gym
strategy: TAILWIND,ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
tactic: ACTIVATE CHATOT TAILWIND KILOWATTREL
plan: Room concept - the wind itself, weaponised. First two useful turns: Chatot, faster than almost anything the player has at this stage, uses Tailwind, and that Tailwind is a wind move - Kilowattrel's Wind Power reads it and comes out of the turn Charged, so its next Thunderbolt is doubled. Mental Herb lets Chatot shrug off one Taunt and still set the wind. Turn two spends the charge and the speed together rather than re-setting Tailwind; Chatot's own Taunt is the alternative opening when the player's lead is a setter rather than an attacker. Alternate deployment: Chatot supporting Mega Aerodactyl is the physical mode - Rock Slide and Earthquake under Tailwind from a body that outspeeds essentially everything here. Reserves & disruption: Choice Specs Sheer Force Braviary-Hisui is the immediate special breaker, Togekiss is Serene Grace speed control and spread Fairy damage, and Unfezant is the crit lane - Super Luck plus Scope Lens plus Focus Energy reaches a guaranteed critical hit, which ignores the player's Intimidate, Reflect and Defense boosts entirely. Hurricane is inaccurate without rain and nothing here makes rain; do not grant it accuracy. Chatot is the fragile piece of the engine: it has no priority, so a faster Taunt, Fake Out or knockout on turn one stops the wind before it starts. Level offsets are individual: Chatot +0 as the support setter, Mega Aerodactyl -1, Unfezant +3 to make the crit lane threatening, the rest +2.
crack: Fake Out or a faster Taunt on Chatot is the cleanest answer to the room; Rock, Electric and Ice coverage hit most of the six, Wide Guard blanks Rock Slide and the spread Fairy damage, Unfezant's Focus Energy turn is a free turn for you, and Kilowattrel loses its charge the moment it attacks - deny it the Tailwind turn and it is an ordinary frail bird. Otherwise outlast the four Tailwind turns.

## E0289 TRAINER_WINONA_1 class=leader
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Leader concept - the whole sky, not one weather. Each of Winona's rooms showed the player a single trick; she brings two legendaries, a custom Mega and three of the best support tools in the type, and no two of her members are answered the same way. First two useful turns: Zapdos applies immediate Thunderbolt/Hurricane pressure while Altaria decides between Tailwind and Haze - Haze is the new and important option, because it erases a player's Dragon Dance, Calm Mind or Belly Drum on the turn it was supposed to pay off, and Natural Cure means Altaria can cycle a status off by switching. Turn two brings Enamorus into the matchup it wants: Contrary means Superpower raises its Attack instead of dropping it, so repeated Superpowers get stronger, and the AI must treat that as a growth curve rather than a one-shot. Alternate deployment: Gale Wings Talonflame gives priority Brave Bird from full health regardless of Tailwind, which is the answer when the player wins the speed war outright; Choice Specs Noivern with Infiltrator ignores screens and Substitutes. Reserves & disruption: Mega Skarmory is a separate endgame rather than a seventh attacker - Iron Defense into Body Press wins a long game that Winona's fast half cannot. AI executability: TAILWIND and SETUP both have real users; Ace Pokemon is correct for a team whose plan pivots on Enamorus. Could this team be cooler? The composition earns retention and the Mega register confirms it should keep it - Skarmorite has no other home in the campaign, so moving the stone to Altaria would orphan a custom Mega. What was genuinely weak was Altaria itself: four slots of Dragon Pulse, Roost, Helping Hand and Tailwind made the leader's signature Pokemon the least threatening thing on the field and gave her six broadly interchangeable attacking turns. Haze replaces Dragon Pulse so Altaria is the reason a player's setup plan fails, which is a much more memorable role for her ace than a resisted Dragon attack. Honest counterplay: Rock and Ice hit almost everything, but Skarmory laughs at both and needs special Fire or Electric instead - Winona is deliberately not solvable with one coverage move. Level offsets are individual and a clear step beyond the +2 gym rooms: Altaria, Talonflame and Noivern +3, Zapdos and Enamorus +2 as already-strong legendaries, Mega Skarmory +1.
crack: No single type answers her. Rock and Ice beat the fast half; Mega Skarmory needs special Fire or Electric damage and ignores both. Taunt Altaria before it Hazes away your setup, keep Talonflame below full health so Gale Wings loses priority, and refuse to let Enamorus stack Superpowers - every Contrary turn you allow makes the next one worse.

## E0297 TRAINER_MYLES class=ace
strategy: REDIRECTION,SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Togekiss redirects when it preserves a worthwhile Lucario attack; otherwise Tailwind or Air Slash may be better. The improvement worth naming is that Magmortar's Choice Specs locks the team's only Fire answer. Offsets individual and flat at +1 with Whiscash at +2 as the setup closer.
crack: Spread damage, Taunt (or outlasting Togekiss' Tailwind behind Protect), powder-independent disruption and Fairy/Ground pressure. Disposition: Keep; never credit all back-line setup plans to one impossible simultaneous protected board.

## E0298 TRAINER_PAT class=ace
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: 4
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Feraligatr and Bouffalant apply immediate complementary physical pressure; turn two pivots to special damage when Intimidate/burn makes continuing unfavorable. Alternate deployment: Electivire's Electroweb supports Mega Sceptile's special offense. Reserves & disruption: Simisear is genuinely mixed by EVs and moves, while Lycanroc supplies priority. No allied Electric hit is authored to guarantee Motor Drive. Counterplay & audit: Physical mitigation, priority, Grass/Ground/Fairy pressure and special bulk against the alternate mode. Disposition: Keep; don't invent a Motor Drive activation because two Electric-associated species are present. Could this team be cooler? Kept, and it is deliberately the broadest coverage six in the stretch: Sheer Force Feraligatr trading secondary effects for 1.3x damage, Motor Drive Electivire turning an Electric attack into a Speed boost, Mega Sceptile as the special Grass/Dragon lane, and Tough Claws Lycanroc-Dusk with Accelerock for priority. Reckless Bouffalant behind a Clear Amulet cannot be Intimidated out of Head Charge. The honest weakness is the same as most six-attacker teams - no support turn, no speed control, no way to buy a setup - so Pat is a wall of good individual builds rather than a plan. Flagged rather than rebuilt, because the coverage itself is what makes the fight hard. Offsets individual and flat at +1 with Lycanroc-Dusk at +2 as the priority closer.
crack: Physical mitigation, priority, Grass/Ground/Fairy pressure and special bulk against the alternate mode. Disposition: Keep; don't invent a Motor Drive activation because two Electric-associated species are present.

## E0299 TRAINER_MARCEL class=ace
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: First two useful turns: Shiftry buys Pawmot a useful action with Fake Out or sets Tailwind when speed matters; Pawmot attacks with Wild Charge, Close Combat or Play Rough. Turn two spends the Tailwind rather than re-setting it. Roles: Pawmot is the fast physical Electric/Fighting lead, and Volt Absorb makes it a free switch-in to the Electric attacks aimed at the team's Flying answers; Close Combat and Play Rough hit the Dark, Steel and Dragon bodies that wall its Electric STAB. Alternate deployment: Intimidate Stoutland supports Haxorus's Dragon Dance or immediate physical pressure, and its Wild Charge is the second Electric lane. Reserves & disruption: Shiftry's Wind Rider takes a free Attack boost from its own Tailwind, but its attacks are special Leaf Storm and Dark Pulse, so the boost matters only for immunity to wind moves - do not count it as damage. Haxorus is the ace: Mold Breaker ignores Levitate, Sturdy, Multiscale and Bulletproof, which is exactly the tool a Dragon Dance sweeper needs against the walls that would otherwise stop it. Offsets individual: Haxorus +1 above the rest as the ace.
crack: Ground, Psychic, Flying and Fairy attacks hit Pawmot super-effectively; priority denial and Fake Out on Shiftry stop the Tailwind; Fairy and Ice pressure on Haxorus before Dragon Dance; physical mitigation such as Intimidate and Will-O-Wisp blunts the three physical attackers; Fire, Bug, Flying and Fighting answer Shiftry.

## E0301 TRAINER_GABBY_AND_TY_5 class=regular
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Choice Scarf Toxtricity attacks or uses Snarl while Kommo-o attacks or takes Iron Defense only behind a safe board; turn two respects the Choice lock and the real Body Press value the Iron Defense created. Alternate deployment: Cloud Nine Altaria is the pair's actual identity - it suppresses every weather effect on the field, which at this point in the campaign means the player's own rain, sun or sand team is switched off for the whole fight, and so is any weather they were relying on to outspeed. Reserves & disruption: Primarina's Liquid Voice makes Hyper Voice a Water attack that hits both slots, and Punk Rock raises Toxtricity's sound moves while halving the sound damage it takes - so Boomburst-style pressure from the player is also blunted. Counterplay & audit: Wide Guard, Fairy and Ground pressure, Taunt on the Tailwind, and special mitigation. Could this team be cooler? Asked honestly, the answer is no - and that is the point of keeping it. The reporters are the campaign's recurring ordinary encounter, and this is the one team in their run that does something no gym or villain does: it deletes weather as a mechanic for four Pokemon at once, which is a genuinely awkward surprise immediately before an arc built almost entirely on rain, sun and sand. Changing it would remove the only weather-suppression team in the campaign. The sets are correct as built: Cloud Nine is the concept, Punk Rock and Liquid Voice are the sound-and-water lane around it, and Kommo-o is the Body Press body that does not care about the missing weather.
crack: Wide Guard against the spread sound moves; Fairy and Ground pressure; Taunt Altaria before the Tailwind; bring a team that does not need weather to function.

## E0302 TRAINER_BRENDAN_LILYCOVE_MUDKIP class=rival
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 5
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: The Lilycove rival is one design in six printings: five fixed members that are the rival's actual team, plus the starter that beats the player's. First two useful turns: Swellow's Flame Orb activates at the end of turn one and Guts then raises its Attack by 50 percent while the burn cannot reduce it, because Facade doubles against a statused user - so the rival's opening is to survive one turn and then hit with a 140 base power attack. Manectric's Lightning Rod draws every Electric attack on the field to itself and converts it into +1 Special Attack, and Volt Switch keeps the rival's positioning ahead of the player's. Alternate deployment: Breloom's Toxic Orb plus Poison Heal makes it immune to further status while healing an eighth per turn, and Spore is the most reliable disruption in the game; Hariyama's Thick Fat halves the Fire and Ice attacks aimed at the rival's Grass and Flying members and Fake Out buys the Mega turn. Reserves & disruption: Mega Salamence is the rival's constant ace in every variant - Aerilate turns Double-Edge into a Flying attack with a 20 percent boost, and Dragon Dance behind Intimidate makes it the win condition. The sixth slot is the starter chosen to beat the player's, and it is the only thing that changes between the six branches. Counterplay & audit: Grass and powder immunity, Electric immunity or a non-Electric answer to Manectric, Fairy and Ice pressure on Salamence, and denying the free Dragon Dance turn. Could this team be cooler? The old six branches differed in both the starter and the Mega, which meant the rival had three different aces and no identity at all - and two of those Megas, Metagross and Tyranitar, now belong to Magma's Space Center raid and its hideout gate. It is cooler now because the rival is a person the player can learn: the same five Pokemon, the same Guts-and-Poison-Heal status engine, the same Mega Salamence, and one slot that says "I picked the one that beats yours". That is a rival, rather than a randomiser.
crack: Grass typing or Safety Goggles beats Spore; do not aim Electric attacks at Manectric; Fairy, Ice and Rock pressure on Salamence; Fake Out and priority deny the Dragon Dance turn; the Flame and Toxic Orbs are Knock Off targets on turn one.

## E0303 TRAINER_GABBY_AND_TY_6 class=regular
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Noivern sets Tailwind or attacks while Exploud fires Boomburst - 140 base power into every other Pokemon on the field, including its own partner. That is the reporters' actual trick: both Noivern and Gardevoir carry Telepathy, which makes them immune to damage from an ally's moves, so Exploud can spam its best move with no cost while the player's side takes it twice. Scrappy also lets Boomburst and Hyper Voice hit Ghost types, which are the usual answer to a Normal attacker. Alternate deployment: Sylveon's Pixilate makes Hyper Voice a Fairy spread attack and Throat Spray raises its Special Attack the first time it uses a sound move. Reserves & disruption: Super Fang from Noivern halves a wall the sound lane cannot break. Counterplay & audit: Soundproof is a complete immunity to four of the eight moves on this team, Wide Guard blocks every spread sound move, and Steel and Poison typing answers the Fairy lane. Could this team be cooler? Asked honestly, no - this is already one of the best-built ordinary encounters in the campaign and the audit leaves the roster alone. What it did not do before was state its own combination: Telepathy exists on two of the four specifically so that Boomburst is free, and that is the thing a player should notice and steal. The reporters escalating from weather suppression in their fifth battle to an ally-immune spread engine in their sixth is exactly the recurring-encounter arc this campaign wants.
crack: Soundproof, Wide Guard or a Throat Chop user shuts most of this team down; Steel and Poison answer the Fairy lane; Rock, Ice and Dragon pressure on Noivern; Exploud is slow and frail.

## E0304 TRAINER_BUFFEL class=boss
strategy: REDIRECTION,SETUP
mega_slots: 2
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Final expedition trial. Fake Out and Follow Me create setup turns for Calyrex or the reserved Volcarona. Mega Milotic punishes Intimidate, while Zamazenta supplies Wide Guard against the spread attacks that normally bypass redirection. Preserve the authored coordination planner; use only revealed opponent information.

## E0311 TRAINER_GABRIELLE_1 class=ace
strategy: SNOW,SETUP
mega_slots: 1
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Mega Abomasnow's Snow Warning is automatic and snow raises the Defense of every Ice type on the field by 50 percent, so Gabrielle's whole team becomes physically bulkier the moment the battle starts. Alolan Sandslash's Slush Rush doubles its Speed in that snow and Cetitan's does the same, so the team has two sweepers running off one weather. Alternate deployment: Frosmoth's Ice Scales halves every special attack it takes, which is the answer to the Fire and Rock special coverage a player brings to an Ice team, and Quiver Dance behind that makes it the win condition. Reserves & disruption: Avalugg's Sturdy guarantees one survived hit and Body Press converts its 184 base Defense - already raised by snow - into damage; Mamoswine's Thick Fat halves the Fire and Ice attacks aimed at it and Ice Shard is the priority finish. Counterplay & audit: replace the weather and both Slush Rush sweepers halve, Fire and Fighting and Rock and Steel all bite, and Frosmoth folds instantly to physical damage. Could this team be cooler? The old build was flagged RAIN and SNOW at once and actually ran both - a Gorebyss using Rain Dance beside a Snow Warning Abomasnow, so the two engines deleted each other every time either resolved. That is a genuine strategic defect, not a flavour note. It is cooler now because it is one committed weather with two Slush Rush abusers, an Ice Scales special wall and a Body Press physical wall, and because Frosmoth and Cetitan are both first appearances in the campaign. Asked again after the revision: the team now has a real reason to be an ace-class fight instead of a pile of cold Pokemon.
crack: Change the weather and the team loses half its Speed; Fire, Fighting, Rock and Steel pressure; Frosmoth dies to any physical attack; Stealth Rock-style chip undoes Sturdy.

## E0317 TRAINER_GRUNT_MT_PYRE_2 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The grave robbers, working the tomb floors. First two useful turns: Weavile's Fake Out buys the turn and Antique Polteageist spends it on Shell Smash - +2 Special Attack, +2 Special Defense-free, +2 Speed - with a White Herb restoring the defence drops immediately, so the crew converts one bought turn into a genuine sweeper. Mismagius' Levitate keeps it above the Ground attacks the player brought for the earlier Poison crew. The summit's permanent fog opens every battle with Misty Terrain, which stops burns, poison, paralysis, sleep and confusion on any grounded target, so this crew carries no status move: Mismagius attacks with Mystical Fire, whose Special Attack drop the terrain does not block, instead of the Will-O-Wisp the fog would blank. Alternate deployment: if Polteageist is threatened, Carracosta's Sturdy guarantees it survives one hit and Shell Smash on a 74/85 body behind Solid Rock is the slower, tougher version of the same plan. Reserves & disruption: Weavile's Knock Off strips the White Herb or Focus Sash the player is relying on and Icicle Crash covers the Flying answers. Counterplay & audit: Fake Out and Taunt beat both setup lines, Haze undoes them, and Dark and Ghost pressure removes the frail bodies. The terrain protects the player's grounded Pokemon from status too, so the fight is decided by damage and setup alone. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Bruxish, Weavile, Wailord and Polteageist - a whale inside a mountain tomb, and Wailord and Bruxish are both wanted elsewhere. It is cooler now because it commits to the location: Aqua looting a graveyard means a haunted teapot, a witch's hat and a fossil turtle, and the room's idea is that a bought turn becomes a Shell Smash twice over. Cursed Body on Polteageist also makes the player's strongest attack a gamble - disabling it is a real, visible consequence.
crack: Fake Out, Taunt or priority denies the setup turn; Misty Terrain means nothing on this team can burn or poison you, so commit physical attackers freely; Haze and Clear Smog undo it; Dark and Ghost pressure; Knock Off the White Herb before the Smash.

## E0318 TRAINER_GRUNT_MT_PYRE_3 class=grunt
strategy: TRICK_ROOM,REDIRECTION
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The upper tomb, and the mountain's one Trick Room. First two useful turns: Spiritomb has no weaknesses except Fairy and 35 base Speed, so it is the safest Trick Room setter in the region; it sets the room when the slow pair can spend it and uses Sucker Punch or Snarl otherwise, with Pressure draining the player's PP on the long turns and Pain Split keeping the setter alive for a second room. The summit's permanent fog opens every battle with Misty Terrain, which blocks burns and every other status on grounded targets, so the crew's disruption is stat drops rather than status: Snarl lowers both foes' Special Attack and Drifblim's Strength Sap lowers one foe's Attack while healing Drifblim, and the terrain blocks neither. Huntail then attacks first at 52 Speed under reversed order, and Shell Smash is available when the room is already up and the player has no answer left. Alternate deployment: Lumineon's Storm Drain is the room's redirection - it pulls every single-target Water attack on the field to itself, gains +1 Special Attack for each, and it is the Water immunity the player does not expect from a fish. Reserves & disruption: Drifblim's Aftermath punishes the contact knockout, Strength Sap is the physical deterrent Will-O-Wisp used to be, and Mystical Fire is the special one. Counterplay & audit: Taunt the setter, Fairy pressure on Spiritomb, spread Water moves ignore Storm Drain, and running out the five turns is always available. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Jellicent, Amoonguss, Lanturn and Golisopod - a Grass mushroom redirecting on a mountain of the dead, and three species other rooms now own. It is cooler now because the redirection and the room come from the location: a ghost with no weaknesses sets the tempo, a deep-sea eel profits from it, and a butterfly fish quietly turns the player's Water coverage into free Special Attack. Storm Drain instead of Rage Powder also means Safety Goggles - the player's usual answer - does nothing.
crack: Taunt or Fairy pressure on Spiritomb; the terrain means nothing here can burn or poison you, but Snarl and Strength Sap still shrink your attacks; spread Water moves bypass Storm Drain; Protect through the five turns; Ghost and Dark pressure on Drifblim.

The old block was Carracosta, Liepard, Qwilfish and Drapion - all four of which now belong to other rooms, and none of which said "summit". It is cooler now because the gate crew is made of predators that belong on a cold mountain: a climbing Hisuian cat, a disaster omen, a snow bride and a psychic piranha, with one clean escalating idea - Fake Out, then a doubled-speed, doubled-power Acrobatics once the Berry is gone. Asked again after the revision: yes, and the crit lane on Absol is the part worth keeping, because it is the one thing in the Aqua arc that punishes a player who wins by stacking defensive boosts.
crack: Deny or Knock Off the Berry to stop Unburden and keep Acrobatics at half power; Rock and Steel types resist Acrobatics; Fighting and Fairy pressure; Fire, Rock and Steel on Froslass; Bruxish is frail and dies to priority.

## E0320 TRAINER_MATT_MT_PYRE class=admin
strategy: RAIN,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Matt's first showing, and the template the player is meant to recognise when he returns to the hideout with screens and a Last Respects clock. First two useful turns: Pelipper's Drizzle is free on entry - this is Matt's bird in both of his battles - and its own action is Tailwind or Wide Guard depending on whether the crew needs speed or cover. Araquanid then attacks: Water Bubble doubles the power of its Water moves and halves incoming Fire damage, so a rain-boosted, Bubble-doubled Liquidation off a modest Attack stat hits harder than anything else in the Aqua arc so far. Alternate deployment: Ludicolo's Swift Swim makes it the fastest thing on the field in rain and Fake Out plus Ice Beam gives Matt a disruption lane; Heliolisk's Dry Skin heals it in the same rain that makes its Thunder never miss, and Electroweb is its speed control. Reserves & disruption: the summit's permanent fog opens every battle with Misty Terrain beside Matt's rain, and the terrain stops poison, paralysis and burns on grounded targets - so Heliolisk carries Electroweb rather than Glare, and Okidogi runs Guard Dog rather than Toxic Chain. Guard Dog turns an Intimidate into +1 Attack and cannot be forced out by Roar or Whirlwind, which is how Matt's guard dog holds the gate against the bulky ground the player brings. Mega Golisopod is the ace - First Impression is priority, and the plain Golisopod guarding the Aqua Hideout later is deliberately the same species without the stone. Counterplay & audit: replace the weather and three of the six engines weaken at once; Electric and Rock on Pelipper; Grass and Fairy pressure. Could this team be cooler? The previous build shared Toxicroak, Seismitoad and Golisopod with the Aqua Hideout grunts, so the admin looked like a grunt with better levels. It is cooler now because every slot states something the grunts cannot: an Intimidate-proof guard dog, an ability that doubles a move's power, a Dry Skin rain healer and a priority Mega. Araquanid is the standout - a water-bubble spider is a genuinely strange, memorable thing for a pirate to keep, and doubled Water damage is the clearest possible statement that the rain is not decoration.
crack: Change the weather first; Electric and Rock pressure on Pelipper; Fairy and Psychic on Okidogi, and never lead Intimidate into it; Wide Guard and Protect blunt the spread turns; Golisopod's First Impression only works on its first turn out.

## E0322 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_2 class=grunt
strategy: SAND,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room two, the drill gallery, and the hideout's first weather engine. First two useful turns: Hippowdon's Sand Stream is automatic, so its own action is High Horsepower or a Yawn that forces the switch Sandslash wants; Sandslash attacks at doubled Sand Rush Speed immediately rather than waiting. Stunfisk is the room's answer to the rain lead, and it only works because this is a Ground room: Discharge and Electroweb hit every adjacent Pokemon, and every one of its partners - Hippowdon, Sandslash, Golem - is a Ground type immune to Electric. So the spread Electric moves never touch an ally. Discharge is four-times effective on a Water/Flying rain setter, super-effective on any Water attacker, and paralyses 30% of the time; Electroweb slows both foes, which is the speed control a Swift Swim lead otherwise takes away. Ground/Electric takes Water only neutrally, Assault Vest and 109 base HP take a rain-boosted special hit, and Sludge Bomb hits Fairy-type Water attackers super-effectively. Alternate deployment: Golem is the bait for the obvious counter. Sturdy guarantees it survives the four-times Water or Grass hit the player brings for a Ground room, Weakness Policy turns that hit into a free +2/+2, and it answers with exactly the punch that type fears - Thunder Punch into the Water attacker, Fire Punch into the Grass one. Reserves & disruption: sand chips every non Rock/Ground/Steel body on the player's side for the whole room and is the setup the deeper sand rooms reuse; Static paralyses whatever touches Stunfisk. Counterplay & audit: Grass and Ground hit Stunfisk super-effectively, and Ground types ignore its Electric moves entirely. Could this team be cooler? The pure-Ground crew had no reply to a rain lead: every member took doubled or quadrupled Water and rain erased the sand. Donphan was the redundant second Sturdy body, and its Thunder Fang was a token Water answer. It is cooler now because the Electric answer is shaped by the room: a trap-fish from the gallery mud fires spread lightning that its Ground crewmates are built to ignore.
crack: Grass or Ground removes Stunfisk first, or lead your own Ground type and its Electric moves do nothing; after that Water and Grass flatten Hippowdon and Sandslash. Hit Golem with a neutral attack so Weakness Policy never fires, or break Sturdy with a multi-hit move. Your own weather ends Sand Rush, and Wide Guard blanks the Rock Slides and Discharge.

## E0324 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_3 class=grunt
strategy: TRICK_ROOM
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room four, the relic dig, and the hideout's junior Trick Room - deliberately a smaller rehearsal of the room Maxie himself runs at the bottom. First two useful turns: Claydol sets Trick Room only when it survives the turn and at least one of Bronzong, Runerigus or Garbodor is on the field to spend it; otherwise it attacks with Earth Power. Bronzong under reversed order fires Gyro Ball at its real 33 Speed, which is close to maximum power. Alternate deployment: Runerigus' Wandering Spirit swaps abilities with a contact attacker, which can strand a Guts, Speed Boost or Protean sweeper with a useless ability for the rest of the fight. Reserves & disruption: Garbodor lays Toxic Spikes only if the door crew's set was removed, otherwise it attacks; Aftermath punishes the contact finish. Counterplay & audit: Taunt the setter, run out the five turns behind Protect, or use Fighting and Fire pressure that does not need contact. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Claydol/Garbodor/Umbreon/Camerupt - a second Camerupt in the same hideout and an Umbreon, which is a moonlight Eeveelution and about as un-Magma as a Pokemon gets. It is cooler now because every member is something the crew dug out of the volcano - a clay idol, a temple bell, a cursed coffin lid and the base's own garbage - and because Wandering Spirit gives the room a signature the player will remember: punching Runerigus can cost you your ability for the rest of the battle.
crack: Taunt or KO Claydol before the room turns; Protect through the five turns; Wandering Spirit only triggers on contact, so spread and special attacks are safe answers.

## E0325 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_4 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room five, the boiler landing, where the raid stops being polite. First two useful turns: Scrafty's Fake Out buys exactly one turn and is spent on the turn Magcargo actually wants - Simple Shell Smash is +4/+4/+2 in one action, and the White Herb immediately restores the defence drops, so the room's whole plan is to survive to that second turn. Alternate deployment: if Magcargo is not safe, Revavroom takes Shift Gear instead and the room becomes a physical Steel/Poison race. Reserves & disruption: Heatmor's Tough Claws Fire Lash cuts Defense on the target every hit, which is the follow-up lane when the setup line is denied. Counterplay & audit: Taunt, Fake Out of your own, Haze/Clear Smog, or simply removing the 50 HP Magcargo before it moves. Could this team be cooler? The old block had Galarian Slowking on a Magma grunt, which is a poisoned royal from another region's story entirely. It is cooler now because the four members are one mechanical idea escalating - buy a turn, take the single most explosive setup move in the game on a Pokemon nobody respects, and back it with a second Shift Gear engine so the player cannot solve the room by aiming at one target. Magcargo going from a joke to a +4 attacker in one turn is the memory the room is built to leave.
crack: Fake Out, Taunt, priority and spread damage all beat a 50 HP setup sweeper; Haze or Clear Smog undoes the Smash; Ground and Water attacks remove Magcargo outright.

## E0326 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_5 class=grunt
strategy: SAND,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE

Bisharp attacks; if anything lowers its stats, Defiant answers with +2 Attack and Sucker Punch becomes lethal. It is cooler now because the room punishes the player's reflexes in two directions at once: Intimidating or Icy Winding the team feeds Defiant, and attacking Sandaconda hands the room its weather. Every "safe" opening move the player has is the wrong one somewhere.

## E0327 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_15 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room seven, the ambush on the stairs. First two useful turns: Incineroar opens with Fake Out and its Intimidate has already cut the player's physical lead, then Armarouge fires Armor Cannon - 120 base power that drops its own defences, which is exactly the trade an ambush crew makes. Alternate deployment: Klawf's Anger Shell means the player's own strong hit flips it to +1 Attack, +1 Special Attack and +1 Speed below half HP, so chipping it is worse than killing it. Reserves & disruption: Muk's Poison Touch poisons through contact and Parting Shot from Incineroar resets a bad board. ## E0328 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_6 class=grunt
strategy: REDIRECTION
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room eight, the web gallery, and the hideout's one redirection room. First two useful turns: Ariados chooses between Sticky Web, which slows every later Magma room's opposition for the rest of this battle, and Rage Powder, which buys Infernape or Magmortar a clean turn - it does not do both, and Insomnia means sleep will not shortcut the choice. Infernape uses Fake Out only when the bought turn is actually worth more than immediate damage. Alternate deployment: Magmortar behind redirection is the room's real damage, with Heat Wave into both slots. Reserves & disruption: Nidoking's Sheer Force Earth Power is the answer to Fire-immune and Rock bodies. The signature here stays the honest doubles question Rage Powder asks: spend a spread move and waste the redirection, or aim single-target and lose the turn.

## E0329 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_7 class=grunt
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room nine, the stokers' pit, built entirely out of contact punishment. First two useful turns: Electabuzz leads and is the room's answer to the Water the player brings for a Fire room. It is pure Electric, so Water hits it only neutrally, and it outspeeds the usual rain lead: Thunderbolt is four-times effective on a Water/Flying rain setter and super-effective on any Water attacker, and Light Screen halves the rain-boosted special Water hits aimed at the Fire members for five turns. Static paralyses whatever touches it and Eviolite keeps it standing; Volt Switch pivots it out once the screen is up. Centiskorch takes Coil behind that board - +1 Attack, +1 Defense and +1 accuracy, which makes Fire Lash reliable - or attacks immediately; Arcanine's Intimidate lands and it uses Extreme Speed or Flare Blitz, with Wild Charge as its own Water answer. Alternate deployment: Weezing's Levitate ignores the Ground lane the player brought for this hideout, and Will-O-Wisp plus Black Sludge grinds physical attackers down. Reserves & disruption: rain halves the room's Fire damage, so Electabuzz's screen and Thunderbolt carry the rain turns while the burners wait them out. Counterplay & audit: Ground types ignore Electabuzz entirely, special attackers ignore the burn package, and Fire immunity or Guts inverts the room. Could this team be cooler? The all-burn room had no reply to a Water lead - rain halved every Fire move and Larvesta, the Eviolite wall, was just another Water-weak body. The Electric member is the answer the owner's rule names for an all-Fire team, and Electabuzz inherits Larvesta's Eviolite role while keeping the room's idea: touch anything here and you are burned or paralysed. Electabuzz and its rival Magmar's line share the stokehold, one floor apart.
crack: Lead Ground types - Electabuzz cannot touch them and every Fire member is Ground-weak; otherwise remove Electabuzz first with a physical hit that does not make contact, then Rock and Water pressure removes Centiskorch and Arcanine. Special attackers ignore the burns; Flash Fire, Water Veil, Guts and Lum Berry invert them; Brick Break or Psychic Fangs removes Light Screen.

## E0331 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_9 class=grunt
strategy: ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room eleven, the standing stones, and the hideout's most distinctive small engine. First two useful turns: Stonjourner does not need an action to matter - Power Spot raises every ally attack by 30 percent simply by standing in the slot, so Choice Band Darmanitan's Sheer Force Flare Blitz lands at roughly double a normal grunt's damage on turn one. Stonjourner's own action is Wide Guard against the player's obvious spread answer, or Rock Slide. Alternate deployment: if Darmanitan is removed, Camerupt takes the boosted slot and Heat Wave becomes the spread threat. Reserves & disruption: Crustle's Shell Armor denies critical hits and its Sturdy-adjacent bulk holds the slot while the battery stays alive. Counterplay & audit: the whole room collapses if Stonjourner leaves the field, so it is a redirection-free "protect the battery" puzzle; Water, Grass, Steel and Fighting all remove it. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Camerupt/Honchkrow/Shiftry/Darmanitan - two Dark birds-and-trees that belong to a forest, not a magma chamber. It is cooler now because Power Spot is a mechanic almost no trainer in the campaign uses and it produces a single, legible threat: kill the rock circle or take double damage. Stonjourner is also exactly the right image for a volcano base - a henge the crew dug up and stood back on its feet - and this is Camerupt's second and last grunt appearance.
crack: Remove Stonjourner first and the room's damage halves; Wide Guard and Protect blunt the boosted spread turn; Water and Ground pressure covers everything here.

## E0333 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_10 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room thirteen, the ash pit, haunted by three ghosts. First two useful turns: Palossand's Water Compaction gives it +2 Defense every time a Water attack hits it, which inverts the answer the player has been using on Fire and Ground rooms all hideout; its own action is Shadow Ball or Shore Up. Hisuian Typhlosion attacks with Infernal Parade, which doubles in power against a statused target - and this hideout has been applying Toxic Spikes, burns and poison since room one, so that condition is usually already met. Trevenant is the charred tree that answers the rain lead: Grass/Ghost resists Water and Electric, so rain-boosted Hydro Pumps and Surf barely scratch it, Horn Leech hits Water types super-effectively and heals, and Poltergeist is its heavy Ghost hit. Alternate deployment: Mightyena's second and final grunt appearance is the Intimidate body that keeps the slower members alive, and Howl is the room's setup. Could this team be cooler? The room punished habits but not the rain lead - Palossand, Typhlosion and Alolan Dugtrio all took super-effective special Water, and Palossand's Water Compaction only raises Defense. Dugtrio's Sand Force lane depended on a leftover sandstorm that rain removes anyway. It is cooler now because the ash pit is a ghost story: a sand-castle ghost, a ghost of fire and a burnt tree spirit, and the tree is the one member that shrugs off the storm.
crack: Hurricane, Fire, Ice or Dark removes Trevenant; Grass and Ice attacks beat Palossand where Water does not; enter clean of status to halve Infernal Parade; Fighting and Fairy pressure on Mightyena, and Water still works on Typhlosion once Trevenant is gone.

## E0334 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_11 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room fourteen, the armoury, and the first room the player should expect to lose to. First two useful turns: Orthworm's Earth Eater makes it immune to Ground attacks and heals it for a quarter instead, so it holds a slot indefinitely and Coil makes it a real attacker while it does; the player's Ground lane, which this hideout has been demanding since room two, is dead here. Pangoro's Iron Fist Drain Punch is the sustain lane and Parting Shot resets a losing board. Alternate deployment: Aggron's Heavy Metal doubles its weight, which makes the player's Heavy Slam and Grass Knot maths worse and its own Heavy Slam maximal. Reserves & disruption: Toedscruel is the odd one out on purpose - cave fungus, not a Magma recruit - and its Mycelium Might Spore ignores abilities like Overcoat-adjacent immunities but always moves last, so the AI should use it as a board-flip, not an opener. Counterplay & audit: Fighting, Fire and Ground-free coverage; Safety Goggles or a Grass typing blanks Spore. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Mamoswine/Mandibuzz/Scolipede/Delcatty, which included an Ice mammoth and a house cat in a volcano. It is cooler now because the room is a specific, teachable wall: everything the player has been rewarded for bringing to this hideout - Ground coverage and physical priority - is exactly what Earth Eater, Heavy Metal and Iron Fist sustain answer. Orthworm, Toedscruel and Pangoro are all first appearances.
crack: Bring Water, Grass or Special Fighting coverage rather than Ground; Safety Goggles or Grass typing beats Spore; Aggron folds to Fighting and Ground special attacks.

## E0335 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_12 class=grunt
strategy: SUN,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room fifteen, the caldera floor, and the rehearsal for Courtney's sun. First two useful turns: Torkoal's Drought is automatic, so its first action is a full-HP Eruption - 150 base power, sun-boosted, into both of the player's slots - and the room is designed so the player must answer that before turn two. Scovillain's Chlorophyll doubles its Speed in that sun, and its Rage Powder is a Fire-typed redirection almost nothing else in the campaign offers, so it can buy Torkoal a second full-power Eruption. Alternate deployment: Volcarona takes Quiver Dance behind the redirection; at +1 in sun it is the single most dangerous grunt Pokemon in the hideout. Reserves & disruption: Garchomp is the non-sun lane and its Rough Skin punishes contact. Counterplay & audit: rain or sand replacement immediately halves Eruption's Fire boost, chip damage lowers Eruption's power directly, and Rock pressure ends Volcarona in one action. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Sableye/Torkoal/Excadrill/Garchomp - a Quash gimmick attached to a sun engine that did not need it. It is cooler now because the room is a two-stage threat that teaches the boss fight: Eruption punishes you for not acting, and the thing that buys Eruption a second turn is a Fire-typed Rage Powder user you cannot answer with Safety Goggles habits alone. Chip Torkoal and the room's damage collapses - that is the lesson the player needs before Maxie.

## E0336 TRAINER_GRUNT_MAGMA_HIDEOUT_13 class=grunt
strategy: SAND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room sixteen, the iron gate outside the admins' chamber, and the hardest grunt fight in the campaign so far. First two useful turns: Tyranitar's Sand Stream overwrites whatever weather the player installed in the last room - including the rain a player brings for a Rock and Steel gate - and its Assault Vest makes the special answer to it fail; it attacks immediately, and Ice Punch is there for the Ground or Flying lead that expects to walk through. Probopass' Magnet Pull traps the player's Steel switch-ins - the exact Pokemon a player brings to answer a Rock and Ground hideout - and its Rock/Steel body ignores the sand while Leftovers keeps it there. Alternate deployment: Steelix takes Wide Guard against the player's spread answer - the rain-boosted Surf and the Earthquake this gate invites - and Body Press converts its enormous Defense into damage. Reserves & disruption: Ferrothorn is the thorn in the gate and the answer to its obvious counter. Grass/Steel resists Water, takes Ground only neutrally and cannot be chipped by the sand, so the Water and Ground attackers that fold the other three meet a wall that Power Whips them for super-effective damage and Leech Seeds whatever stays; Iron Barbs punishes contact, Gyro Ball off its crawling Speed is the room's Steel damage into Fairy and Ice, and the Occa Berry means the one Fire attack the player saves for it takes two tries. That frees Probopass to carry Dazzling Gleam, a spread hit into the Fighting attackers the rest of the gate fears. Garchomp is absent here; this room deliberately has no fast member, so the player who solved earlier rooms with speed has to solve this one with damage. Counterplay & audit: Fighting still breaks all four if it survives the Gleam, Fire burns Ferrothorn through its berry, and Steel-typed players must be aware they cannot switch out; Water and Ground still work on Tyranitar, Steelix and Probopass once Ferrothorn is removed or ignored. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Rhyperior/Amoonguss/Pangoro/Tauros - a mushroom and a cow standing between the player and Maxie - and the first rebuild's Rhydon only repeated Tyranitar's Rock and Ground lane, so rain and a Landorus walked through all four. It is cooler now because it is a gate rather than a team: sand to undo the player's weather, a trap to punish the natural Steel answer, a wall that converts defence into offence, and a thorned iron bar across the Water and Ground answer everything else here fears. Trapping the player's Steel pivot with Magnet Pull immediately before the admin gauntlet is the most consequential grunt decision in the hideout.
crack: Do not bring a Steel pivot you need to rotate; Fire through Ferrothorn's Occa Berry, then Water and Ground into the other three; Fighting pressure once Probopass is gone; Taunt on Steelix and Ferrothorn; the room has no speed control, so fast special attackers dictate the pace.

## E0337 TRAINER_TABITHA_MAGMA_HIDEOUT class=admin
strategy: SAND,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Tabitha runs the excavation, so his battle is the hideout's sand engine executed properly. First two useful turns: Gigalith's Sand Stream with Smooth Rock lasts eight turns and Excadrill's Sand Rush doubles its Speed for all of them, so the opening is Gigalith attacking while Excadrill outruns almost anything the player owns. Gigalith's own alternative action is Wide Guard when the player's answer to a Rock-heavy admin is a spread move. Alternate deployment: Coalossal's Steam Engine is NOT self-activated here - the previous Mt Chimney fight already used that trick and repeating it would be a weaker second showing. Instead Coalossal is a trap: any Fire or Water attack the player aims at it, including the Water attack that is 4x super effective and the obvious answer to a Rock/Fire body, grants +6 Speed if it survives, and Weakness Policy grants +2/+2 on top. Killing it outright is correct; chipping it is a disaster. Reserves & disruption: Volcanion is the faction's captured volcano spirit and Steam Eruption off Choice Specs is the admin's raw damage; Krookodile's Moxie converts a knockout into a sweep. Mega Golurk with Iron Fist is the ace and its Shadow Punch and Drain Punch are both boosted. Counterplay & audit: one large hit on Coalossal rather than two, weather replacement to strip Sand Rush, Wide Guard against the paired Rock lane, and Water/Ground pressure. Could this team be cooler? The previous build had Dragapult firing Surf at its own Coalossal - a 4x super effective Water attack on a Rock/Fire ally, which is a genuine design defect, not a clever combo, and Dragapult is a Ghost dragon with no Magma identity. It is cooler now because removing the broken self-hit turns Coalossal into the fight's real puzzle, because Excadrill here foreshadows the Mega Excadrill Tabitha brings to the Space Center, and because Volcanion - literally the volcano Pokemon - is the correct legendary for a man who digs into volcanoes.
crack: Kill Coalossal in one action or leave it alone entirely; replace the sand to halve Excadrill; Water, Ground and Fighting pressure; Mega Golurk is fast for its class but folds to Ghost, Dark, Grass and Water.

## E0338 TRAINER_COURTNEY_MAGMA_HIDEOUT class=admin
strategy: SUN,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Courtney is the faction's analyst, and the analyst's fight is the sun engine built so that the obvious answer to sun does not work. First two useful turns: Torkoal leads and sets Drought from base 20 Speed, so against a Drizzle lead its sun is applied last and wins the turn-0 weather race outright; Heat Rock makes it eight turns. Gastrodon leads beside it and Storm Drain is the trap: every single-target Water attack aimed at Torkoal, or at anything else on her side, is pulled into a Water-immune body and converts into +1 Special Attack for Earth Power and Ice Beam, so the player's Water answer feeds her instead of ending her. Torkoal's own action is a full-HP sun Eruption into both slots or a Yawn that forces the switch Storm Drain is waiting for. Alternate deployment: Great Tusk's Protosynthesis triggers on the sun and Headlong Rush lands from +1 Attack; Turtonator is the second body that does not fold to Water - Fire/Dragon takes it neutral - and its White Herb Shell Smash followed by sun Heat Wave is the mid-game sweep. Reserves & disruption: Ninetales is the second sun source. Its Drought re-sets the weather the moment it enters after a Pelipper has left, and Sunny Day re-sets it by hand while it is standing there, so a player who wins the weather once has to win it again. Mega Pyroar is the ace: Competitive means any Intimidate or Icy Wind hands it +2 Special Attack and Hyper Voice plus sun Heat Wave is the spread finish. Counterplay & audit: spread Water damage (Surf, Muddy Water) still hits the sun bodies around Gastrodon; Grass into Gastrodon; Rock and Ground into the Fire bodies; kill Turtonator before the Smash, and remove Ninetales before the second sun. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and it is now. The previous build was six Fire-coloured bodies and one base-100 Drought that any Pelipper overwrote for free on turn 0, which switched off the ability, the Paradox ability and a move before anyone acted, and left every member 2x into the Water attack that followed. It is cooler now because the analyst's weather actually arrives - it comes from the slowest Drought in the game and can be retaken twice - and because Storm Drain turns the player's Water response into her damage, which is the same trick Tabitha's Coalossal pulls with Steam Engine one room earlier and makes the two admins visibly the same faction. Chi-Yu, Ceruledge and Salazzle were good species that never got to act; the roster that replaces them is the one the analyst would build after reading the player's team.
crack: Spread Water damage goes around Storm Drain; Grass into Gastrodon; Rock and Ground into Torkoal, Ninetales and Pyroar; kill Turtonator before Shell Smash; take Ninetales out before it can bring the sun back; never lower Pyroar's stats.

## E0339 TRAINER_MAXIE_MAGMA_HIDEOUT class=leader
strategy: TRICK_ROOM,SUN,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
field: WEATHER_SUN
plan: Maxie wakes Groudon before this battle and fights with it standing in the chamber, so the battle opens in the harsh sunlight of a starting field. That sun is a head start, not a lock - a player's Drizzle replaces it on switch-in - so Maxie now carries the sun himself. First two useful turns: Torkoal and Runerigus lead. Torkoal's base 20 Speed with zero Speed IVs makes it the slowest Drought on any board, so its switch-in ability resolves after the player's Drizzle and the sun is back before anyone moves; Heat Rock holds it for eight turns. That protects the room: with the sun up, a turn-one Weather Ball is a Fire move and every Water attack is halved, so nothing one-shots the setter. Runerigus sets Trick Room: Ghost typing makes Fake Out miss it, Mental Herb cures the first Taunt the moment it lands, and base 145 Defense keeps it standing. Torkoal's own turn is a sun Heat Wave into both slots, Solar Beam into the Water body that came to beat Fire, or Earth Power into the Fire and Electric types, and under the room it moves first. Alternate deployment: Mega Camerupt is the payload. Quiet, zero Speed, Eruption at full HP is 150 base power into both slots from a Mega Special Attack stat, multiplied by the sun and arriving first under the room; Heat Wave is the fallback once it is chipped. Reserves & disruption: Stakataka is the second setter in reserve, and its Focus Sash guarantees it one action - the room again if Runerigus falls, or Wide Guard against the spread answer. If the player re-sets rain by switching their Drizzle user back in, Torkoal re-takes the sun on its own next entry, and the pair AI already pivots a Drought reserve in whenever the sun is gone. Torterra's Curse lowers its own Speed, so under the room it climbs to +2/+2 while getting faster, and Wood Hammer punishes the Water bodies. Copperajah is the Steel anchor whose Weakness Policy pays out on the Ground or Fighting hit it takes. Counterplay & audit: switching the Drizzle user out and back in wins the weather again until Torkoal re-enters, and Cloud Nine, Air Lock or Utility Umbrella blunt the sun; the sun boosts the player's Fire too, and Torterra and Copperajah are Fire-weak; a second Taunt after the Herb, Imprison or the player's own Trick Room ends the room; spread damage breaks Stakataka's Sash; Protect through five turns; Haze erases Curse; Ice into Torterra; Ground and Fighting into Stakataka, Torkoal and Camerupt. Could this team be cooler? The concept was right and the weather was borrowed. On the replaceable-weather build a rain lead overwrote the opening sun, rain Weather Ball one-shot Runerigus on turn one, Prankster Taunt stopped Stakataka, the room never went up, and five of six members were Water-weak with nothing able to bring the sun back. Rhyperior contributed least - 4x Water-weak, and its Lightning Rod covered a lane rain teams rarely use - so Torkoal takes its slot. It is cooler now because each Maxie fight gets its sun differently: Ninetales' fast Drought and Tailwind at Mt. Chimney, the slowest Drought on the field inside the room here, and Courtney's Mega Charizard Y at Mossdeep. Torkoal is also Courtney's hideout lead one room earlier; hers wins the race to power Eruption and Yawn beside a Storm Drain trap, Maxie's wins it to protect a Trick Room setter.
crack: Switch your Drizzle user out and back in to take the weather back until Torkoal re-enters; Cloud Nine, Air Lock or Utility Umbrella blunt the sun; the sun boosts your Fire too, and Torterra and Copperajah are Fire-weak; a second Taunt after Mental Herb, Imprison or your own Trick Room breaks the room; spread damage through Stakataka's Sash; Protect and pivot through five turns; Haze undoes Curse; Ice into Torterra; Ground and Fighting into Stakataka, Torkoal and Camerupt; chip Camerupt so Eruption weakens.

## E0342 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_3 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The hull breakers, and the hideout's setup room. First two useful turns: Barbaracle takes Shell Smash behind Toxapex's Wide Guard or Seismitoad's immediate damage, and Tough Claws then makes a +2 Razor Shell and Stone Edge lethal in one turn. Rotom-Heat is the room's boiler, and it is there for the Grass blade that cuts three Water shells four times over: Fire/Electric takes a quarter from Grass and Steel, Levitate ignores Ground, and Overheat removes a Grass/Steel attacker in one hit. Its second job is the Smash turn itself - Will-O-Wisp halves the physical attacker that would otherwise remove Barbaracle before it moves, and Lum Berry means a sleep move cannot switch the boiler off first. Alternate deployment: if the Smash is threatened, the room reverts to Seismitoad's Water Absorb attrition - Water/Ground also takes nothing from the Electric attacker that the Water room invites - with Toxapex's Regenerator stall and Rotom-Heat burning whatever hits physically. Reserves & disruption: Toxapex's Baneful Bunker-free set is deliberate - it protects with Wide Guard for the side rather than itself, which is the harder and more useful action here; Rotom-Heat's Thunderbolt is the answer to opposing Water and Flying bodies. Counterplay & audit: Haze, Clear Smog, Fake Out, Taunt and priority all beat a one-turn setup plan; Water and Rock remove Rotom-Heat, after which Grass takes Seismitoad and Barbaracle four times over; Electric still breaks Barbaracle and Toxapex. Could this team be cooler? The old block put Scrafty - a Magma-side street thug, now correctly housed in the Magma Hideout - next to two shell Pokemon, so the room's idea was half-formed. The rebuild fixed the theme but left three of four members four times weak to Grass, and one Grass/Steel blade walked through the room without taking a hit; Drednaw, the second Smash body, gave its slot to Rotom-Heat. It is cooler now because the setup room finally guards its own setup: one Shell Smash sweeper, a Wide Guard against the spread answer, a burn against the physical answer, and a Fire hit waiting for the Grass answer. The room still asks a clean question - can you remove Barbaracle in the one turn before it doubles its Speed?
crack: Haze or Clear Smog, Fake Out, Taunt, or a spread move on the Smash turn; Water or Rock on Rotom-Heat, then Grass on Seismitoad and Barbaracle; Electric on Barbaracle and Toxapex; Wide Guard only covers spread moves, so single-target damage still lands.

## E0343 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_5 class=grunt
strategy: RAIN,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The rain callers, and the first automatic weather in the hideout. First two useful turns: Politoed's Drizzle is free and Damp Rock holds it eight turns, so Politoed's own action is Helping Hand into Barraskewda's Swift Swim Liquidation or Icy Wind to bank a speed advantage for after the rain. Barraskewda at doubled Speed outruns essentially the entire player roster at this stage and its rain-boosted Water attacks are the room's damage. Alternate deployment: Kilowattrel's Wind Power charges from Tailwind and its Thunder never misses in rain, so the room can layer speed control on top of weather. Reserves & disruption: Jellicent absorbs the Water attacks the player brings against a rain team and Water Spout under rain is a second spread threat. Counterplay & audit: weather replacement is the single best answer, followed by Electric and Grass pressure and Taunt on the Tailwind. Could this team be cooler? It was already the strongest Aqua block and it survives largely intact - the concrete change is that Politoed is now the hideout's only Drizzle user, because Matt was also carrying one and a faction reads as orchestrated only when the engine has a single owner per room. Asked again after the revision: the room could add a fifth trick, but it should not; this is the plain, correct statement of the rain engine that Matt then escalates, and its value is that the player learns the pattern here and pays for it later.
crack: Replace the weather; Electric and Grass pressure; Taunt Politoed before Helping Hand; Barraskewda is frail and dies to priority or a spread move.

## E0344 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_7 class=grunt
strategy: RAIN,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The ice hold, and a deliberate variation rather than a repeat of the rain room. First two useful turns: nothing here has Drizzle - Lapras sets Rain Dance by hand off a Damp Rock, which costs a real turn, and the room is built so that turn is worth paying for. Beartic's Swift Swim doubles off that manual rain and Cloyster's Skill Link Icicle Spear fires five hits every time, which is the answer to Focus Sash, Sturdy, Disguise and substitutes all at once. Alternate deployment: Cloyster can Shell Smash instead when the board is safe; its White Herb is deliberately absent so the defence drops are real and the player has a window. Reserves & disruption: Dragonite is the crew's answer to the counters a Water and Ice hold invites - sailors say it tows wrecked ships to shore, and here it tows the room through the Electric, Grass and Fighting attackers that break the other three. It resists Grass and Fighting, takes Electric only neutrally, and Multiscale halves the first hit it takes; Dual Wingbeat strikes the Grass and Fighting answers twice for super-effective damage and, like Icicle Spear, does not stop at a Focus Sash; Dragon Claw hits the Dragon-typed Electric legends; Extreme Speed finishes what the rain sweep left standing. Beartic's Earthquake is the Electric answer for the Dragonite pairing - Dragonite flies over it - so a rain-fast Beartic hits the Electric attacker before it moves. Lapras' Freeze-Dry hits the Water answers the player brings for 2x. Counterplay & audit: Taunt or knock out Lapras before the manual rain; Rock hits all four, though Lapras and Beartic answer Rock types with Water; Ice four-times Dragonite once Multiscale is broken; Steel walls both multi-hit lanes. Could this team be cooler? The old block had a second Barraskewda - the exact species the previous room already used - plus Thievul, a Galarian fox with no maritime identity, and the first rebuild's Kingler left three of four members open to the same Electric, Grass or Fighting hit. It is cooler now because it answers "how do you do rain twice without repeating yourself": this room has to earn its weather, which makes Lapras a real target rather than scenery, it pairs the weather with multi-hit and Freeze-Dry lanes that the automatic rain room does not have, and the sea dragon that hauls the wrecks is the one body the usual Water and Ice answers cannot cleanly break. Killing Lapras on turn one is still the correct play and the room knows it.
crack: Remove or Taunt Lapras before Rain Dance and the room loses its engine; Rock pressure on all four, Ice into Dragonite once Multiscale is gone; Electric and Grass still break Lapras and Cloyster, and Fighting breaks Beartic, if Dragonite is dealt with first; Cloyster's Smash turn leaves it fragile.

## E0345 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_4 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP,ALLY_COMBO
mega_slots: 4
plan: The quartermaster's cabin, and the only grunt in either hideout who has been trusted with a Mega Stone. First two useful turns: Clawitzer's Mega Launcher doubles every pulse move it owns, and the room's signature is that this includes Heal Pulse - a full half of an ally's maximum HP restored in one action, from a grunt. The crew's plan is therefore to keep Mega Eelektross alive through damage the player expected to be lethal. Crobat Taunts the setup or support the player needs, or pivots with U-turn. Alternate deployment: Mega Eelektross has no weaknesses while Levitate holds and Wild Charge plus Crunch covers the field; it is the ace and the Mega slot. Reserves & disruption: Alolan Muk's Poison/Dark typing is immune to Psychic and its Knock Off strips the item the player was relying on. Counterplay & audit: Taunt or Encore the healer, burst damage that outpaces a 50 percent heal, Ground attacks the moment Levitate is off, and Fairy pressure on the Dark bodies. Could this team be cooler? The old block had the same four members but Clawitzer was a plain three-pulse attacker, which wasted the one ability in the game that turns a support move into a game-changer. It is cooler now because a grunt with a Mega and a doubled Heal Pulse is a genuine difficulty spike that the player will remember and complain about in the right way - and because the counterplay is clean and teachable: Taunt the shrimp, not the eel.
crack: Taunt, Encore or Knock Off the Clawitzer; burst the Eelektross past the heal rather than chipping it; Fairy and Ground pressure; Crobat folds to Rock, Electric and Psychic.
