## E0346 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_6 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The pressure crew, guarding the pump room, and the most spiteful four Pokemon in the hideout. First two useful turns: Toxicroak's Fake Out buys the turn, and Pyukumuku is the reason the room is dangerous - Innards Out means that whoever lands the knockout blow immediately takes damage equal to Pyukumuku's remaining HP, so the player must choose between leaving a Recover-spamming wall alive and killing it into a full-HP retaliation. Wishiwashi opens in Solo Form and flips to School Form above a quarter HP, so it looks like free damage and is not. Alternate deployment: Overqwil's Intimidate plus Swords Dance is the physical lane once the stall bodies have soaked two turns. Reserves & disruption: Toxicroak's Dry Skin heals it on the Water attacks the player brought for this whole hideout. Counterplay & audit: chip Pyukumuku with a Pokemon you are willing to lose, or ignore it; Psychic and Ground pressure on the Poison pair; Electric and Grass on Wishiwashi. Could this team be cooler? The old block repeated Toxapex from the hull-breaker room and finished with Falinks, a Roman phalanx of bugs that has no place on a pirate crew. It is cooler now because it commits fully to being the attrition room - Innards Out, Schooling and Dry Skin are three separate ways of making the player's damage not count - and because Innards Out is the single most memorable "wait, what just happened" interaction available at this stage and no other trainer in the campaign has it.
crack: Knock out Pyukumuku with an expendable body or leave it entirely; Psychic, Ground and Electric pressure; Wishiwashi is trivial once below a quarter HP, so commit fully rather than chipping.

## E0347 TRAINER_GRUNT_AQUA_HIDEOUT_8 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP

plan: The boatswain outside Matt's door - the hardest Aqua grunt and the last gate before the admin. Reserves & disruption: Walrein's Thick Fat halves the Ice and Fire attacks aimed at it and Ice Body plus Protect stalls if the room needs time. The old block had Vileplume and Mismagius - a flower and a ghost witch standing in a pirate hideout for no stated reason. It is cooler now because it is unmistakably a ship's crew at its most dangerous: a sea serpent, a living fossil that headbutts through walls, an armoured isopod with priority and a walrus that refuses to die. Relicanth's recoil-free Head Smash is the memorable piece - the player expects a harmless fossil and takes 150 base power.

## E0348 TRAINER_MATT class=admin
strategy: RAIN,SETUP
mega_slots: 5
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Matt at the hideout is the same man the player fought on Mt Pyre, escalated rather than replaced - Pelipper is his bird both times, and the difference is what stands behind it. First two useful turns: Pelipper's Drizzle is free on entry and Wide Guard covers the crew's first spread turn while Kingdra, at doubled Swift Swim Speed and holding Choice Specs, fires a rain-boosted Hydro Pump before the player's lead moves. Alternate deployment: Grimmsnarl's Prankster gives priority Reflect and Light Screen, so the admin's screens land before the player's first attack and every subsequent exchange runs at half damage - this, not raw power, is what makes Matt harder than his grunts. Reserves & disruption: Archaludon's Electro Shot charges instantly in rain and fires a 130 base power Electric attack while gaining +1 Special Attack every time; it is the answer to the Water-resist walls the player brings. Basculegion's Last Respects grows 50 base power for every fainted teammate, so the longer the fight runs the more the admin's final slot hurts. Mega Malamar with Contrary turns Superpower's drops into +1/+1 every hit. Counterplay & audit: Brick Break, Defog or simply outlasting the screens; weather replacement; Taunt on Grimmsnarl before the screens resolve. Could this team be cooler? The previous build's Drizzle came from a Politoed that the grunt two rooms earlier already owned, which made the admin look like a bigger grunt instead of the same recurring villain. It is cooler now with Pelipper carried over from Mt Pyre: the player recognises the opening, expects the Mt Pyre plan, and gets screens, an instant Electro Shot and a Last Respects clock instead. Asked again after the revision - the team is a genuine boss because it wins by compounding advantages rather than by one big attack, and each of the four engines has a separate, honest answer.
crack: Taunt Grimmsnarl or break the screens; replace the rain to halve Kingdra and slow Electro Shot; kill Basculegion early while Last Respects is still weak; Fairy and Bug pressure on Malamar, Electric and Rock on Pelipper.

## E0354 TRAINER_LILA_AND_ROY_1 class=ace
strategy: SETUP,REDIRECTION
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Rillaboom's Grassy Surge covers the field in Grassy Terrain the moment it enters, which heals every grounded Pokemon each turn, boosts Grass moves by 30 percent and halves Earthquake - and, critically, it immediately consumes Hawlucha's Grassy Seed. That gives Hawlucha +1 Defense and triggers Unburden, doubling its Speed for the rest of the battle; a Swords Dance behind that is the pair's win condition. Rillaboom's own opening is Fake Out or a terrain-boosted Grassy Glide, which is priority while the terrain holds. Alternate deployment: if Hawlucha is threatened, Amoonguss uses Rage Powder to pull the attack away, and its Regenerator heals it back on the rotation. Reserves & disruption: Tsareena's Queenly Majesty blocks every priority move aimed at this side, which is the standard answer to a doubled-Speed sweeper, and Trop Kick lowers the target's Attack on every hit. Counterplay & audit: Flying types, Levitate and Safety Goggles all ignore Rage Powder; replacing the terrain stops the healing and Grassy Glide's priority; Fire, Ice and Flying pressure answers all four. Could this team be cooler? The old build was a Tatsugiri and Dondozo Commander pair - a lovely engine, but the Mossdeep gym's Sylvia showcases exactly that combination two chapters later, so the route couple were spoiling the gym's best room. It is cooler now because Grassy Seed Unburden Hawlucha is a famous, spectacular doubles engine that appears nowhere else in the campaign, because the four members form one chain rather than two pairs, and because Queenly Majesty and Rage Powder give the team two different, honest ways of protecting the sweeper.
crack: Replace the terrain; Safety Goggles or a Flying type ignores Rage Powder; Fire, Ice and Flying pressure; Hawlucha is frail, so remove it before the Seed resolves or accept a doubled-Speed attacker.

## E0364 TRAINER_AURON class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: First two useful turns: Auron is an Eeveelution specialist and the team is built so each member is immune to something the others fear. Jolteon's Volt Absorb heals on Electric attacks, Vaporeon's Water Absorb heals on Water attacks, Flareon's Flash Fire is immune to Fire and gains a 50 percent Fire boost from it, and Umbreon is immune to Psychic - so three of the four most common coverage types on a player's team are liabilities here. Choice Scarf Jolteon attacks or Volt Switches; Vaporeon's Helping Hand fires the turn it would otherwise be chipping. Alternate deployment: Umbreon is the engine. Foul Play uses the target's own Attack stat, so a Swords Dance or Dragon Dance sweeper hits itself harder than Umbreon ever could, and Wish plus Protect heals the partner for half of Umbreon's own large HP. Reserves & disruption: Flareon's Guts-free Flash Fire boost makes Flare Blitz the physical finish. Counterplay & audit: Fighting, Ground and Fairy attacks are the answers none of the four absorb; special attackers beat Foul Play entirely. Could this team be cooler? The old build was Lucario, Vaporeon, Corsola and Jolteon - two Eeveelutions and two unrelated bodies, with no reason for any of them to stand together. It is cooler now because the trainer has a concept the player reads instantly from the first two sprites, and because the concept is competitively real rather than cosmetic: a team of four absorption abilities means the player's usual coverage is actively wrong and they have to find the one type nobody here resists.
crack: Fighting, Ground and Fairy attacks; special attackers ignore Foul Play; Taunt denies Wish and Helping Hand; each member is individually frail once you stop feeding its ability.

## E0367 TRAINER_NIKKI class=regular
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Lapras attacks or uses Icy Wind for speed control while Corsola uses Life Dew when both Pokemon on Nikki's side are missing HP, and Recover or Scald otherwise - a Life Dew with nobody hurt is a wasted turn and the AI should treat it that way. Alternate deployment: Eviolite Croconaw is the interesting slot. Sheer Force drops the added effects of Liquidation and Crunch for 30 percent more power, Eviolite raises both defences by half, and Dragon Dance gives an unevolved Pokemon one boost that turns it into a real sweeper. Reserves & disruption: Palossand is the mermaid's sandcastle and the answer to the Electric attacker every player sends at a Water team. Ghost/Ground takes nothing from Electric moves, and Earth Power is a single-target super-effective hit on that attacker that never touches Nikki's own side. Sludge Bomb covers the other two answers - the Grass attacker that hits Corsola four times over and the Fairy half of an Electric/Fairy guardian - and Rindo Berry halves the first Grass hit the sandcastle takes. Shore Up and Corsola's Life Dew keep it standing; Corsola's Regenerator heals it every time it switches out, and Mental Herb means one Taunt cannot stop its healing. Counterplay & audit: Grass is the clean answer - four times on Corsola, super-effective on Croconaw and Palossand; Electric still breaks Lapras, Corsola and Croconaw once Palossand is removed, and Dark, Ghost, Ice and special Water all hit the sandcastle; Taunt denies Life Dew, Recover and Shore Up once the Mental Herb is spent. Could this team be cooler? A Swimmer with Audino and Zweilous had two land Pokemon doing a sea job. The same small engine now swims: Corsola is the nurse Audino was - Regenerator carried over, Life Dew healing both Pokemon at once where Wish healed one a turn late - and Croconaw is the half-grown Eviolite attacker Zweilous was, with Dragon Dance keeping the dragon in the joke. The rebuild's fourth slot was Swanna, four times weak to Electric on a team where every member was Electric-weak, so a single Thunderbolt folded the whole party; a girl who says she is a mermaid now keeps a sandcastle on the Route 126 shallows, and that castle is the one thing on the beach an Electric attacker cannot touch. It still gives an ordinary fight one decision the player has to get right: spend two attacks on Croconaw before it dances, or let Life Dew undo the first one.
crack: Grass pressure on Corsola, Croconaw and Palossand, with Grass hitting Corsola four times over; remove Palossand with Dark, Ghost, Ice or special Water first, then Electric sweeps the other three. Kill Croconaw before Dragon Dance - it is an unevolved Pokemon without its Eviolite. Taunt Corsola after the Mental Herb is used and the healing stops.

## E0368 TRAINER_BRENDA class=regular
strategy: SNOW,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Glaceon uses Snowscape only when the snow actually changes the race - it doubles Glaceon's own Speed through Slush Rush, raises the Defense of Ice types, and makes Blizzard never miss, so all three payoffs arrive at once and the turn is worth spending. Octillery attacks immediately. Alternate deployment: if the snow is answered, Seaking's Water Veil makes it immune to the burn a physical attacker fears and Drill Run covers the Rock and Electric bodies that beat the Ice lane. Reserves & disruption: Alomomola's Regenerator and Wacan Berry let it absorb one Electric attack and heal it back on rotation, and Safeguard blocks the status a stall fight would otherwise lose to. Counterplay & audit: Fire, Rock, Steel and Fighting pressure; replacing the weather removes Slush Rush and Blizzard's accuracy in one action; Taunt denies Safeguard and Helping Hand. Could this team be cooler? This is an ordinary route trainer and the roster stays. The concrete judgement: Brenda is the campaign's small, honest statement of manual snow - one setter whose own ability profits from the turn it spends, which is a tidier lesson than the automatic Snow Warning the player meets at Gabrielle, and the two encounters read as a matched pair. The one change worth naming is that Blizzard's perfect accuracy in snow is now stated in the plan, because it is the actual reason the Snowscape turn is not wasted.
crack: Change the weather; Fire, Rock, Steel and Fighting pressure; Taunt the support moves; Glaceon is frail and dies to any priority once the snow is gone.

## E0377 TRAINER_ROGER class=regular
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 2
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Roger fields nothing but crustaceans and the team is built around Adaptability and pincers. Crawdaunt's Adaptability makes Crabhammer and Knock Off hit at 2x STAB rather than 1.5x, so a Swords Dance behind a safe board is lethal; Mega Kingler is the ace and Hyper Cutter means its Attack cannot be lowered by Intimidate. Alternate deployment: Crabominable's Iron Fist boosts Ice Hammer, Drain Punch and Mach Punch - three of its four moves - and Mach Punch is the priority that closes a chipped board. Reserves & disruption: Choice Scarf Dracovish is the surprise; Fishious Rend doubles to 170 base power when Dracovish moves first, which a Scarf guarantees against most of the field, and Strong Jaw boosts it further. Counterplay & audit: Electric and Grass pressure answers every member, Fairy answers the Dark lane, and forcing Dracovish to move second removes most of its damage. Could this team be cooler? Asked honestly, no - a trainer who owns only crabs is already one of the campaign's most memorable ordinary encounters, and the audit keeps it intact. The concrete judgement: the theme is doing real competitive work rather than being a costume, because three of the four share a physical Water lane that a single resist wall cannot hold, and Dracovish punishes the Choice Scarf the player brings to outrun them. The one thing to preserve deliberately is that Mega Kingler, not Crawdaunt, is the ace - the joke lands harder when the biggest crab wins.
crack: Electric and Grass pressure; Fairy on the Dark lane; slow Dracovish down with Tailwind of your own or a Scarf and Fishious Rend halves; Intimidate is useless against Hyper Cutter.

## E0379 TRAINER_ATHENA class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 4
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: First two useful turns: Athena's team is pure speed and the fight is decided in the first two exchanges. Mega Kangaskhan opens with Fake Out, which Parental Bond makes hit twice - the second hit does not flinch again, but it is free chip damage - while Pheromosa, at 151 base Speed, attacks immediately and Beast Boost raises its highest stat after every knockout. Infernape's Mach Punch is the priority lane. Alternate deployment: Accelgor is the team's honest trick. Final Gambit deals damage equal to Accelgor's current HP and faints it, so at full HP it is a guaranteed removal of almost anything on the player's side; the Bug Gem plus Unburden set means it can instead double its Speed after a single Bug attack. The AI must only spend Final Gambit when Accelgor's actual current HP makes the trade worth it. Counterplay & audit: every member here is frail - Pheromosa has 37 base defences - so any priority move, any spread move and Fake Out of your own all invert the fight; Rock, Flying and Psychic pressure answers the Bug and Fighting lanes. Could this team be cooler? Asked honestly, no, and the audit keeps it. The concrete judgement: it is the campaign's glass-cannon fight, it owns that identity completely, and Final Gambit gives an ordinary ace trainer one genuinely startling moment that costs the player their best Pokemon if they leave it at full HP. The counterplay is clean - hit first, hit both slots, or hit with priority - and the fight punishes exactly the players who have been winning by out-bulking things.
crack: Priority and spread moves beat the whole team; Fake Out denies the opening; chip Accelgor to make Final Gambit worthless; Rock, Flying and Psychic pressure.

## E0382 TRAINER_KATELYN_1 class=regular
strategy: REDIRECTION,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: First two useful turns: Butterfree is still the whole encounter. Compound Eyes raises Sleep Powder's accuracy to a practical certainty, and Butterfree also carries Rage Powder and Tailwind - so on turn one it either removes a player Pokemon from the game or hands its side a speed window, and on turn two it does the other. Qwilfish and Lanturn attack into whichever window it created. Alternate deployment: Qwilfish's Intimidate cuts the physical answer on entry and Flip Turn pivots it back out; Lanturn's Volt Absorb turns the Electric attack a Water team invites into healing, and its Ice Beam answers the Grass switch-in. Reserves & disruption: Jellicent with Cursed Body is the stall body - Strength Sap drains the target's Attack and heals Jellicent by that amount, Will-O-Wisp halves the next physical attacker, and Cursed Body can disable the player's best move outright. Counterplay & audit: Safety Goggles, a Grass typing or Insomnia beats both powder moves at once, spread moves ignore Rage Powder, and Grass pressure splits the three swimmers. Could this team be cooler? A Swimming Triathlete had Fearow, Zebstrika, Butterfree and Galarian Corsola - no Water at all. Three slots now swim and keep their jobs: Qwilfish is the Intimidate attacker Fearow was, Lanturn is the special Electric attacker Zebstrika was, and Jellicent keeps Corsola's Cursed Body, Strength Sap and Will-O-Wisp stall set. Butterfree is the one non-Water member and it earns the slot: its Rage Powder pulls the single-target Grass attacks meant for the swimmers, and Bug/Flying takes a quarter from them. Compound Eyes Sleep Powder beside redirection still forces the real decision - answer the sleep or answer the redirection - and Butterfree is frail enough that killing it solves both.
crack: Safety Goggles, Grass typing, Insomnia or Vital Spirit beats the powders; spread moves ignore Rage Powder; Butterfree dies to almost anything that connects, and Rock or Electric attacks do it double. Grass attacks hit Qwilfish and Jellicent super-effectively once Butterfree is gone, a Ground type ignores Lanturn's Thunderbolt, and special attackers ignore Strength Sap.

## E0383 TRAINER_ALEXA class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: First two useful turns: Feraligatr's Sheer Force removes the secondary effects of Liquidation and Ice Punch and raises their power by 30 percent in exchange, and Dragon Dance behind a safe board makes it the fast physical threat; Alolan Marowak holds a Thick Club, which doubles its Attack outright and makes Shadow Bone hit like a legendary's attack. Alternate deployment: Mega Mawile is the ace, and its stone is the most extreme item-into-stat trade on the team - Mega Evolution hands it Huge Power, doubling an Attack stat that was already raised, so Play Rough and Iron Head from a slow, bulky Steel/Fairy body end most targets, and Sucker Punch is its answer to the faster attacker that tries to outrun it. Reserves & disruption: Technician Scizor's Bullet Punch is boosted by 50 percent and is the priority finish; Harvest Exeggutor recycles its Sitrus Berry and Hypnosis is the disruption; Ampharos' Electroweb is the speed control that lets the slower physical members move first. Counterplay & audit: Knock Off is the single best move against this team - it removes the Thick Club and the Sitrus Berry in one action, though it cannot take a Mega Stone - and Fire, Fighting, Ground and Steel pressure answers the rest. Alexa is the campaign's "item matters" trainer: Thick Club, Sitrus plus Harvest, a Mega Stone and a Life Orb are four different ways of turning an item into a stat, and a player who carries Knock Off is rewarded enormously.
crack: Knock Off strips the Thick Club and the Berry; Fire and Ground attacks answer Mega Mawile and Scizor, and Intimidate or Will-O-Wisp halves the Huge Power damage; Fighting and Ground pressure on the rest; Taunt denies Hypnosis and Dragon Dance.

## E0384 TRAINER_RUBEN class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 4
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: First two useful turns: Ruben is the campaign's healer, and Meganium is the reason - Triage gives every healing move on it +3 priority, so Heal Pulse restores half of an ally's maximum HP before anything else on the field acts. The opening is therefore Heatmor or Dragonite attacking while Meganium holds the board together from behind a Light Screen. Alternate deployment: Mega Lopunny is the ace and Scrappy lets Fake Out and Close Combat hit Ghost types, which are the usual answer to a Normal-Fighting attacker; Fake Out also buys Dragonite the turn its Dragon Dance needs, and Multiscale means Dragonite survives the first hit at full HP regardless. Reserves & disruption: Milotic's Competitive punishes the Intimidate and Icy Wind the player brings against a physical team with +2 Special Attack; Florges' Flower Veil makes every Grass-typed ally - Meganium - immune to stat drops and status, which protects the healer specifically. Counterplay & audit: Taunt is the key, because it shuts off Heal Pulse, Light Screen and Wish at once; burst damage that outpaces a priority half-heal, Steel and Poison pressure on the Fairy lane, and Ice and Fairy on Dragonite. Could this team be cooler? The old build's ace was Mega Latias, which the audit has now given to Kathleen as the Mossdeep gym's examiner, and the team was one slot short of stating its own idea. It is cooler now because every remaining member exists to keep somebody else alive or to punish the player for interfering: priority healing, a Fairy that blocks stat drops on the healer, a Competitive wall, Multiscale, and a Mega whose Fake Out buys the healer its turn. Fighting a team you cannot chip down is a genuinely different problem from anything else on these routes.
crack: Taunt the Meganium and the whole structure stops; burst damage rather than chip; Fire, Ice, Bug and Flying pressure; Steel and Poison answer Florges.

## E0388 TRAINER_PRESTON class=gym
strategy: TAILWIND,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Conservative
plan: Room one of the Mossdeep observatory: reflection and speed. First two useful turns: Xatu's Magic Bounce returns Taunt, Thunder Wave, Will-O-Wisp, Leech Seed, Spore and every entry hazard straight back at the sender, so Preston's opening is simply to stand there and set Tailwind while the player's support move hits their own side. Espeon is the second Magic Bounce body, so the mirror covers both slots at once. Alternate deployment: Sigilyph holds a Flame Orb and Magic Guard means the burn deals it no damage at all; Psycho Shift then hands that burn to a physical attacker, which is the room's way of crippling a sweeper without using a status move the mirror would have to bounce. Reserves & disruption: Harvest Exeggutor recycles a Sitrus Berry, Grumpig's Thick Fat blunts the Fire and Ice answers, and Alakazam's Magic Guard makes Life Orb, hazards, weather and poison deal it nothing at all until it Mega Evolves into Trace. Counterplay & audit: attack, do not status - every support move the player owns is a liability in this room; Dark types ignore nothing here, but U-turn, Knock Off and raw damage all work. Could this team be cooler? The old room was six Psychics whose first move was the word PSYCHIC, led by a Xatu whose Magic Bounce was never mentioned in the plan. It is cooler now because the room has a rule the player discovers by breaking it: the first status move they use comes back. Simple's Swoobat and Magic Guard's Sigilyph state the gym's thesis early - in this building, abilities matter more than stats.
crack: Do not use status moves; raw damage, U-turn and Knock Off; Dark, Bug and Ghost pressure on the Psychic bodies; Taunt Xatu before Tailwind only if you can stomach it bouncing.

## E0389 TRAINER_VIRGIL class=gym

## E0390 TRAINER_BLAKE class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room three: screens, and the gym's positioning room. First two useful turns: the male Meowstic has Prankster, so Reflect and Light Screen go up with priority before the player's first attack lands - the female Meowstic has Competitive instead, so any attempt to lower her stats hands her +2 Special Attack. Blake therefore opens with a screen and dares the player to Icy Wind or Intimidate the pair. Alternate deployment: Farigiraf's Armor Tail blocks every priority move aimed at Blake's side while it is on the field, so Fake Out, Sucker Punch, Extreme Speed, Aqua Jet and priority Taunt simply fail - behind screens, that removes the two fastest ways to break a defensive setup at once. The old room had Klefki and Metang bolted onto two screen setters, with no reason for the six to be together. It is cooler now because it is the gym's positioning puzzle rather than its bulk puzzle: priority is switched off by Armor Tail, targeting is unreliable because of two Ally Switch users, and damage is halved by screens. The player has to give up precision and win on raw output.

strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Room four: the blade room, and the gym's answer to the idea that Psychic types are frail special attackers. First two useful turns: Veluza's Sharpness multiplies every slicing move by 1.5, so Choice Band Aqua Cutter and Psycho Cut land like a pseudo-legendary's attacks; Bruxish's Strong Jaw does the same for its biting moves and Psychic Fangs additionally breaks Reflect and Light Screen, which is exactly the answer to Blake's room one door earlier. Alternate deployment: Malamar's Contrary turns Superpower's Attack and Defense drops into +1 and +1, so every swing makes it stronger; it is the room's win condition if left alone for three turns. Reserves & disruption: Espathra's Speed Boost climbs while Protect banks turns, and Rabsca's Revival Blessing can bring back a fallen partner at half HP, which resets an exchange the player thought they had won. Mega Gallade has Sharpness too, so the room ends with the same mechanic doubled. Counterplay & audit: Haze and Clear Smog undo Contrary, Fairy and Bug pressure answer the Dark and Psychic bodies, and killing Rabsca before it revives is a real priority. Could this team be cooler? The old room was the right six species with generic sets and no stated connection. It is cooler now because Sharpness and Strong Jaw are named as the room's shared idea - this is the physical-Psychic room - and because Revival Blessing gives the player a genuinely novel problem: a knockout that does not stay bought.
crack: Haze the Contrary boosts; remove Rabsca before it revives anything; Fairy, Bug and Ghost pressure; Malamar and Veluza are both slow without support.

## E0392 TRAINER_SAMANTHA class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Room five: the trap, where the player stops being allowed to leave. First two useful turns: Wobbuffet's Shadow Tag prevents every non-Ghost opponent from switching out, and it has no attacking move at all - Counter returns double a physical hit, Mirror Coat returns double a special one, and Encore locks the player into whatever they just did. The correct read against Wobbuffet is to do nothing large, which is exactly what Samantha's partners need. Alternate deployment: with the player pinned, Reuniclus takes Calm Mind behind Magic Guard, which means Life Orb recoil, poison, burn, sandstorm and hazards all deal it zero damage, and Swoobat's Simple doubles every stat change so one Calm Mind is +2/+2. Reserves & disruption: Grumpig is the clock - its Toxic badly poisons a trapped target that cannot switch the poison off, which beats the stall the player would otherwise use to outlast the trap, and Thick Fat halves the Fire and Ice attacks aimed at it while it takes Nasty Plot. The aces are the immediate special pressure: Choice Specs Indeedee-M, and Oricorio in its Pa'u style, whose Revelation Dance is a Psychic attack and whose Quiver Dance raises Special Attack, Special Defense and Speed at once behind a Focus Sash. Toxic fails into Steel and Poison types, so Grumpig attacks those instead.
crack: Bring a Ghost type and switching still works; do not throw a big attack into Wobbuffet; Taunt the setup turns and the Toxic; Dark and Bug pressure on the Psychic bodies, and Rock, Electric and Ice on Oricorio and Swoobat; spread damage breaks Oricorio's Sash.

## E0393 TRAINER_MAURA class=gym
strategy: PERISH_TRAP,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Room six: the Slowpoke shrine, and the gym's perish trap. First two useful turns: Gothitelle's Shadow Tag locks the player in and Jynx sings Perish Song - three turns later everything on the field that was present when it was sung faints, and the player cannot switch out of it. Maura's own side can switch freely, which is the entire point. Alternate deployment: the four Slowpoke-line members are the clock. Slowking, Galarian Slowking and Galarian Slowbro all carry Regenerator or Assault Vest bulk, so they survive the three turns without spending actions on recovery. Mega Slowbro is the ace. Reserves & disruption: Galarian Slowking's Curious Medicine resets the player's stat boosts when it enters, which undoes a setup sweeper for free, and its Assault Vest set carries Focus Blast for the Dark type that walls every Psychic move in the shrine. Galarian Slowbro drops its redundant Psychic for Shadow Ball beside Shell Side Arm, so the Ghost type that walks through Shadow Tag to break the trap takes a super-effective hit on the way in. Counterplay & audit: a Ghost type still ignores Shadow Tag, Taunt or Soundproof denies Perish Song, and killing Gothitelle within three turns escapes it. Could this team be cooler? The old room was Jynx, Gothitelle, Amoonguss and three unrelated Water Psychics, so the trap and the bodies had nothing to do with each other. It is cooler now because the room is a shrine to one family: four Slowpoke-line Pokemon rotating on Regenerator behind a trap, which is both a genuinely strong competitive structure and the single most visually coherent room in the gym. The two Galarian members now also guard the shrine's doors - Shadow Ball for the Ghost, Focus Blast for the Dark - so the counters the player plans around the trap cost something.
crack: A Ghost type ignores Shadow Tag, but keep it away from Galarian Slowbro's Shadow Ball; a Dark type walls the Psychic attacks, but Galarian Slowking's Focus Blast is its one punish, and that misses three times in ten. Taunt or Soundproof denies Perish Song; remove Gothitelle inside three turns; Electric, Grass and Dark pressure on the Slow line.

## E0394 TRAINER_SYLVIA class=gym
strategy: ALLY_COMBO,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
tactic: COMMANDER TATSUGIRI NONE DONDOZO
plan: Room seven: terrain and Commander, the gym's two-body room. First two useful turns: Tatsugiri and Dondozo enter together and Commander fires immediately - Tatsugiri climbs into Dondozo's mouth, becomes untargetable, and Dondozo gains two stages in every stat at once. Only Dondozo acts during the occupied turns, so the room is a single enormous body rather than two, and Order Up gains an extra boost from the Curly form inside it. Alternate deployment: female Indeedee's Psychic Surge covers the floor in Psychic Terrain, which blocks every priority move against grounded targets - the answer a player reaches for against a +2 wall - and turns Expanding Force into a spread attack at 120 base power. Terrain Extender holds it eight turns. Reserves & disruption: Indeedee also has Follow Me, so she can redirect a committed attack away from Dondozo entirely, and After You can hand Dondozo the turn order it lacks. Wyrdeer's Intimidate cuts the physical answers and Musharna's Telepathy makes it immune to spread damage from its own partner. Counterplay & audit: knock out Tatsugiri before they pair, or remove Dondozo with special damage while the boosts are only defensive; Grass and Electric pressure; terrain replacement restores your priority. Could this team be cooler? The old room already had the Commander pair and Indeedee, but Sigilyph and Dusknoir were bolted on with no relation to either engine. It is cooler now because every remaining slot serves the same plan: Follow Me protects the whale, Psychic Terrain switches off the priority that answers it, Intimidate blunts the physical answer and Telepathy makes the partner immune to the collateral. It is the most tightly built room in the gym and the hardest to unpick in one turn.
crack: Remove Tatsugiri on turn one or accept a +2 Dondozo; special attacks bypass its Defense boost; Grass and Electric pressure; replace the terrain to get priority back.

## E0395 TRAINER_NATE class=gym
strategy: ALLY_COMBO,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
tactic: INSTRUCT ORANGURU INSTRUCT DELPHOX
plan: Room eight: Instruct, and the gym's "that attack happened twice" room. First two useful turns: Oranguru uses Instruct on Mega Delphox, which immediately repeats Delphox's last move at full power with no charge, no recoil and no turn spent - two Mystical Fires or two Psychics in one turn, from a Mega special attacker. The AI must only spend Instruct when Delphox has actually already moved this turn and its last move is worth repeating; Instructing a Protect or a failed move is worthless. Alternate deployment: Choice Specs Beheeyem is the other Instruct target, and Analytic adds 30 percent when it moves last, which it usually does - an Instructed Analytic Psychic is the single largest number in the gym outside the leaders. Reserves & disruption: Mimikyu's Disguise guarantees it survives one hit and Swords Dance behind that free turn is a real threat; Orbeetle is the room's shield - Light Screen and Reflect halve the damage the player can do to the Instruct pair for five turns, Mental Herb stops one Taunt from shutting the screens off, and Bug Buzz is the gym's answer to the Dark types that walk through its Psychic attacks; Alolan Exeggutor's Harvest and Dragon lane covers the rest. Counterplay & audit: remove Oranguru first even though Delphox is the scarier body, Taunt denies Instruct, and Dark types resist the Psychic half of the room.
crack: Remove or Taunt Oranguru and the room halves; Dark and Ghost pressure on Delphox and Beheeyem; Fire, Flying and Rock attacks on Orbeetle before the screens go up, or Brick Break and Psychic Fangs through them; break Disguise with a spread move before Mimikyu sets up.

## E0396 TRAINER_MACEY class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room nine: the apprentices. First two useful turns: five of Macey's six are unevolved Pokemon holding Eviolite, which raises their Defense and Special Defense by 50 percent each - Metang, Girafarig, Duosion, Kadabra and Elgyem are all individually far bulkier than the player expects, and the room's whole plan is to be un-knockable for long enough that its graduate can finish the fight. Metang opens with Earthquake; Elgyem's Telepathy makes it immune to that Earthquake, which is the room's one clean doubles interaction. Alternate deployment: Duosion's Magic Guard plus Eviolite means Life Orb, poison, hazards and weather do nothing to it while it Calm Minds; Girafarig's Sap Sipper turns the Grass attacks the player brought for a Psychic gym into +1 Attack. Reserves & disruption: Kadabra's Magic Guard and Encore lock a player who spent a turn setting up. The ace is Armarouge, a Charcadet that grew by putting on a fallen warrior's armour - the apprentice who has already graduated. Choice Scarf lets it outspeed the room's usual answers, Armor Cannon is its largest hit at the cost of its own Defense and Special Defense, and it carries the room's answers to the room's counters: Fire for the Bug and Steel attackers, Aura Sphere for the Dark types, Energy Ball for the Water and Ground attacks aimed at it. Flash Fire absorbs the Fire attacks aimed at it. Counterplay & audit: Knock Off removes Eviolite and the room collapses; spread moves beat the bulk faster than single targets; Dark, Bug and Ghost pressure.
crack: Knock Off or Trick removes Eviolite and halves the room; spread damage; Dark and Ghost pressure; Water, Rock and Ground attacks answer Armarouge, and its Choice lock and self-lowered defenses after Armor Cannon are the window to remove it.

## E0397 TRAINER_CLIFFORD class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Room ten: the machine room - one engine and five Psychic machines it powers. First two useful turns: Pincurchin's Electric Surge is automatic and Terrain Extender holds it eight turns; the terrain boosts grounded Pokemon's Electric moves by 30 percent and stops every grounded Pokemon from falling asleep, and Alolan Raichu's Surge Surfer doubles its Speed in it, so Raichu opens with a boosted Thunderbolt or a Nasty Plot beside Pincurchin's Thunderbolt. Electric Terrain does not block priority; Pincurchin is slow enough to be priority's first target, so it Protects or Recovers rather than trading when focused. Pincurchin is the room's one non-Psychic body and exists only to run the terrain. Alternate deployment: Metagross is the gym's supercomputer - Assault Vest bulk, Meteor Mash and Zen Headbutt, and a terrain-boosted Thunder Punch - and Choice Specs Alakazam is the immediate special nuke, with Energy Ball for the Ground and Water answers to the room and Focus Blast and Shadow Ball for the Dark, Steel and Ghost bodies that resist Psychic. Reserves & disruption: Claydol and Bronzong are the ancient machines; both float on Levitate, so the Earthquake that hits Pincurchin, Raichu and Metagross cannot touch them. Claydol sets Reflect and answers Ground attackers with Ice Beam; Bronzong holds a Weakness Policy, so the Fire, Dark or Ghost hit the player aims at a Steel machine doubles its Attack and Special Attack for Gyro Ball and Zen Headbutt. Floating members do not get the terrain's sleep protection.
crack: Replace the terrain and Raichu's Speed and the Electric boost disappear at once; Ground attacks on Pincurchin, Raichu and Metagross; Dark and Ghost pressure on the five Psychics; do not feed Bronzong's Weakness Policy with a hit that leaves it standing; Alakazam is frail and Choice-locked; Taunt stops Reflect and Nasty Plot.

## E0398 TRAINER_NICHOLAS class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Room eleven: Gravity, and the gym's last idea before the twins - a Psychic room in which every attack that normally misses stops missing. First two useful turns: Sableye has Prankster, so its Gravity goes up with priority on turn one; Bronzor is the backup setter, so removing the Focus Sash imp does not end the room. Sableye is the room's one non-Psychic body and is there to set the field. Under Gravity every move on the field gains roughly 67 percent relative accuracy, every Flying type and every Levitate user is dragged to the ground and becomes hittable by Ground attacks, and Fly, Bounce and Magnet Rise stop working. Alternate deployment: Bronzor's Hypnosis is 60 percent accurate in an ordinary battle and close to certain under its own Gravity, and Galarian Mr. Mime's Vital Spirit makes it immune to that sleep, so the pair can fire it into a crowded board without a partner casualty; Mr. Mime then attacks with Blizzard and Focus Blast behind a Nasty Plot. Reserves & disruption: Galarian Rapidash is the physical lane and its whole Choice Band moveset normally misses - High Horsepower, Play Rough, Megahorn and Zen Headbutt - so under Gravity it is the most reliable attacker in the gym, and High Horsepower hits the grounded Flying and Levitate bodies; Pastel Veil keeps its whole side from being poisoned. Hypno is the special coverage: Focus Blast lands under Gravity, and Focus Blast and Dazzling Gleam are the room's answers to the Dark types that ignore Psychic. The ace is Hisuian Braviary, and it is the exception that proves the room: Gravity drags Nicholas's own bird out of the sky and costs it its Ground immunity, and in exchange its Tinted Lens Hurricane cannot miss, while Esper Wing raises its Speed with every hit. Bronzor runs Heatproof rather than Levitate on purpose - Gravity would switch Levitate off anyway.
crack: Taunt Sableye before the priority Gravity and be ready for Bronzor to re-set it; Gravity grounds your team too, but it grounds Nicholas's ace as well, so Ground coverage is live on Braviary; Rock, Electric, Ice, Dark and Ghost answer Braviary; Fire, Fighting and Ground on Bronzor; Steel and Poison on Rapidash, whose Choice lock is the window; Galarian Mr. Mime and Hypno are frail against Dark and Bug pressure.

## E0399 TRAINER_KATHLEEN class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Room twelve: the examiner. Kathleen tests students, so she fields only species the player has already met in this gym, each built the way it should have been - Espeon, Starmie, Reuniclus, Beheeyem, Medicham, and as her ace the Mega Slowbro from Maura's room, now the patient win condition Maura never made it. First two useful turns: Espeon's Magic Bounce opens as it did in room one, but here it is a Calm Mind sweeper rather than a screener, and Medicham's Pure Power Fake Out and High Jump Kick are the immediate pressure. Alternate deployment: Reuniclus can set Trick Room, which turns Beheeyem, Reuniclus and the zero-Speed Slowbro into the fast ones; without the room, Choice Scarf Analytic Starmie is the late damage and Wise Glasses Beheeyem the immediate damage. Reserves & disruption: Mega Slowbro is the ace - Shell Armor refuses critical hits, its Defense is enormous, and Calm Mind behind Slack Off makes it the most patient body in the gym; Scald burns the physical attacker that tries to break it. Counterplay & audit: Dark and Ghost pressure across the board, Taunt on the Calm Minds and Slack Off, and Grass and Electric answers for Mega Slowbro. Asked again after the revision: it earns its slot, because after eleven rooms of gimmicks the last door before the leaders is the one that just plays well, and now every species in it is one the player was taught to beat.
crack: Dark, Ghost and Bug pressure; Taunt the Calm Mind and Slack Off turns; break Espeon's mirror with damage rather than status; Grass and Electric answer Mega Slowbro before it stacks Calm Minds; let Trick Room expire rather than fighting inside it.

## E0400 TRAINER_TATE_AND_LIZA_1 class=leader
strategy: TRICK_ROOM,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: The twins are one trainer with two minds and the fight is built so that both halves of every pair matter. First two useful turns: Solrock and Lunatone enter together - the sun and the moon, the gym's emblem - and Solrock sets Trick Room with a Mental Herb so Taunt cannot stop the first attempt, while Lunatone spends a Power Herb on an instant Meteor Beam, which is a 120 base power Rock attack plus a free +1 Special Attack with no charging turn. That is the twins' opening statement: the room reverses and the first attack is already at full power. Alternate deployment: Tapu Lele's Psychic Surge is the second half of the engine. Psychic Terrain blocks all priority against grounded targets, which is the only fast answer to a Trick Room team, and it boosts Expanding Force into a 120 base power spread move for Hatterene and Gardevoir. Hatterene, at 29 base Speed with Magic Bounce, is both the fastest thing on the field under Trick Room and immune to every status move aimed at it. Reserves & disruption: Cresselia is the twins' durability - Lunar Blessing-free Moonlight, Helping Hand and a second Trick Room so the room can be re-set when it lapses. Mega Gardevoir with Pixilate turns Hyper Voice into a Fairy-typed spread attack off an enormous Special Attack stat, under terrain, under Trick Room, moving first. Counterplay & audit: this is the hardest board in the region to unpick with one action - Taunt is answered by Mental Herb and Magic Bounce, priority is answered by Psychic Terrain, and speed is answered by Trick Room. The honest answers are Wide Guard against the spread attacks, Steel and Poison typing against the Fairy lane, Dark types that ignore the Psychic half entirely, and surviving the five turns. Could this team be cooler? The six species were already right - Solrock and Lunatone are the twins' identity and Tapu Lele, Hatterene, Cresselia and Mega Gardevoir are the correct escalation. What was missing was the statement that the two field effects are one machine: Trick Room decides the order and Psychic Terrain removes the only tool that beats it. Asked again after the revision: the leaders are a major step beyond their twelve rooms because they run two of the gym's showcased mechanics simultaneously, at the highest levels in the building, and because every individual answer the player learned in a single room is covered by the other half of the pair here.
crack: Wide Guard and Protect against the spread turns; a Dark type ignores the Psychic lane and Psychic Terrain does not stop its priority-free damage; Steel and Poison answer Pixilate; outlast the five Trick Room turns and the twins are the slowest team in the region.

## E0401 TRAINER_GRUNT_SPACE_CENTER_3 class=grunt
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The Space Center raid is Magma away from its volcano, so the whole group is a machines-and-fuel strike team rather than the stone-and-magma work crew of the hideout - no member of this raid appears in the hideout and the identity shifts from Rock/Ground to Steel, Electric and Poison. This is the perimeter watch on the ground floor. First two useful turns: Salandit's Corrosion poisons Steel and Poison types, which is the only way this room threatens the Steel walls a player brings to a facility raid, and Fake Out buys Seviper its Coil turn. Coil raises Attack, Defense and accuracy, which is what makes Seviper's Poison Jab and Crunch reliable rather than a coin flip. Alternate deployment: Bastiodon's Sturdy plus Soundproof-free Wide Guard holds the pair of slots while the setup lands, and Metal Burst returns the player's biggest physical hit at 1.5x. Reserves & disruption: Rapidash is the room's speed and its Flame Body punishes contact. Counterplay & audit: Fighting, Ground and Water pressure; spread moves beat the Metal Burst plan by splitting damage; Steel-typed players must respect Corrosion. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Salazzle, Palossand, Amoonguss and Hypno - two of which belong to Courtney and the Magma Hideout, and Hypno belongs to the Psychic gym two chapters earlier. It is cooler now because the perimeter reads as a guard post at a rocket facility: a poison lizard that can rust the player's Steel answer, a coiling snake, a living shield wall that reflects your own biggest hit, and a horse for speed. Metal Burst is the memorable piece - the room wants the player's strongest attack.
crack: Spread damage rather than one big physical hit; Fighting, Ground and Water pressure; Taunt denies Coil; Bastiodon has no recovery and folds to special Ground and Fighting attacks.

## E0402 TRAINER_GRUNT_SPACE_CENTER_1 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The fuel bay. First two useful turns: Rampardos' Mold Breaker Head Smash is 150 base power off a 165 base Attack stat and Mold Breaker means Sturdy, Solid Rock, Filter and Levitate do not save the target - this is the single hardest-hitting attack any grunt in the campaign owns, and the recoil is real, so the room is a race it wants to win in two turns. Emboar's Reckless boosts Flare Blitz, Double-Edge and Wild Charge, so the pair are a matched set of self-damaging brawlers. Alternate deployment: Arbok's Intimidate cuts the physical answer and Glare paralyses regardless of the target's type, which is how the room survives to swing twice. Reserves & disruption: Honchkrow's Moxie converts any knockout into a snowball and Sucker Punch closes out a chipped board. Counterplay & audit: Rampardos is slow and frail on the special side - Protect and Wide Guard buy the turn its recoil needs, and Fighting, Water, Ground and Fairy all answer the room. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Mightyena, Camerupt, Shiftry and Honchkrow - which is the Mt Chimney Maxie team in miniature, fielded by a grunt three chapters later. It is cooler now because the fuel bay has one legible, frightening idea: two recoil attackers who do not care about their own HP, backed by paralysis so they get the turns they need. Mold Breaker Head Smash ignoring Sturdy also quietly answers every Focus Sash and Sturdy lead the player has been leaning on all raid.
crack: Protect or Wide Guard the Head Smash turn and let the recoil work; Fighting, Water and Ground pressure; Fairy answers Arbok; do not let Honchkrow get a free knockout.

## E0403 TRAINER_GRUNT_SPACE_CENTER_4 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP,REDIRECTION
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The magnet bay, and the strangest room in the raid: three magnetic Pokemon, the moon Clefable the space centre keeps for luck, and Gravity. First two useful turns: Probopass opens with Gravity beside Clefable - Gravity grounds every Flying and Levitating Pokemon on the field and raises the accuracy of every move, which turns Clefable's Blizzard into a sure spread hit - while Clefable's Follow Me draws the single-target Fighting and Ground attacks that would otherwise hit Probopass four times over; once the field is set, Probopass attacks with Earth Power, Power Gem and Thunderbolt off enormous Special Defense. Alolan Golem attacks with Rock Slide and Earthquake, or takes Rock Polish when the board allows, doubling its Speed for the turns that follow. Alternate deployment: Eviolite Magneton's Analytic adds 30 percent when it moves last, which is often. The Ground answer: every magnet is weak to Ground and Gravity grounds the grunt's own side as surely as the player's, so the room does not pretend to float - Clefable is the answer instead. A Gravity-sure Blizzard hits Garchomp, Landorus and the other Earthquake users twice or four times over, Moonblast punishes the Dragon and Fighting counters, Unaware ignores the boosts of whatever tries to sweep through, and Follow Me pulls High Horsepower and Close Combat off the magnets. Reserves & disruption: Galvanize turns Golem's Double-Edge into an Electric attack with a power bonus, and Probopass's Magnet Pull stops the player's Steel types from switching out of a bad matchup. Golem's Earthquake hits its own grounded partners, so it prefers Rock Slide and Galvanize Double-Edge beside Magneton or Clefable.
crack: Spread Ground attacks ignore Follow Me, so an Earthquake user that outspeeds or survives Clefable's Blizzard still breaks the magnets, and Steel or Poison attacks remove Clefable first; Fighting and Ground hit Probopass four times over. Expect to be grounded, so do not rely on Levitate or a Flying typing; Blizzard misses often once the five Gravity turns end, and Taunt or Fake Out on Probopass stops the field before it starts. Golem is slow until Rock Polish, and Water and Grass hit it hard.

## E0404 TRAINER_GRUNT_SPACE_CENTER_2 class=grunt
strategy: TAILWIND,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The hangar, and the raid's support room. First two useful turns: Klefki's Prankster puts Reflect and Light Screen up before the player's first attack resolves, which means the hangar fight runs at half damage from turn one; its alternative priority action is Thunder Wave on whichever target the crew cannot outrun. Aerodactyl then sets Tailwind or attacks - it is the fastest thing in the raid and Unnerve stops the player's Berries from activating at all. Alternate deployment: Skarmory's Sturdy plus screens is close to unkillable for three turns, and Body Press converts its 140 base Defense, doubled again by Iron Defense, into real damage. Reserves & disruption: Obstagoon's Guts turns the status the room's own Thunder Wave-heavy plan invites into a 50 percent Attack boost, and Obstruct cuts the Defense of anything that hits into it. Counterplay & audit: Brick Break and Defog remove the screens, Taunt beats Prankster only if you are faster or Dark-typed, and Fire, Electric and Fighting pressure answer the Steel bodies. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Flygon, Crobat, Drapion and Klefki - three Poison and Flying bodies from the hideout with one screener bolted on. It is cooler now because it is honestly a support room rather than four attackers: screens, Tailwind, paralysis and an Unnerve that quietly switches off the player's Sitrus and Lum Berries for the whole fight. Berry denial at exactly the point in the campaign where the player has learned to rely on Berries is the memorable, slightly cruel idea.
crack: Break the screens with Brick Break or Defog, or simply outlast five turns; a Dark-typed lead ignores Prankster; Fire and Electric pressure on Skarmory and Klefki; Fighting on Obstagoon.

## E0405 TRAINER_GRUNT_SPACE_CENTER_5 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The control room, held by infiltrators. First two useful turns: Zoroark enters disguised as the last member of the party - the player sees Shiftry and targets Shiftry, and the attack lands on a 60/40/40 Dark type instead. The illusion breaks the moment Zoroark is hit, so the crew's plan is to use that one turn of misdirection on Kingambit's Swords Dance. Alternate deployment: Kingambit's Supreme Overlord raises its Attack by 10 percent for every fallen ally, so it is deliberately placed to come in late; Kowtow Cleave never misses and Sucker Punch closes. Reserves & disruption: Cacturne's Water Absorb heals on the Water attacks the player packed for a Fire-and-Rock faction, and Sucker Punch gives the room a second priority lane. Shiftry's Wind Rider makes it immune to the Tailwind and wind moves the hangar crew taught the player to expect, and it trades a redundant fourth Dark move for Air Slash - the crew's one answer to the Fighting type every Dark room invites, and to Bug as well. Counterplay & audit: spread moves ignore the illusion entirely, Fighting and Fire answer Kingambit but Fighting leads must respect Shiftry's Air Slash, Fairy is the cleaner answer, and killing Kingambit's allies is exactly what it wants. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Reuniclus, Pangoro, Nidoking and Sandaconda - a Trick Room setter with three unrelated bruisers and no reason to be at a space centre. It is cooler now because infiltration is the correct idea for the room where Magma takes the controls: an Illusion that costs the player a turn, then a Dark/Steel general who gets stronger every time the player wins an exchange. The two mechanics point the same way - the longer the fight runs, the worse the board gets - and the counterplay, a single spread move, is clean and fair.
crack: One spread move breaks the illusion and reveals the board; Fighting and Fire pressure on Kingambit, with the Fighting type kept away from Shiftry's Air Slash - a Fairy type hits three of the four super-effectively and fears only Kingambit's Iron Head; do not Water-attack Cacturne; remove Kingambit before its allies fall rather than after.

## E0407 TRAINER_GRUNT_SPACE_CENTER_7 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: The launch gantry, and the last grunt before Tabitha - the hardest non-admin fight in the villain arc. First two useful turns: Metagross is the thematic centre of the whole raid, because Maxie's entire plot is a meteorite and Metagross is the thing meteorites become; Clear Body refuses Intimidate and Icy Wind, and Meteor Mash has a one-in-five chance of raising its own Attack on every hit. Duraludon fires Draco Meteor or Flash Cannon while Stalwart means redirection - Follow Me, Rage Powder, Storm Drain and Lightning Rod - simply does not work on it. Alternate deployment: Cradily's Storm Drain is the room's own redirection and Suction Cups means it cannot be dragged out by Whirlwind, Roar or Dragon Tail. Reserves & disruption: Armaldo's Battle Armor denies critical hits and its Rock and Bug lanes cover the Psychic and Grass answers. Counterplay & audit: Fire and Ground pressure on the Steel pair, Fighting on Armaldo and Cradily, and spread Water moves bypass Storm Drain. Could this team be cooler? The old block was Liepard, Mamoswine, Incineroar and Miltank - a cat, a mammoth and a dairy cow guarding a rocket. It is cooler now because the gantry crew is built from things that fell out of the sky or were dug out of the ground: a meteorite, an alloy tower, and two revived fossils. Stalwart on Duraludon is the sharpest single detail - the player's redirection support, which has been the answer to spread threats all game, is switched off against exactly the attacker that needs answering.
crack: Fire and Ground attacks on Metagross, Duraludon and the Steel lane; Fighting pressure; spread Water bypasses Cradily; Duraludon ignores your redirection, so protect the target directly instead.

## E0408 TRAINER_TABITHA_MOSSDEEP class=admin
strategy: SAND,SETUP
mega_slots: 3
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Tabitha's second fight is the same man with the same squad and one upgrade the player can see coming - the Excadrill he already fought now holds a Mega Stone. First two useful turns: Gigalith's Sand Stream is free and Smooth Rock holds it eight turns; Mega Excadrill's Sand Rush then doubles its Speed and it outruns the entire player roster while hitting off a Mega Attack stat. Gigalith's own action is Wide Guard against the spread answer or Rock Slide. Alternate deployment: Coalossal returns as the same trap it was in the hideout - Steam Engine grants +6 Speed to any Fire or Water attack that fails to kill it, and the Water attack is both the obvious answer and 4x super effective, so the player must commit fully or not at all. Reserves & disruption: Landorus' Sheer Force Earth Power ignores the Rocky Helmets and Rough Skins the player brings to a physical team; Magnezone's Magnet Pull traps the Steel switch-ins that answer a Rock and Ground admin, which is the same lock his hideout gate crew used and the player should now recognise as a Tabitha habit; Krookodile's Moxie punishes a sloppy knockout. Counterplay & audit: replace the sand and Mega Excadrill loses half its Speed, kill Coalossal outright, and do not bring a Steel pivot you need to rotate. Could this team be cooler? The old build had Steelix, Gliscor and Darmanitan bolted onto the sand core - three species the Magma Hideout grunts already own, so the admin read as a grunt with better levels. It is cooler now because it reads as a person: Gigalith, Excadrill, Coalossal and Krookodile are Tabitha's, the player has fought them before, and the fight is about what he added rather than who he is. Magnet Pull returning as his trapping habit is the character detail worth keeping.
crack: Weather replacement halves Mega Excadrill; one large hit on Coalossal or none at all; keep a non-Steel pivot available; Water, Grass, Fighting and Ice pressure.

## E0409 TRAINER_COURTNEY_MOSSDEEP class=leader
strategy: MEGA_REVEAL
mega_slots: 1
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: This is a two-owner multi battle and Courtney's half is still the engine room, but her engine is now the one a Drizzle lead cannot outrun. First two useful turns: the opening board is Courtney's Charizard beside Maxie's Camerupt. A player's Drizzle wins the switch-in weather race, but Mega Evolution resolves at the start of turn one, after every switch-in ability, so Mega Charizard Y's Drought lands last and the sun is up before anyone moves; MEGA_REVEAL makes that transformation the first thing her side does. The same sun powers Charizard's Heat Wave into both slots and Maxie's Eruption at the end of the turn. Solar Beam fires without charging and is the lane into the Water and Ground bodies that came to beat Fire, Scorching Sands answers the Rock and Electric types aimed at Charizard, and a sun-turned Weather Ball becomes a Fire move that Heatran's Flash Fire eats. No SUN flag is set: the sun comes from a Mega transformation the flag gate cannot read and no member knows Sunny Day, so MEGA_REVEAL is the directive that carries the plan. Alternate deployment: Great Tusk holds Booster Energy, so Protosynthesis raises its Attack whether or not the sun survives - if the player re-sets rain by bringing the Drizzle user back in, Headlong Rush and Close Combat still hit from the boosted stat, and Rock Slide punishes the Flying rain setter. Reserves & disruption: Heatran is her ace and the anchor of the trap. Magma Storm holds a target for four to five turns while the rest of the enemy side works, Flash Fire turns the player's Fire answer into her damage, and Shuca Berry takes the edge off the Ground hit that is its one crippling weakness; it gives up its Mega Stone so the Mega belongs to the weather. Counterplay & audit: switching the Drizzle user out and back in re-sets rain after the Mega turn, and Charizard cannot Mega Evolve twice; Rock is 4x into Mega Charizard Y and Electric is 2x; Wide Guard blanks Heat Wave and Eruption on the same turn; Ground and Water pressure on Heatran; Fairy, Flying and Psychic on Great Tusk. Could this team be cooler? Yes. The old lead was a base-100 Ninetales whose Drought any slower Pelipper overwrote before turn one, so Great Tusk's ability, Maxie's Eruption and every Fire move ran in rain and the whole enemy side fell to Scald while the player lost nothing. That Ninetales also repeated Maxie's Mt. Chimney lead and her own hideout backup, and Great Tusk's set was a copy of her hideout's. It is cooler now because the analyst has answered the answer: the sun arrives after the Drizzle instead of before it, her Paradox no longer depends on it, and her half reads as a new plan rather than her hideout with members cut.
crack: Switch your Drizzle user out and back in after Charizard has Mega Evolved; Rock (4x) and Electric into Mega Charizard Y; Wide Guard the turn-one Heat Wave and Eruption; Ground and Water into Heatran; Fairy, Flying and Psychic into Great Tusk.

## E0409 TRAINER_MAXIE_MOSSDEEP class=leader
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: 1
plan: Maxie's half of the multi battle spends the sun that Courtney's Mega brings. First two useful turns: Camerupt now leads, so the opening board really is Mega Camerupt beside Courtney's Charizard. Both Mega Evolve at the start of turn one, Mega Charizard Y's Drought overwrites any Drizzle, and Camerupt's Eruption resolves last - full HP, 150 base power, sun-boosted, from a Mega Special Attack stat into both slots. If Camerupt is chipped first, Eruption weakens proportionally and Earth Power and Sheer Force Ancient Power take over. Alternate deployment: Raging Bolt is the answer that does not need the weather. Electric/Dragon resists Water, Fire, Grass and Electric; Protosynthesis in Courtney's sun raises its Special Attack; Thunderclap is priority Electric into an attacking Pelipper or Gyarados, Thunderbolt reaches the Water types that hang back, Draco Meteor is the heavy hit and Snarl blunts the special Water sweepers; Assault Vest keeps it on the field. Reserves & disruption: Copperajah is the slow Steel anchor that holds a slot while Courtney's Magma Storm runs; Heavy Metal doubles Heavy Slam's weight, Power Whip is its Grass lane into the Water and Ground bodies that expect to wall a Fire side, and Weakness Policy pays out on the Ground or Fighting hit. Counterplay & audit: Fake Out, Wide Guard, Protect or chip Camerupt on turn one; bring the Drizzle user back in after the Mega turn; Ice, Dragon, Fairy and Ground into Raging Bolt; Fire and Fighting into Copperajah; Water and Ground into Camerupt. Could this team be cooler? Yes. The old half was three members of the hideout stone crew with near-identical sets, Camerupt sat in the last slot behind Ace Pokemon so the turn-one Eruption the plan promised could not happen, and Rhyperior was a 4x Water weakness beside a sun that never arrived. It is cooler now because the party order produces the choreography the prose describes, and because Maxie's reserve is primeval thunder rather than another stone - the Magma thesis of an older, wilder land instead of a repeat of the hideout. Mega Camerupt is his signature in all three fights, but here it leads and spends someone else's sun; Copperajah returns with a different set and job.
crack: Fake Out, Wide Guard, Protect or chip damage on Camerupt's turn-one Eruption; bring your Drizzle user back in after the Mega turn; Ice, Dragon, Fairy and Ground into Raging Bolt; Fire and Fighting into Copperajah; Water and Ground into Camerupt.

## E0410 TRAINER_SOPHIA class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: a Grassy Terrain dance troupe. Tapu Bulu's Grassy Surge is the stage; Bellossom dances on it and Oricorio's Dancer copies every dance the instant it is performed. First two useful turns: Bellossom opens with Quiver Dance or Petal Dance while Tapu Bulu attacks; Oricorio's Dancer copies the dance for free, so one turn buys two boosted special attackers. Turn two spends the real boosts instead of stacking a third dance. Alternate deployment: when powder or Fire pressure denies the troupe, Mudsdale's Stamina/Body Press mode and Tapu Bulu's Horn Leech carry the fight with no setup. Reserves & disruption: Grassy Terrain heals the grounded members and halves Earthquake; Oricorio is airborne and gains none of it. Dancer copies dances only, never Horn Leech or Revelation Dance. Petal Dance locks Bellossom for two to three turns and confuses it afterwards; that cost is real. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and it now is: the old Contrary Lurantis was a solo stat-trick that Oricorio's Dancer could never touch, so the team's signature ability did nothing. Replacing it with Bellossom - a rare, literally dancing Grass mon with both Quiver Dance and Petal Dance - turns Dancer from decoration into the team's engine and gives the player a real decision (stop the dance on turn one or race two snowballing special attackers). Healer also quietly cures Tapu Bulu's status. Overrepresented? Lurantis is retained by Vito (E0487) so nothing is lost; Bellossom and Oricorio-Pom-Pom appear nowhere else in the campaign, and Mudsdale's other owner Herman (E0422) is retooled to Copperajah. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2 - the Mudsdale anchor pays for the two fragile dancers.
crack: Break the dance on turn one: Taunt or Encore Bellossom, or knock it out before Oricorio can copy. Fire, Flying and Ice attacks punish the whole troupe, Haze erases both copies at once, and terrain replacement removes Bulu's healing. Mudsdale folds to special attacks despite the Assault Vest.

## E0412 TRAINER_SEBASTIAN class=ace
strategy: REDIRECTION,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: a clean redirection-and-finisher pair backed by two very different walls. First two useful turns: Amoonguss uses Rage Powder only when it protects a Swords Dance or a Technician Bullet Punch worth protecting, and Spore when a legal non-Grass, non-Overcoat target is up; Scizor either dances or attacks. Turn two cashes the boost with Bullet Punch or Bug Bite rather than building a second time. Alternate deployment: Amoonguss plus Donphan is the no-setup mode, trading Rage Powder turns for immediate High Horsepower and Knock Off. Reserves & disruption: Dunsparce is the endgame - Coil raises Attack, Defence and accuracy while Eviolite gives it Dudunsparce-grade bulk it does not otherwise have, and Serene Grace doubles Body Slam's paralysis. Rapid Spin needs real hazards or a valued Speed boost, not automatic utility scoring. Could this team be cooler? The concept is sound and stays, but the Leftovers Dunsparce was a slow wall that could never finish anything. Giving it Eviolite is the smallest change that makes the third phase genuinely threatening: a Coil-boosted Eviolite Dunsparce with 60% paralysis Body Slam is a memorable, faintly absurd wall-breaker and exactly the kind of underdog the campaign wants, and it asks the player to carry a Fighting or special answer rather than out-muscling it. Overrepresented? Scizor, Donphan and Dunsparce are unique in the campaign; Amoonguss's other nearby owner Makayla (E0435) is rebuilt around Abomasnow, so this is now the only Rage Powder team in the stretch. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2.
crack: Fire attacks erase Scizor and Amoonguss together, Safety Goggles or a Grass type ignores the powder, and spread damage goes around Rage Powder entirely. Taunt shuts Coil and Swords Dance; without priority Scizor is slow. Dunsparce cannot outlast a Fighting attacker.

## E0420 TRAINER_SANTIAGO class=regular
strategy: SNOW
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: a tide-pool angler whose snow arrives with the lead, not three switches later. First two useful turns: Vanilluxe now leads, so Snow Warning is live before the first attack and its Blizzard is accurate from turn one; Gastrodon attacks or recovers behind it. Turn two chooses between Freeze-Dry into a Water target and Flash Cannon into a Rock or Fairy one. Alternate deployment: Poliwrath and Dewgong are the physical/control second wave - Encore punishes a repeated setup or protect, and Icy Wind keeps the speed advantage the snow team wants. Reserves & disruption: Storm Drain and Water Absorb solve different problems and neither is a general damage immunity; Gastrodon draws opposing single-target Water attacks, Poliwrath only absorbs what actually hits it. Could this team be cooler? The species are a good, unusual set for a coastal regular, but the team advertised SNOW while keeping its only weather setter on the bench, so its signature never happened and Blizzard was a coin flip. Leading Vanilluxe costs nothing and turns a mislabelled team into a real two-phase snow fight; Dewgong's illegal Freeze-Dry becomes Ice Beam so the set is honest. That is the whole improvement this trainer needs - a route angler should not also be a six-slot showcase. Overrepresented? Vanilluxe is unique; Dewgong's only other owner is Daphne's Sootopolis ice room (E0468), and Poliwrath's are Hudson (E0448) and Tiffany, all in distinct roles. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/0/1/1.
crack: Replace the weather and Blizzard becomes a liability; Grass and Electric attacks split the team in half; Taunt stops Encore and Recover. Gastrodon's Storm Drain is useless against spread Water and against anything that is not Water at all.

## E0422 TRAINER_HERMAN class=ace
strategy: TRICK_ROOM,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: a genuine Trick Room crew in which every member is deliberately, correctly slow. First two useful turns: Bronzong sets Trick Room when the reversed order actually helps - Mental Herb protects that turn from Taunt - while Gourgeist attacks or burns the biggest physical threat. Turn two spends the room on damage, not on a second support action. Alternate deployment: Bronzong plus Mega Crabominable is the heavy mode, with Iron Fist Ice Hammer and Drain Punch hitting under the reversed order. Reserves & disruption: Guts Heracross converts its Toxic Orb into Facade and Close Combat, Copperajah's Heavy Metal doubles its weight for Heavy Slam, and Florges is a separate Calm Mind/Wish endgame with Flower Veil protecting the team from stat drops. Body Press still reads the user's real Defence. Could this team be cooler? It already had a good idea and a bad implementation: every slow member carried a 31 Speed IV, so the room was fighting its own team. Zeroing Speed IVs on all five Brave/Relaxed/Quiet members is the difference between a Trick Room team and a team with a Trick Room move, and it is the single most valuable change here. Swapping the duplicated Mudsdale for Copperajah keeps the same slow-anvil role while giving the trainer a rarer, heavier signature that actually rewards Heavy Slam, and Mega Crabominable stays as the payoff nobody else in the campaign fields. Overrepresented? Copperajah, Crabominable and Gourgeist are unique; Bronzong and Gothitelle-style room setters are spaced far apart (Conor E0442, Warren E0439 use different setters). Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Taunt the setter, or simply be slower - a Trick Room of your own flips the whole fight. Fire and Flying attacks shred Gourgeist, Heracross and Copperajah; Haze undoes Coil-free boosts; and after five turns the room ends while the team is still built to be slow.

## E0423 TRAINER_SUSIE class=ace
strategy: TAILWIND,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: an open-water swimmer's team for Route 131 - the same Tailwind-then-payoff shape as before, now carried by sea Pokemon instead of a mountain gale, and ended by a Mega Feraligatr whose Dragonize turns Double-Edge into a boosted Dragon attack. First two useful turns: Floatzel opens with Wave Crash or priority Aqua Jet into a weakened target while Mantine sets Tailwind, or Wide Guard when the player's lead threatens a spread attack; Water Absorb means a Water attack aimed at Mantine heals it. Turn two uses the real speed order under Tailwind rather than spending a second support turn. Alternate deployment: Seismitoad and Inteleon are the answer lead for the Electric and Grass types every swimmer invites. Water/Ground Seismitoad is immune to Electric attacks and returns Earth Power, and Sludge Bomb punishes the Grass switch-in; Scope Lens Sniper Inteleon is the fastest body on the team and its critical Hydro Pumps and Ice Beams hit for more than double. Reserves & disruption: Primarina's Liquid Voice turns Hyper Voice into a Water spread attack and Throat Spray gives it +1 Special Attack on the first cast, so Calm Mind is its second boost rather than its first. Mega Feraligatr is the ace: Dragonize makes Double-Edge a boosted Dragon attack, Dragon Dance under Tailwind puts it ahead of everything, and Water/Dragon takes neutral damage from the Electric and Grass attacks that beat the rest of the team. AI executability: TAILWIND is backed by Mantine; SETUP is backed by Calm Mind, Throat Spray and Dragon Dance. Could this team be cooler? The previous team - Arcanine, Corviknight, Probopass, Sceptile, Volcarona and Mega Pidgeot - was a gale team on a Swimmer who stands in the ocean, and its Mega Pidgeot copied Ashley's showcase at the Fortree gym. Every slot is now a sea Pokemon doing the job its predecessor did: Floatzel is the fast physical lead with priority, Mantine carries both the Tailwind Corviknight set and the Wide Guard Probopass held, Seismitoad is the pivot that blanks Electric, Inteleon is the fast special attacker, Primarina is the special setup, and Feraligatr holds the Mega. Level offsets unchanged: +3 across the team, +4 on the Mega.
crack: Electric attacks still answer Floatzel, Mantine, Inteleon and Primarina - aim them away from Seismitoad, which is immune. Grass attacks hit everything except Mantine and Mega Feraligatr, and Seismitoad takes four times from Grass. Taunt Mantine before Tailwind or wait out its turns behind Protect; Wide Guard does nothing against single-target attacks. Fairy attacks hit Mega Feraligatr super-effectively, and every Dragonize Double-Edge costs it recoil.

## E0430 TRAINER_RONALD class=ace
strategy: RAIN,SETUP
mega_slots: 5
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: a fisherman who genuinely believes in his Magikarp, surrounded by the rain team that makes the joke almost work. First two useful turns: Kingdra sets Rain Dance only when its own Swift Swim or Gyarados's pressure repays the turn, otherwise it opens with Hydro Pump or Draco Meteor; Gyarados attacks after Intimidate or dances when the board is safe. Turn two uses the real weather and speed order. Alternate deployment: Raichu's Fake Out and Lightning Rod hold the field for Kingdra before a deliberately timed Mega, and Lightning Rod draws the Electric attack the Water core fears. Reserves & disruption: Magikarp is a genuine novelty slot - under rain its Swift Swim doubles its Speed and Flail from a Focus Sash-preserved 1 HP is a real, ridiculous knockout; it is not a sixth competitive set and must not be scored as one. Tentacruel covers Poison and speed control. Mega Raichu's ability replaces Lightning Rod, so the redirection stops the moment it transforms. Could this team be cooler? It is already one of the campaign's most characterful ideas and the answer is to protect it rather than replace it: the memorable interaction here is rain plus Focus Sash plus Flail, and a player who ignores Magikarp genuinely loses a Pokemon to it. Adding a legend or a second Mega would kill the joke. The only thing worth tightening is the level spread so the surrounding five are actually threatening enough that the player cannot afford to spend two turns laughing. Overrepresented? Kingdra is also Linda's (E0438) and Juan's ace, but this is the only Rain Dance/Life Orb version; Gyarados's other owners are an Aqua grunt and Juan's Mega; Mega Raichu Y appears only here, with Vito holding Raichu X. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/0/1/2.
crack: Replace the weather and both the sweeper and the joke die. Grass and Electric attacks cover the whole roster, spread damage breaks Magikarp's Sash before it can Flail, and Gyarados is one Rock Slide or Thunderbolt from gone. Deny Fake Out with your own and Raichu has no safe turn.

## E0432 TRAINER_PAXTON class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a bare-knuckle Fighting quartet with three entirely different ways to win - sleep, Guts, and a Body Press wall. First two useful turns: Breloom uses Spore only on a legal, non-Grass, non-Overcoat, non-Safety-Goggles target; otherwise Mach Punch or Technician-boosted Bullet Seed is the better opening while Ursaring takes its Flame Orb turn or attacks. Turn two converts Guts Facade and priority rather than hunting a second sleep. Alternate deployment: Luxray's Intimidate and Snarl grind both offensive stats down so Kommo-o can Iron Defence and start returning Body Press damage. Reserves & disruption: Technician boosts each individual Bullet Seed hit but does not fix the hit count the way Skill Link would; Rock Tomb's Speed drop is the set's real speed control. Could this team be cooler? The three win conditions are distinct and the sleep/Guts/wall split is genuinely good design for a route ace, so the composition earns retention. What would make it cooler is not a rarer species but a tighter threat clock: at the authored flat offsets the player could out-tempo all three plans at once, so the levels move up and Kommo-o - the slot the player most often ignores - gets the largest bump, because an Iron Defence Bulletproof wall that survives to turn four is what makes this fight memorable rather than a Breloom check. Overrepresented? Breloom's only other owner uses a Poison Heal set, Ursaring and Kommo-o are unique in the campaign, and Luxray's other appearance is the far later ship trainer Micah (E0505). Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2.
crack: Safety Goggles, a Grass type or Insomnia turns off Spore entirely. Fairy and Flying attacks answer three of the four; Taunt stops Iron Defence; and Bulletproof does not save Kommo-o from a physical Fairy or Dragon hit. Remove the Flame Orb before it activates and Ursaring is merely slow.
