## E0433 TRAINER_DARCY class=ace
strategy: SUN,SETUP
mega_slots: 4
ai: Ace Pokemon
tactic: AFTER_YOU LILLIGANT AFTER_YOU TORKOAL
plan: Concept: the advanced student's answer to Flannery - the same After You plus Eruption idea, but with a second sun setter so the player cannot simply erase the weather once. First two useful turns: the authored tactic makes Lilligant offer After You to Torkoal when that actually lands a full-power Eruption before the opposing threats move; otherwise Lilligant uses Sleep Powder or Leaf Storm and Torkoal attacks in its own slow order. Turn two rechecks Torkoal's current HP, because Eruption scales with it, and whether the ally has already acted - After You is worthless then. Alternate deployment: Lilligant's sleep and support enable Porygon-Z's Adaptability Tri Attack or a Nasty Plot turn when the sun has been taken away. Reserves & disruption: Mega Charizard Y is the second real Drought, so Lilligant's manual Sunny Day is an emergency reclaim, not mandatory redundancy; Chlorophyll only doubles Lilligant's Speed while sun is actually up. Could this team be cooler? The idea was already strong but it was not executable - the block described an After You engine that no tactic line told the AI to perform, so in play it was a generic sun team. Adding the explicit AFTER_YOU tactic is what turns the concept into the fight the prose promises, and the weather-reclaim second setter is exactly the escalation a post-Flannery sun trainer should have. Beyond that it does not need rarer species; Porygon-Z is the one member a player never prepares for, and that is the fight's real teeth. Overrepresented? Torkoal and Lilligant recur with Flannery and Julie (E0490), which is deliberate - this trainer is explicitly a variation on that theme - but the two sun sources, the Focus Sash Lilligant and the Adaptability pivot make the board play differently. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2.
crack: Take the sun and keep taking it; Sleep Powder fails through Safety Goggles and Grass types. Taunt kills After You, Sunny Day and Nasty Plot together. Wide Guard blanks Eruption and Heat Wave, and Torkoal is helpless once it has been chipped.

## E0434 TRAINER_JONATHAN class=ace
strategy: TAILWIND
mega_slots: 4
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: fossil speed - a Sheer Force glass cannon that only works inside the Tailwind window its partner opens. First two useful turns: Aerodactyl sets Tailwind or Taunts the opposing support while Rampardos attacks; Focus Sash means Aerodactyl survives to do at least one of those. Turn two spends the speed window on damage instead of adding more support. Alternate deployment: base Sableye's Prankster Fake Out and Will-O-Wisp protect Rampardos through a turn before a deliberate Mega transition. Reserves & disruption: Sawsbuck's Sap Sipper gives a free Attack boost against Grass attacks and a Clear Amulet against Intimidate; Mega Sableye loses Prankster, so the priority support and the Mega ability can never be counted at the same time. Sheer Force applies only to moves with secondary effects - Zen Headbutt and Fire Punch, not Rock Slide's flinch-free damage - and it cancels Life Orb recoil on those moves. Could this team be cooler? The Sheer Force plus Life Orb recoil-cancel interaction is a genuinely satisfying thing for a player to discover mid-fight, and the base-Prankster-versus-Mega decision is a real choice the AI has to make, so the concept holds. What it needed was pressure: Rampardos folds to a single resisted hit, so the fight only becomes memorable if the Tailwind turn is actually lethal, which the raised offsets provide. A rarer fossil would not improve it - Rampardos's absurd Attack stat is the point. Overrepresented? Aerodactyl recurs with Roxanne's Mega and Hope (E0479) but never with this Taunt/Sash utility role; Rampardos, Sawsbuck and Mega Sableye are unique in this stretch. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2.
crack: Any priority move beats the Tailwind plan, and Water, Grass and Steel attacks answer the fossils. Wide Guard blunts Rock Slide; Taunt stops Tailwind and Will-O-Wisp; and if Rampardos misses once it usually does not get a second turn.

## E0435 TRAINER_MAKAYLA class=ace
strategy: SNOW,SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: a snowfield brawler - Abomasnow brings the weather with it, and Beartic's Slush Rush turns that weather into raw speed on the very first turn. First two useful turns: Abomasnow's Snow Warning is active on entry, so Beartic is already fast; Abomasnow attacks with Wood Hammer or Ice Shard while Medicham opens with Fake Out or a Pure Power Close Combat. Turn two uses the real speed order under snow rather than spending a turn establishing weather that already exists. Alternate deployment: Medicham and Flareon are the weather-independent physical mode - Pure Power and Guts Facade both hit hard with no setup at all. Reserves & disruption: snow raises the Defence of Ice types, so Abomasnow and Beartic are tougher than their listed stats suggest; Flareon's Toxic Orb Guts mode gains nothing from the weather and Charti Berry cushions exactly one Rock attack on Beartic. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and the fix was structural rather than cosmetic. The authored version flagged SNOW while relying on Beartic spending a turn on Snowscape, so the team's own Slush Rush sweeper had to waste the turn it needed to sweep, and the redirecting Amoonguss duplicated Sebastian's team two encounters earlier. Abomasnow solves both at once: automatic snow, a second Ice attacker, a Grass answer to the Water and Ground types that wall the rest, and Ice Shard priority that pairs with Medicham's Fake Out to steal turns. The memorable interaction is now the double opening - Fake Out plus Ice Shard removing a Focus Sash lead before it acts. Overrepresented? Abomasnow's other owner Dianne (E0492) uses a Quiet special Blizzard set in Victory Road, so the physical version here is clearly distinct; Beartic, Medicham and Flareon are unique in the stretch, and Amoonguss now belongs solely to Sebastian (E0412). Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2.
crack: Fire and Fighting attacks hit the whole team, and replacing the weather strips Beartic's Speed and the Ice-type Defence bonus at once. Medicham and Beartic are both fragile - one resisted hit each - and Fake Out only works on the turn they enter.

## E0438 TRAINER_LINDA class=regular
strategy: RAIN,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 1
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: a squall on the open water - Mega Kingdra calls the rain and three very different bodies exploit it. First two useful turns: Kingdra sets Rain Dance or opens with Muddy Water, which is spread and therefore cannot be stolen by an ally, while Lumineon attacks or sets Tailwind. Turn two stacks Swift Swim on top of Tailwind for a speed tier nothing at this stage matches. Alternate deployment: Araquanid's Wide Guard and Water Bubble mode covers Arctozolt, whose Bolt Beak doubles in power whenever it moves before its target - which is precisely what the rain and Tailwind are for. Reserves & disruption: Sticky Web is a long-term investment and must beat an immediate attack to be worth the turn; Water Bubble doubles Araquanid's Water damage and halves incoming Fire. Could this team be cooler? It is cooler now for one concrete reason. Lumineon previously ran Storm Drain, which meant the team's own single-target Water attacks were being pulled away from their targets by their own partner - a real strategic defect, not a stylistic one. Switching it to Swift Swim removes the self-sabotage and makes Lumineon the second rain abuser the RAIN tag always implied, so the fight now reads as one coherent squall instead of three unrelated Water mons. The Bolt Beak plus double speed control interaction is the thing a player will remember, and it is worth the Mega slot. Overrepresented? Mega Kingdra exists only here; Lumineon's other owner Connie runs the Storm Drain version deliberately, in the one gym room built to use it. Araquanid and Arctozolt are unique in the stretch. Levels: cap 76, offsets 0/1/1/1.
crack: Grass and Electric attacks cover the whole team and Ground types are immune to Bolt Beak's best turn. Replace the weather or win the speed war with priority and the Bolt Beak plan collapses; Wide Guard only stops spread moves, so attack Araquanid directly.

## E0439 TRAINER_WARREN class=ace
strategy: TRICK_ROOM
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: two Trick Room setters and four deliberately slow bodies, ending in a Mega Drampa that fires at full power under a room somebody else paid for. First two useful turns: Musharna sets Trick Room or Helping Hands a useful Alolan Golem attack; Galvanize converts Golem's Double-Edge into a same-power Electric attack with STAB, which also means it does nothing at all to a Ground target. Turn two spends the reversed order rather than re-casting the room. Alternate deployment: Cofagrigus is the backup setter for the second half, holding the room open for Mega Drampa's Hyper Voice and Draco Meteor. Reserves & disruption: Torterra and Aggron are the slow physical coverage; Mummy replaces the ability of anything that makes contact with Cofagrigus, which can silently turn off a player's Intimidate, Guts or Multiscale. Telepathy protects Musharna from a partner's spread move, not from opponents. Could this team be cooler? The two-setter structure and the Galvanize trick were good; what made the team weaker than it looked was that every slow member carried a 31 Speed IV, so the room it worked to set was partly wasted on its own side. Zeroing Speed on all five Brave/Quiet/Sassy members is the change that makes the concept function, and Cofagrigus's Mummy is now worth calling out as the signature interaction - a player whose Intimidate lead punches it loses the ability for the rest of the fight. The species set is already unusual enough for a route ace and does not need replacing. Overrepresented? Alolan Golem, Drampa, Cofagrigus and Musharna are unique in the campaign; Torterra recurs only with Owen (E0488) in Victory Road, and the neighbouring room teams (Herman E0422, Conor E0442) use entirely different setters and payoffs. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Taunt the setter, or bring your own Trick Room and reverse the reversal. Ground types ignore Galvanize entirely; Fighting and Fire attacks answer the slow core. Wait five turns and the room ends while the team is still built to be slow.

## E0441 TRAINER_MOLLIE class=ace
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a haunted-cold quartet with no weather and no setup - four immediate threats and one very deliberate accuracy gamble. First two useful turns: Gengar attacks or uses Icy Wind for speed control while Carracosta attacks or selects Wide Guard against a genuine spread threat. Turn two acts on the resulting order instead of assuming every attack is now safe. Alternate deployment: Aromatisse's Helping Hand and Wish support Rotom-Frost's Blizzard and Thunderbolt coverage; Aroma Veil protects the pair from Taunt, Encore and Attract but not from damage or ordinary status. Reserves & disruption: Rotom-Frost's Blizzard is its form's signature attack and there is no snow in this team, so it is a knowingly inaccurate 110-power gamble - the Wide Lens is the honest fix rather than pretending a weather source exists. Rock Gem is a single-use Rock Slide amplifier on Carracosta and Sturdy guarantees it survives one hit to use it. Could this team be cooler? The unusual part - a Ghost, a fossil turtle, a perfumer and an appliance ghost with no shared engine - is actually the point: this is a fight about four independent threats rather than one combo, which is a welcome change of pace between two Trick Room aces. The change it needed was honesty about Blizzard. Wide Lens is a small, legal, in-character fix that makes the gamble a real 91% threat rather than a coin flip, and it keeps Rotom-Frost's identity intact instead of replacing a rare form with a safer one. Overrepresented? Rotom-Frost, Carracosta's Rock Gem set and Aromatisse are unique; Gengar's other campaign owners are Samuel - retooled to Mismagius in this pass - Leaf's Kanto team and Phoebe's Mega, so this remains the only Focus Sash Icy Wind version. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2.
crack: Dark, Ground and Rock attacks hit three of the four, and Aromatisse's Aroma Veil does not stop damage. Single-target attacks walk around Wide Guard; Wish only pays out at the end of the following turn, so kill the recipient first. Blizzard can still miss.

## E0442 TRAINER_CONOR class=ace
strategy: TRICK_ROOM
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a trapper's room - Shadow Tag means the player cannot simply switch out of the reversed speed order. First two useful turns: Hariyama's Fake Out buys Gothitelle the turn it needs to set Trick Room; turn two attacks into the reversed order or uses Helping Hand at a concrete knockout threshold. Alternate deployment: Gothitelle and Ferrothorn are the grinding mode - Leech Seed, Iron Barbs and Gyro Ball punish contact and passivity while the trap holds. Reserves & disruption: Avalugg brings Wide Guard, Body Press and Recover but has no Trick Room of its own, so it must be deployed while the room is still running. Shadow Tag stops switching, not targeting, and does not protect the setter from being attacked; Ghost types and Shed Shell ignore it entirely. The Wiki Berry restores HP at low health and is Modest-safe because Gothitelle's Quiet nature does not lower Special Attack. Could this team be cooler? The trap-plus-room pairing is genuinely nasty and is the right identity for this slot, but like the other room teams here it was quietly sabotaging itself with 31 Speed IVs on every slow member. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2.
crack: Ghost types, Shed Shell and U-turn all beat Shadow Tag. Taunt the setter before Fake Out resolves, or bring your own slow team. Fire and Fighting attacks answer Ferrothorn and Avalugg, and the room lasts only five turns.

## E0446 TRAINER_AARON class=ace
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 4
plan: Concept: a dragon tamer whose whole plan is one speed window and four different ways to spend it. First two useful turns: Noivern chooses Tailwind when it creates a real Tyrantrum turn, or Super Fang to halve a wall that nothing else on the team can break; Tyrantrum attacks. Turn two attacks through the speed window rather than adding more support. Alternate deployment: Noivern's Infiltrator ignores screens and Substitutes, so it is also the answer to a defensive opening, and it supports Mega Dragonite's Dragon Dance or immediate Extreme Speed pressure. Reserves & disruption: Druddigon's Sheer Force plus Haban Berry mode is the bulky coverage lane and the room's answer to its own counters - Sheer Force Iron Head hits both Fairy and Ice super-effectively, and Fire Punch covers the Steel type that resists it; Strong Jaw boosts Tyrantrum's Crunch and Ice Fang specifically, not its Rock or Dragon attacks. Multiscale only halves damage while Dragonite is at full HP, so a single chip attack removes it before the dance. Could this team be cooler? The four-dragon spread is thematically tight and the Strong Jaw/Sheer Force/Multiscale trio gives three distinct damage models, so the composition earns retention. Its real weakness was pacing - at flat offsets the Tailwind turn arrived too late to matter - so the levels rise and Mega Dragonite in particular gets the bump, because the fight should end with a Dragon Dance sweep the player has to pre-empt, not survive by accident. Adding a legendary dragon here would step on Drake's League identity and is deliberately avoided. Overrepresented? Tyrantrum recurs only with Roxanne far earlier in a Rock Head Assault Vest role; Noivern and Druddigon are unique; Dragonite's other owners are Shannon - retooled to Goodra in this pass - and Drake's League Mega, keeping this the only Mega Dragonite before the League.
crack: Ice and Fairy attacks answer everything here except Druddigon, whose Sheer Force Iron Head punishes both - take it out first, ideally with a Steel type that resists Iron Head and walls Dragon Claw. Chip Dragonite once and Multiscale is gone before it dances; priority beats the Tailwind plan outright; Wide Guard blanks Rock Slide, and a Trick Room reverses the entire strategy.

## E0448 TRAINER_HUDSON class=regular
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: a sailor who beats weather teams by refusing to have any - three different absorption abilities and a Cloud Nine switch that turns everyone else's engine off. First two useful turns: Poliwrath attacks while Raticate either attacks or takes its Flame Orb turn; turn two often U-turns into whichever immunity the opposing board has just revealed it needs. Alternate deployment: Cloud Nine Golduck and Volt Absorb Lanturn are the weather-suppression mode - while Golduck is on the field neither side gets rain, sun, sand or snow benefits, which is a real answer to the rain teams that dominate this stretch of coast. Reserves & disruption: Water Absorb, Volt Absorb and Cloud Nine solve three different problems and none of them is general damage reduction; Cloud Nine suppresses weather effects while active but does not clear the weather, so it returns the moment Golduck leaves. Could this team be cooler? It is the right kind of cool already, and the honest judgement is that its value is positional rather than flashy: dropped between Linda's squall, Ronald's rain and the Seafloor Cavern, a deliberate anti-weather sailor is a better encounter than another Swift Swim team would be, and the three-immunity structure teaches the player to check abilities before choosing a spread attack. The improvement it wanted was threat, not novelty - Raticate's Guts Facade and the U-turn pivot are what punish a lazy switch, so the levels rise and the pivot pair leads. Overrepresented? Golduck's Cloud Nine role recurs once, with Annika's Sootopolis room, where it is used to suppress that gym's own rain - a deliberate echo. Poliwrath appears with Santiago and Tiffany in different ability roles; Lanturn's other owner is Crissy's gym room; Raticate is unique. Levels: cap 76, offsets 0/1/1/1.
crack: Grass and Electric attacks split the roster despite the absorbers, priority beats the pivots, and trapping Raticate or Golduck removes the whole plan. Knock off the Flame Orb before it activates and Raticate is harmless; Golduck is frail and dies to focused special damage.

## E0450 TRAINER_MARLEY class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 4
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the last serious trainer before the Seafloor Cavern - powder control, a one-shot Bug Gem nuke, priority from the sky, and a Mega wall that answers projectiles. First two useful turns: Venusaur uses Sleep Powder on a legal target or attacks while Escavalier applies immediate heavy damage; Bug Gem makes its first Megahorn a genuine one-turn removal that it can never repeat. Turn two attacks through the control or repositions after powder denial. Alternate deployment: Talonflame's Gale Wings gives Brave Bird priority while it is at full HP, which supports Mega Chesnaught's slow physical pressure and provides Tailwind if the board turns into a speed race. Reserves & disruption: this Venusaur has Overgrow, not Chlorophyll or Thick Fat, and there is no sun - do not assume a remembered species ability. Bulletproof makes Chesnaught immune to ball and bomb moves including Sludge Bomb, Shadow Ball and Focus Blast, and Spiky Shield damages contact attackers that try to break a protect. Could this team be cooler? The Bug Gem and Bulletproof ideas were already good; the weak link was a third Arcanine-shaped Intimidate body doing the same job as Susie's two encounters earlier. Talonflame is both rarer and more interesting: Gale Wings priority Brave Bird changes how the player must sequence a knockout, and the Gale Wings-at-full-HP condition gives the player a lever - chip it once and the priority is gone. That is a genuine interaction rather than a stat swap. Overrepresented? Talonflame, Escavalier's Bug Gem set and Mega Chesnaught are unique after this pass - Shannon's duplicate Escavalier (E0483) is replaced with Vikavolt - and Arcanine now belongs solely to Susie (E0423). Venusaur recurs with Julie's sun team and Leaf's Kanto team in different ability roles. Levels: cap 76, offsets 1/1/1/2.
crack: Safety Goggles, Grass types and Insomnia turn off Sleep Powder. Fire and Flying attacks hit most of the roster; Rock attacks delete Talonflame. Bulletproof covers ball and bomb moves only, so hit Chesnaught with a beam, a punch or a spread attack instead, and remember Escavalier's Gem is gone after one use.

ESCAVALIER @BUG_GEM OVERCOAT BRAVE 252/252/4/0/0/0 3 | MEGAHORN, IRON_HEAD, DRILL_RUN, PROTECT | ivs=31/31/31/31/31/0 | friendship=255

## E0452 TRAINER_GRUNT_SEAFLOOR_CAVERN_1 class=grunt
strategy: TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: the boarding party at the cavern mouth - Aqua's fastest, meanest raiders, with no weather to hide behind. First two useful turns: Crobat chooses Tailwind or Taunt while Obstagoon attacks after its Flame Orb has activated Guts; Obstruct is a Protect that also drops the Defence of anything that makes contact with it, which is exactly what a boarding-party bruiser should do. Turn two brings the correct fast reserve rather than spending another turn on speed control the team already has. Alternate deployment: Crobat's Infiltrator ignores screens and Substitutes, supporting Barraskewda's Propeller Tail, which cannot be redirected by Follow Me, Rage Powder or Storm Drain - the answer to a player leaning on a redirector. Reserves & disruption: Sharpedo's Speed Boost must be earned turn by turn and previews the boss's signature; Basculin's Adaptability makes Wave Crash brutal at a real recoil cost. This grunt has no rain source, so nothing here is Swift Swim - do not infer weather from Aqua membership. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and the change is about faction identity rather than power. Mightyena is Magma and Aqua's shared filler and already belongs to Maxie's Mt. Chimney team; Obstagoon - a Galarian punk with Guts, Facade and Obstruct - is rarer, unmistakably piratical, and adds a mechanic (a defence-dropping protect) that no other grunt in the campaign fields, so the first room of the raid teaches the player something instead of repeating an earlier fight. Basculin's reckless Wave Crash completes the "everyone here hurts themselves on purpose" character of the crew. Overrepresented? Obstagoon and Basculin appear nowhere else in the Aqua arc; Crobat is shared with Maxie's Magma team as a deliberate cross-faction speed tool; Barraskewda's other owner is Bethany's Sootopolis burst room with a Choice Band instead of a Sash. Levels: cap 84, offsets 0/1/1/1 - the opening room of the escalation.
crack: Priority beats the Tailwind plan; Fairy, Fighting and Electric attacks cover the roster. Chip the recoil users and they finish themselves. Remove Obstagoon's Flame Orb before it activates, and remember Propeller Tail only bypasses redirection - it does not bypass Protect or Wide Guard.

## E0453 TRAINER_GRUNT_SEAFLOOR_CAVERN_2 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: the second landing - a bruiser who buys one Shell Smash, and a poison-reef reserve that does not care whether the smash happened. First two useful turns: Scrafty's Fake Out buys Huntail a survivable Shell Smash, or Scrafty simply attacks when the boost cannot survive; White Herb immediately undoes the Defence and Special Defence drops, so the smash is far less suicidal than it looks. Turn two uses the real boosts, or pivots when opposing priority would end the threat anyway. Alternate deployment: Scrafty's Intimidate and Assault Vest let it front for Glimmora's immediate special coverage when no setup window exists. Reserves & disruption: Muk supplies Helping Hand and heavy physical Poison pressure, and Poison Touch can poison anything it strikes. Glimmora is the reef itself - a Rock/Poison crystal coral whose Toxic Debris scatters Toxic Spikes on the player's side whenever a physical attack hits it, so every physical answer the player sends in afterwards arrives poisoned; Power Gem, Sludge Bomb and Earth Power cover the Fire, Fairy, Steel and Electric bodies that would otherwise wall the crew. Water Veil blocks burns only, not the Defence drops or ordinary status. This is the room after the boarding party and before Shelly, so its levels rise a step and the reef is what the player should remember.
crack: Taunt or a faster attacker stops the smash before White Herb matters; priority removes Huntail after it boosts. Ground and Water attacks answer Glimmora, special attacks avoid its Toxic Debris, and Ground and Psychic answer Muk; Grass and Electric cover the Water half, and Fake Out can be denied with your own. Poison and Steel types ignore the Toxic Spikes.

## E0454 TRAINER_SHELLY_SEAFLOOR_CAVERN class=admin
strategy: RAIN,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: Archie's lieutenant and the cavern's first real wall - she brings the rain the boss will inherit, and every member of her crew is built to use it. First two useful turns: Politoed's Drizzle is live on entry and Damp Rock stretches it to eight turns; it supports Choice Specs Keldeo with Helping Hand or slows the board with Icy Wind while Keldeo fires. Turn two respects Keldeo's lock and the actual weather state rather than assuming both. Alternate deployment: Swift Swim Ludicolo is the rain sweeper and answers the Grass and Water types that wall the rest; Hisuian Qwilfish fronts with Intimidate and Eviolite bulk while Barb Barrage doubles in power against an already-poisoned target. Reserves & disruption: Crawdaunt is the Adaptability priority reserve whose Aqua Jet ignores the speed war entirely, and Mega Dragalge is the special Dragon/Poison lane with Adaptability behind it. Secret Sword deals physical damage using Keldeo's Special Attack, which is why the Specs set works; Justified does not make it a physical attacker. Could this team be cooler? Yes - and the version that reaches the player now is. Clefable's Follow Me was a competent but entirely non-Aqua slot: a Fairy support in a pirate hideout, doing a job the faction has no business doing. Hisuian Qwilfish replaces it with something that reads as Shelly's own prized fish, keeps an Intimidate body in the lead slot, and sets up its own Barb Barrage payoff through Muk's and Dragalge's poison. The result is six water/dark/poison bodies, one weather, and three separate ways to convert it - which is what an admin fight should be before the boss does the same thing bigger. Overrepresented? Politoed's Drizzle recurs with Juan four encounters later, which is intentional contrast - Shelly uses rain for burst, Juan uses it for attrition - and Archie now runs Pelipper instead, so the three rain bosses use three different setters. Keldeo, Crawdaunt and Mega Dragalge are unique in the campaign. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/3/3/2/3/3 - a clear step above the grunt rooms and below Archie.
crack: Take the rain away and half the roster slows down; Grass and Electric attacks hit almost everything. Keldeo is locked into one move, so bait the wrong one. Wide Guard blanks Muddy Water and Icy Wind, Haze erases Swift Swim's benefit indirectly by removing the boosts it enables, and Hisuian Qwilfish folds to Ground and Psychic attacks.

## E0455 TRAINER_GRUNT_SEAFLOOR_CAVERN_5 class=grunt
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: the brig - past Shelly, the crew gets nastier: two separate physical setup threats behind a genuinely obnoxious poison wall. First two useful turns: Mandibuzz sets Tailwind or Taunts the opposing support while Gyarados attacks after Intimidate, or dances when the board is safe; Lum Berry covers exactly one paralysis or burn attempt aimed at the dance. Turn two spends the speed or the boost instead of adding a second setup on the same side. Alternate deployment: Toxapex's Regenerator, Baneful Bunker and Haze mode is the attrition lead - Baneful Bunker protects and poisons whatever makes contact, and Haze erases the player's boosts as well as its own team's. Reserves & disruption: Skuntank supplies Sucker Punch priority and Aftermath damage when it faints; Overcoat makes Mandibuzz immune to powder and weather chip; Foul Play uses the target's own Attack, so it punishes a physical setup sweeper hardest. Could this team be cooler? It is cooler for a structural reason rather than a species-rarity one. The authored version had Arbok's Coil as a third setup plan, duplicating Maxie's Mt. Chimney Arbok exactly and giving the room three slow boosters that could never all be used. Toxapex replaces it with the thing the raid actually lacked - a wall the player cannot simply out-damage - and Haze turns the room into a genuine trap for a player who has been winning these fights by setting up. The memorable moment is a Baneful Bunker that poisons a contact attacker on the same turn it blanks the attack. Overrepresented? Toxapex recurs with Juan's gym in a Wide Guard support role rather than this Haze/Bunker one; Mandibuzz, Skuntank and this Lum Berry Gyarados are otherwise unique, and Arbok is returned to Maxie alone. Levels: cap 84, offsets 1/2/2/2.
crack: Electric, Fairy and Ground attacks answer the roster; Psychic hits Toxapex hard. Taunt stops Tailwind, Dragon Dance and Haze at once. Do not feed Foul Play by boosting Attack, and beware that killing Skuntank costs you HP.

## E0456 TRAINER_GRUNT_SEAFLOOR_CAVERN_3 class=grunt
strategy: RAIN
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: the whale room - the cavern's artillery piece. Wailord brings its own storm and fires a Water Spout that is only terrifying while it is still healthy. First two useful turns: Wailord establishes rain on entry and chooses Water Spout from its execution-time HP, which is why the player must damage it before it acts rather than after; Seaking attacks under Swift Swim, already doubled by its partner's weather. Turn two switches to Hydro Pump once Wailord's HP has fallen far enough that Spout is the weaker option. Alternate deployment: Drapion and Morpeko are the independent physical and control mode that does not care about weather - Sniper turns Drapion's Scope Lens critical hits into genuine removals, and Parting Shot drops both of a target's attacking stats before pivoting. Reserves & disruption: Hunger Switch flips Morpeko's form and therefore Aura Wheel's type at the end of every turn, so the same move is Electric on one turn and Dark on the next; plan around the current form, not the remembered one. Could this team be cooler? The Water Spout artillery idea is one of the best in the Aqua arc and stays. Its defect was self-inflicted: Seaking carried Lightning Rod, which meant the team's own Morpeko had its Electric Aura Wheel pulled away by its partner half the time - a real bug in the plan, not a nuance. Swift Swim fixes it and simultaneously makes Seaking a second rain abuser, so the room finally plays as one rainstorm with a cannon in it. The alternating Aura Wheel type is now a genuine read-the-board puzzle rather than an accident. Overrepresented? Wailord's Drizzle set, Drapion and Morpeko are unique in the campaign; Seaking's other owner Connie deliberately keeps Lightning Rod because her entire gym room is built on redirection. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2.
crack: Hit Wailord first and Water Spout collapses with its HP. Grass and Electric attacks answer the Water pair, Wide Guard blanks Water Spout entirely, and replacing the weather halves Seaking's Speed. Ground types are immune to Electric Aura Wheel but not to the Dark one.

## E0457 TRAINER_GRUNT_SEAFLOOR_CAVERN_4 class=grunt
strategy: RAIN
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: the last door before Archie - four sets of jaws. Every member here bites, and the rain exists purely to make them faster. First two useful turns: Purugly's Fake Out buys Gorebyss the turn to set Rain Dance when Swift Swim and Water pressure repay it; otherwise Gorebyss opens with Muddy Water. Turn two attacks under the doubled Speed or pivots when the weather has been denied. Reserves & disruption: Wave Crash costs Bruxish a third of the damage dealt as recoil; Defiant means any stat drop aimed at Purugly - including an opposing Intimidate - raises its Attack by two stages instead. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and it needed to be. The authored room ended with Toxapex and Overqwil both playing defence, so once Gorebyss fell the last door of an Aqua raid turned into a stall match - exactly the wrong note before the boss. Bruxish replaces the wall with a rare, vicious Water/Psychic biter that keeps the pressure up, breaks screens, and gives the room a real identity: the jaws room, where Fake Out, Intimidate and Defiant all interact on the first turn. Overqwil's Intimidate now has an aggressive partner to protect rather than a second wall to hide behind. Overrepresented? Bruxish and Gorebyss are unique in the campaign; Toxapex is moved to the brig room (E0455) where its Haze has a purpose; Purugly and Overqwil appear once each elsewhere in different roles. ## E0458 TRAINER_ARCHIE class=leader
strategy: RAIN,SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
field: WEATHER_RAIN
plan: Concept: the Aqua apex. Archie wakes Kyogre before this battle and fights with it rising behind him, so the chamber keeps Kyogre's weather: the battle opens in rain authored as a starting field. A Drought, Sunny Day or Desolate Land can still take it, and Pelipper's Drizzle takes it back each time it switches in; that contest is the answer to having his rain taken, and it mirrors Maxie in Groudon's chamber: each leader fights inside the weather of the legend he has just woken, and neither legend is on his team. First two useful turns: with the rain already falling, Pelipper's turn is spent on Wide Guard against a spread lead, Tailwind when the board is a speed race, or Hurricane, which never misses in this rain; Wacan Berry halves the Electric hit that is 4x into it. Toxicroak leads beside it with Fake Out, stealing the player's first action while Dry Skin heals it every turn the rain holds. Turn two commits to the real board: Seismitoad's Swift Swim doubles its Speed in the rain, so Hydro Pump and Earth Power come before most of the player's team can answer, and Earth Power is the super-effective hit into a Primal Groudon whose own Water weakness the rain keeps live. Alternate deployment: Dhelmise is the ghost ship's anchor and the fight's signature - Anchor Shot deals Steelworker-boosted damage and traps the target, so a player planning to switch around the rain simply cannot, and Power Whip covers the Grass and Water types that expect to wall this roster. Alolan Muk is the weather-independent lane: Poison/Dark typing, Knock Off, Gunk Shot and Poison Touch on contact. Reserves & disruption: Mega Sharpedo is the closer; its base Speed Boost must be earned before transforming, because the Mega ability replaces it. Counterplay & audit: your own weather replaces the rain until Pelipper returns; Cloud Nine or Air Lock switch it off while they stand, and Utility Umbrella shields one Pokemon; Electric and Grass attacks answer most of the roster; Ground answers Toxicroak and Muk; Fire, halved in the rain, still answers Dhelmise; Wide Guard blanks Hurricane; Ghost types and Shed Shell escape Anchor Shot; deny Fake Out with your own; chip Sharpedo before it transforms. Could this team be cooler? The roster already was; the board was not. In the playtest a player's Mega Charizard Y overwrote the only rain setter on turn one, Solar Beam and sun Heat Wave ran through the Water half, and Archie fell in five turns with four of the player's six standing. It is cooler now because the fight opens where the scene says it happens, in the rain of the Kyogre Archie has just woken, and a player who takes the weather has to keep taking it while Pelipper can come back for it. Overrepresented? Pelipper's other owner is Bridget's fossil-rain gym room, a different weather payoff; Toxicroak, Dhelmise and Alolan Muk appear nowhere else in the campaign; Seismitoad's other owner Tiffany runs the Water Absorb version in the Sootopolis gym.
crack: Your own weather replaces the opening rain until Pelipper switches back in; Cloud Nine or Air Lock switch it off while they stand, Utility Umbrella shields one Pokemon. Electric and Grass attacks answer most of the roster; Ground answers Toxicroak and Muk; Fire still answers Dhelmise; Wide Guard blanks Hurricane; Ghost types and Shed Shell escape Anchor Shot; deny Fake Out with your own; chip Sharpedo before it transforms.

## E0459 TRAINER_ANDREA class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: room one of eleven - the volley room. Sootopolis opens by teaching the player that a Water gym can simply out-damage them, with multi-hit attacks that ignore Substitutes, Focus Sashes and Sturdy. First two useful turns: Cloyster uses Shell Smash only when Floatzel's Choice Band damage buys it a survivable turn; otherwise both simply attack, because Skill Link Icicle Spear at five hits is already close to a boosted attack. Turn two handles Wave Crash recoil, the Sash and opposing priority before committing the boosted Cloyster, whose Drill Run backs up the room's Electric answer - the Sash is its protection, so Protect gave way for it. Alternate deployment: Mamoswine fronts for Simipour's special coverage, which covers the Grass types the rest of the room fears with Grass Knot. Mamoswine is the room's answer to the Electric lead that would otherwise walk through six Water bodies: Ground typing makes it immune, Loaded Dice Icicle Spear keeps the volley lesson at four or five hits and hits Electric-Dragons twice over, High Horsepower punishes the Electric-Fighting and Electric-Steel types, Thick Fat blunts the Fire hit aimed at its Ice half, and Ice Shard finishes what the volley leaves. Reserves & disruption: Jellicent's Water Absorb, Will-O-Wisp and Recover is the attrition lane and the room's only recovery; Adaptability Basculin is raw immediate pressure with Head Smash as a deliberate all-in. Could this team be cooler? The Water spread and the multi-hit lesson are the right opening note for the gym, so the composition earns retention, but six Water bodies folded to a single Electric attacker; the one off-type slot goes to a second volley attacker rather than another Water-Ground, which the later rooms already cover with Quagsire, Gastrodon, Swampert, Seismitoad and Whiscash. Simipour is the one member worth defending explicitly - a Torrent monkey looks like filler, but it is the room's special answer to an opposing Grass lead, and removing it would make the room one-dimensional. Overrepresented? Cloyster, Floatzel, Simipour and Basculin appear only here; Jellicent and Mamoswine each have one distant other owner in different roles, and no other Sootopolis room runs Shell Smash as its headline - Olivia's Carracosta uses a Weakness Policy version with the opposite risk profile.
crack: Electric attacks no longer cover the room: Mamoswine is immune and hits back, so remove it first with Water, Grass, Fighting or Steel attacks - Thick Fat takes the sting out of Fire - and a smashed Cloyster's Drill Run punishes the Electric type too, so break its Sash with a multi-hit or spread move. Grass attacks still hit five of six. Cloyster is physically bulky and specially fragile, so answer it on the special side before it smashes; priority finishes Floatzel after Wave Crash recoil. Will-O-Wisp is the room's only real disruption, so a Fire type or a special attacker ignores it entirely.

## E0460 TRAINER_CRISSY class=gym
strategy: RAIN
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: room two - the squall room, where the rain is made by hand and three different bodies are built to live inside it. First two useful turns: Swanna sets Rain Dance when its own Weather Ball - which becomes a 100-power Water attack in rain - and Lanturn's pressure both profit; Hydration then cures Swanna's status at the end of every rainy turn, so status is not an answer to it. Lanturn attacks or Volt Switches into the right body. Turn two exploits the actual weather rather than assuming every Water member needs it. Alternate deployment: Alomomola's Regenerator bulk and Mantine-free support line covers Choice Specs Primarina, whose Liquid Voice turns Hyper Voice into a spread Water attack that hits both opposing slots - the room's biggest single click. Reserves & disruption: Wishiwashi is only dangerous in School Form, which requires it to be above one quarter HP at level 20 or higher, so damaging it below that threshold reverts it to a nearly harmless fish mid-fight; Golisopod's Emergency Exit force-switches it at half HP, which interacts badly with its own Choice Band lock and is a real, visible weakness. Could this team be cooler? The room's best feature is that three of its six change form or state mid-battle - Schooling, Emergency Exit and rain-dependent Weather Ball - so the board the player faces on turn five is not the one they planned for on turn one. That is worth keeping. The change made is a swap of Mantine for Alomomola: Mantine's Water Absorb duplicated Tiffany's entire room concept four doors later, whereas Alomomola's Regenerator and Wish support is the natural partner for a manual-rain team that needs to survive the turn it spends setting weather. Overrepresented? Alomomola, Swanna, Wishiwashi, Golisopod and Primarina are each unique in the campaign; Lanturn's other owner is the route sailor Hudson in a Volt Absorb role. Bridget's room uses automatic Drizzle instead, so the gym has one manual-rain room and one automatic one by design. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Grass and Electric attacks cut through everything. Knock Wishiwashi under a quarter HP and it stops being a threat; push Golisopod to half and it removes itself. Replace the weather and Swanna loses both its Weather Ball power and its Hydration cure. Wide Guard blanks Liquid Voice Hyper Voice.

## E0461 TRAINER_BRIANNA class=gym
strategy: TRICK_ROOM
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: room three - the deep, where the water stops moving. This is the gym's Trick Room room: six deliberately slow Water bodies, zero Speed IVs across the board, and a sea cucumber whose knockout costs the attacker as much as it cost Brianna. First two useful turns: Slowking sets Trick Room behind a Mental Herb that protects the turn from Taunt, while Relicanth attacks - Rock Head means Head Smash costs it nothing at all, which under reversed order is the hardest single hit in the gym. Turn two spends the room on damage instead of a second support action. Alternate deployment: Quagsire's Unaware ignores every stat boost the player has accumulated, so a Shell Smash or Dragon Dance sweeper walks straight into a wall that does not care; Yawn forces the player to switch or sleep. Reserves & disruption: Pyukumuku's Innards Out deals damage equal to the HP it had left to whatever knocks it out, so the player's strongest attacker pays for the knockout; Counter and Mirror Coat return double the damage of the hit it survives, and Helping Hand powers Relicanth or Crawdaunt when neither applies. Crawdaunt's Adaptability makes Crabhammer and Crunch hit twice as hard as a normal STAB would, Assault Vest covers its Special Defense, and Aqua Jet is the one move in the room that ignores the room. Toxapex closes: Wide Guard shuts out the player's spread attacks, Baneful Bunker poisons anything that punches it, and Regenerator heals it on every switch. Could this team be cooler? The room was already the gym's best-built Trick Room, but three of its six - a Ghost-type coral, a Dark/Poison Hisuian pufferfish and a pure Ice iceberg - were not Water types in the Water gym. Each slot keeps its job on a Water body: Pyukumuku replaces Cursola as the member the player must think twice about knocking out, Crawdaunt replaces Overqwil as the slow physical Dark attacker with Aqua Jet, and Toxapex replaces Avalugg as the Wide Guard, Recover and contact-punishing closer. Overrepresented? Cursola, Overqwil and Avalugg leave the gym; Pyukumuku, Crawdaunt and Toxapex appear in no other Sootopolis room. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Taunt the setter, or bring your own Trick Room and reverse the reversal. Finish Pyukumuku with a weak attack or a spread move you do not mind paying for, and never give it a big hit to Counter or Mirror Coat. Grass and Electric attacks answer most of the room, though Toxapex takes Grass neutrally and Quagsire is immune to Electric; Psychic and Ground hit Toxapex, Fighting hits Relicanth and Crawdaunt, and after five turns the room ends while every member is still slow.

## E0462 TRAINER_CONNIE class=gym
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND,ALLY_COMBO,REDIRECTION
mega_slots: NONE
tactic: ACTIVATE LUMINEON SURF GASTRODON
plan: Concept: room four - the redirection room, and the gym's cleverest single lesson. Every attack the room fears is pulled somewhere it cannot hurt: two Storm Drain bodies swallow Water, Seaking's Lightning Rod swallows Electric, and Magmar's Follow Me drags the Grass attack aimed at Gastrodon into a Fire type that shrugs it off. First two useful turns: the authored tactic fires Lumineon's Surf into the board, hitting both opponents while Gastrodon absorbs the friendly half and raises its own Special Attack by one stage; Gastrodon then attacks with a boosted Earth Power or recovers according to the resulting state. Turn two picks the best real target rather than repeating the mutual boost forever. Alternate deployment: against a Grass lead, Magmar is the replacement that changes the board. Follow Me pulls every single-target attack onto it, so a Grass hit meant for Gastrodon, which takes four times damage from Grass, lands on a Fire type that halves it; Follow Me is not a powder, so Grass types cannot ignore it the way they ignore Rage Powder. Flame Body can burn the physical Grass attacker that makes contact, Heat Wave hits both foes and a Grass/Steel attacker for four times damage, and Will-O-Wisp cripples the physical attacker Heat Wave cannot finish. Magmar's Pokedex says its heat waves ignite grass and trees, and Sootopolis is a volcano crater that filled with rain, so the one fire in the Water gym has a home in it. Follow Me also outranks Storm Drain and Lightning Rod while it lasts, so Magmar calls attention only when Grass is the threat, and it stays out of the Surf lane because Surf hits it for double. Seaking's Choice Band physical line and Octillery's Sniper special coverage are the mode to use when Storm Drain would get in the team's own way. Reserves & disruption: this is the one room in the gym where allied Storm Drain is an asset rather than a hazard, which is why Huntail's set carries no single-target Water attack at all - Shell Smash into Double-Edge, Crunch and Ice Fang keeps it entirely out of the redirection matrix, and the Double-Edge recoil is the price of staying off the Water lane. Could this team be cooler? The Storm Drain concept is one of the best ideas in the gym and stays exactly as it is, but a Grass pair swept the room in four turns: both Storm Drain bodies took double or quadruple damage from Grass and nothing on the team resisted it. The fix follows the room's own logic. Starmie, whose Hydro Pump and Thunderbolt were themselves being pulled into its allies' Storm Drain and Lightning Rod, gives its slot to the redirector that completes the set - Water, Electric and now Grass each have somewhere to go. Overrepresented? Seaking's Lightning Rod is unique to this room; Lumineon's other Storm Drain is a Mt. Pyre grunt's, and Gastrodon's three other owners are all Storm Drain sets away from Sootopolis; Starmie leaves the room and keeps five other owners; Magmar's only other owner is Axle's physical Vital Spirit set, and the Follow Me and Flame Body support idea last appeared on Dwayne's Magby at the start of the game, so this is its evolved echo.
crack: Grass attacks still hit both Storm Drain bodies hard, but while Magmar is calling them they land on Magmar instead: remove it first with Water, Ground or Rock attacks, beat Follow Me with Fake Out or Taunt, or aim a Water attack at the lead pair while Magmar is calling, because Follow Me outranks Storm Drain and sends it into the Fire type for double damage. Wide Guard blanks Surf entirely, which shuts the engine off in one move. Taunt stops Tailwind, Recover and Follow Me; exploit Seaking's Choice lock; Fire types ignore Will-O-Wisp and Heat Wave.

## E0463 TRAINER_BRIDGET class=gym
strategy: RAIN,SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: room five - the fossil reef. Automatic Drizzle plus a wall of prehistoric shells: three Swift Swim fossils that double their Speed the instant the rain lands, and a Mega Swampert to close. First two useful turns: Pelipper's Drizzle is live on entry, so Omastar is already fast; it opens with Tailwind, a U-turn pivot, or spread Muddy Water, and Omastar either attacks or takes a Shell Smash that White Herb immediately repairs. Turn two uses the real combined Swift Swim and Shell Smash speed rather than stacking further setup with no payoff. Alternate deployment: Pelipper and Mega Swampert are the physical rain mode once the transformation grants its actual post-Mega ability - base Swampert has Torrent and none of that Speed, so the order of operations matters. Reserves & disruption: Armaldo's Swift Swim is the room's surprise, a Rock/Bug fossil nobody expects to outrun them in rain; Drednaw's Strong Jaw powers Crunch and Liquidation specifically; Kabutops supplies Aqua Jet priority for when the rain is finally taken away. Could this team be cooler? The Drizzle-plus-Swift-Swim concept was already right for a Sootopolis room, and the honest judgement is that it needed sharpening rather than replacing. Armaldo is the concrete improvement: swapping the duplicated Ludicolo for a rarely used fossil with Swift Swim makes the room read as a single coherent idea - every non-Mega member is a fossil or a seabird - and gives the player a genuine surprise, because an Armaldo that moves first is not something the campaign has taught them to expect. That also frees Ludicolo to belong solely to Shelly's Aqua crew. Overrepresented? Armaldo, Omastar, Kabutops and Drednaw are unique in the campaign after this pass; Pelipper's other owner is Archie, whose Damp Rock storm serves an entirely different roster; Mega Swampert appears only here. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Replace the weather and four members lose half their Speed at once. Grass attacks are devastating against the entire room; Electric answers Pelipper and Omastar. Wide Guard blanks Muddy Water, Taunt stops Tailwind and Shell Smash, and priority catches Omastar before White Herb ever matters.

## E0464 TRAINER_OLIVIA class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: room six - the artillery room. Mega Launcher pulses, a Water Bubble spider that doubles its own Water damage, a cormorant that spits its catch back at whoever hit it, and a clay guardian that fires itself at the Electric attacker the rest of the room fears. First two useful turns: Carracosta takes a Shell Smash only when Araquanid's attack or Wide Guard buys a survivable window; unlike every other Shell Smash in the gym this one holds a Weakness Policy rather than a White Herb, so the Defence drops stay and the gamble is real - the payoff is that a super-effective hit raises both attacking stats by two more stages on top, and its Earthquake is aimed squarely at the Electric attacker that just triggered it. Araquanid's Wide Guard shelters its own side from that quake, so the lead pair can spread-attack without friendly fire. Turn two uses the actual boosts and Policy state. Alternate deployment: against an Electric lead, Golurk is the replacement that changes the board. Ground/Ghost typing makes it immune to Electric attacks, to Fighting attacks and to Fake Out, so an Electric/Fighting attacker has almost nothing left to hit it with and an Electric/Dragon one is down to its Dragon moves; Iron Fist powers Headlong Rush, a 120-power Ground punch that hits Electric types for double at the cost of a Defence and Special Defence stage each time, Ice Punch covers the Dragons and Flying types that dodge Ground, and Drain Punch buys back what Headlong Rush costs. Golurk were built by an ancient civilisation to protect people, and Sootopolis is the old city built around the Cave of Origin. Carracosta's Earthquake hits Golurk too, so the two share the field only behind Araquanid's Wide Guard. Golurk fronts for Choice Specs Clawitzer, whose Mega Launcher raises every pulse move by half again. Reserves & disruption: Cramorant is the room's signature - after it uses Surf it holds a catch, and the next attacker to strike it takes damage and either a Defence drop or paralysis in return, whichever form it swallowed. Relicanth-free Rock coverage comes from Carracosta's Stone Edge. Surf also hits the ally slot, which is why Cramorant is paired with the Water-resistant members rather than the frail ones or the Water-weak Golurk. Could this team be cooler? The Policy gamble, the pulse artillery and Gulp Missile were already the room people describe afterwards; what failed was the matchup a Water gym most expects. Five of six members took double damage or more from Electric, Cramorant four times, and an Electric/Dragon beside an Electric/Fighting swept the room in five turns because Carracosta's Earthquake was its only Ground attack. Tentacruel's Liquid Ooze was the narrowest job in the room, so its slot goes to the one member that Electric plan cannot touch. Overrepresented? Tentacruel leaves the room and keeps a Weather Institute grunt and Ronald; Golurk's three other owners - Lucas's No Guard set, Drew's Life Orb punches and Tabitha's Mega - all come well before the Aqua arc ends, and none carries Headlong Rush or an Assault Vest.
crack: Electric attacks no longer cover the room: Golurk is immune and hits back, so remove it first with Water, Grass, Ice, Ghost or Dark attacks - every Headlong Rush it lands lowers its own defences, and the Assault Vest leaves it no Protect. Grass attacks still cover the five Water types, though Araquanid's Leech Life and Cramorant's Hurricane and Ice Beam hit back. Attack Carracosta with a neutral hit so the Weakness Policy never fires, and finish it with priority once the Defence drops land. Do not strike Cramorant after it has surfaced; kill it from range or let it waste the catch. Wide Guard blanks Surf and Earthquake.

## E0465 TRAINER_TIFFANY class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: room seven - the tide pool, built entirely on absorption. Three of the six are immune to Water attacks, so Vaporeon's spread Surf is a full-power attack on both opposing slots that heals its own partner instead of hurting it, and the one outsider absorbs the attack the rest of the pool is weakest to. First two useful turns: Vaporeon Surfs while a Water Absorb partner stands beside it, gaining a free quarter of HP for that ally; Seismitoad or Mantine attacks in the other slot. Turn two sets Wish only when a real recipient will still be alive at the end of the following turn. Alternate deployment: against a Grass lead, Hisuian Goodra is the replacement that changes the board. Sap Sipper makes it immune to Grass attacks and raises its Attack by one stage each time one is aimed at it, and Steel/Dragon typing resists Steel as well, so a Grass/Steel attacker is left with only its Fighting moves; Fire Punch hits Grass types for double and Grass/Steel types for four times, Heavy Slam is its Steel attack, and Breaking Swipe hits both foes and lowers their Attack, which blunts a physical Grass pair before it reaches Seismitoad, whose Ground half makes Grass four times as effective. Vaporeon's Surf does only half damage to Goodra's Dragon half, so the absorption pair can bring it in beside them at a small cost. Vaporeon's support also enables Barbaracle's White Herb Shell Smash, but Surf is genuinely unsafe beside Barbaracle, so the AI must switch lanes rather than blindly repeat the engine - the lead pair and the setup pair are two separate modes. Reserves & disruption: Feraligatr's Sheer Force Dragon Dance is the non-absorbing finisher; Tough Claws boosts every contact move Barbaracle has, and Sheer Force removes Life Orb recoil on Feraligatr's Liquidation, Ice Punch and Crunch. Could this team be cooler? The Water Absorb Surf engine is a clean, teachable idea and the right seventh room, but a Grass/Steel attacker hit Seismitoad and Whiscash for four times damage and its Beast Boost carried it through the rest. Whiscash was also the room's least distinctive member - an Assault Vest Oblivious set an Aqua Hideout grunt already carries - so its slot goes to a member that extends the absorption idea instead of breaking it: the tide pool drinks Water, and its one outsider drinks Grass. Overrepresented? Hisuian Goodra appears nowhere else in the campaign, and base Goodra's three owners are special ace-trainer sets; Whiscash leaves the room; Vaporeon's other owners include Hope in Victory Road with a Helping Hand support set; Seismitoad, Mantine, Barbaracle and Feraligatr each have other owners, none in the Sootopolis gym.
crack: Grass attacks still hit Seismitoad four times over and the other Water types twice, but not Hisuian Goodra, which is immune to them and grows stronger: remove it first with Fighting or Ground attacks, its only weaknesses, and lean on special attacks while Breaking Swipe is lowering your Attack. Electric answers Vaporeon and Mantine. Taunt stops Wish and Shell Smash. Attack Barbaracle or Feraligatr and the engine's own Surf becomes a liability the AI has to route around.

## E0466 TRAINER_BETHANY class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: room eight - the strike team. This is the gym's speed-and-priority room: everything here either moves first or hits before the player can react. First two useful turns: Palafin leads and uses Flip Turn or a hard switch only when a healthy receiver exists, because leaving the field transforms it into Hero Form - on its second entry it is a different, far larger Pokemon with a Jet Punch that outspeeds everything. Barraskewda attacks beside it with Propeller Tail, which cannot be pulled away by Follow Me, Rage Powder or Storm Drain. Turn two commits to whichever of the two transformations or boosts actually landed. Alternate deployment: Azumarill's Belly Drum is admitted only after a survival check - it costs half its HP for a maximised Attack that Huge Power then doubles again, making Aqua Jet a priority attack with no realistic counterplay except killing it first. Reserves & disruption: Greninja's Protean changes its type to match each move as it uses it, so it is never the type the player prepared for; Sniper Inteleon converts its critical hits into removals; Mega Kingler is the closer, with Crabhammer's high critical rate behind it and High Horsepower as the room's single answer to the Electric type that otherwise out-trades six Water bodies. Palafin's late placement must not strand an untransformed Hero with no reserve left to pivot into. Could this team be cooler? The Zero to Hero idea is the best single mechanic in the gym and stays as the room's spine, but the authored roster buried it: Dhelmise and Crawdaunt were slow bodies in a room about speed, and Mega Sharpedo made this the campaign's third owner of that exact stone after Archie and the Elite Four's Dark specialist. Rebuilding around Barraskewda, Greninja and Inteleon makes every slot serve the same idea, and Mega Kingler gives the room a Mega nobody else in the campaign fields. The memorable moment is the pivot itself - the player kills a modest dolphin and then meets Hero Form two turns later. Overrepresented? Mega Kingler, Palafin and Inteleon appear only here; Greninja's other owner is Felix's Mega version deep in Victory Road; Barraskewda's other owner is a Seafloor grunt with a Focus Sash; Azumarill's remaining owners are the route trainer Kara and nobody else, since Wally's Victory Road team is rebuilt to his canonical partners.
crack: Kill Palafin before it ever leaves the field and Hero Form never happens. Electric and Grass attacks answer the room, but keep the Electric type away from Mega Kingler's High Horsepower; priority of your own beats Jet Punch's users after Belly Drum. Fake Out and Intimidate both blunt the opening, and Inteleon and Greninja fold to any neutral hit.

## E0467 TRAINER_ANNIKA class=gym
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: room nine - the still room. Annika is the gym's contrarian: her Golduck's Cloud Nine switches off every weather effect in the building, including her own colleagues' rain, and the rest of her team is built to win without any. First two useful turns: Golduck attacks or takes a Nasty Plot while Quaquaval opens with Aqua Step, which raises its own Speed on every use and stacks with Moxie's Attack boost after each knockout - a self-accelerating sweeper that needs no weather at all. Turn two uses the real boosts rather than adding a third. Alternate deployment: Empoleon's Competitive doubles its Special Attack whenever any stat of its is lowered, so an opposing Intimidate, Snarl or Icy Wind hands it a free Nasty Plot; Assault Vest bulk lets it stay in for that. Reserves & disruption: Milotic's Flame Orb self-burn activates Marvel Scale for a 50% Defence boost at the cost of residual damage, and Hisuian Samurott's Sharpness sharply boosts its slicing moves while Ceaseless Edge lays Spikes on every hit; its Choice Band Drill Run is the room's answer to the Electric type that would otherwise sweep it, and Sucker Punch keeps the Dark coverage Knock Off used to add. Basculegion is the Adaptability special closer. Note that Aqua Step is a dance, so an opposing Dancer copies it. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and it needed the change more than any other room except Brianna's. Pyukumuku's Counter-and-Toxic opening was pure passivity in a gym that is supposed to be eleven consecutive benchmarks, and the block's own prose flagged it for anti-passivity testing. Replacing it with Basculegion gives the room a real sixth threat, a Water/Ghost typing nothing else in the gym has, and an immunity to the Fighting and Normal attacks the player brings for Empoleon and Milotic. What remains is the room's genuinely distinctive idea - three different self-boosting mechanisms (Aqua Step, Moxie, Competitive) that all reward the player for attacking, so patience is punished and impatience is punished differently. Overrepresented? Quaquaval, Empoleon, Hisuian Samurott and Basculegion are unique in the campaign; Milotic's other owner is Champion Wallace's Mega; Golduck's Cloud Nine echoes the route sailor Hudson deliberately, as the player-side lesson resurfacing as an opponent.
crack: Do not lower Empoleon's stats. Electric and Grass attacks answer most of the room, but a Band-locked Samurott's Drill Run punishes the Electric type - lure it into Ceaseless Edge first or remove it; Dark and Ghost hit Basculegion. Haze erases Aqua Step and Moxie together, Taunt stops Nasty Plot, and Milotic's burn is doing steady damage to itself all fight.

## E0468 TRAINER_DAPHNE class=gym
strategy: SNOW
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: room ten - the freeze. The last room before Juan is the one that changes the weather itself: Lapras lays down snow by hand, which raises every Ice type's Defence by half, and the room's Ice Body members then heal from the same storm every turn. First two useful turns: Lapras opens with Snowscape when the team can bank the Defence boost and the passive healing, or with Freeze-Dry, which is the room's signature - an Ice attack that is super effective against Water, so this room beats the rest of its own gym. Walrein attacks beside it with Thick Fat cutting incoming Fire and Ice damage in half. Turn two uses the real weather state and picks between Blizzard's now-perfect accuracy and single-target damage. Alternate deployment: Cryogonal's Levitate makes it immune to Ground attacks, and its Icy Wind and Rapid Spin support Samurott's physical line. Reserves & disruption: snow makes Blizzard never miss, so Walrein's Choice Specs lock is far less dangerous than it looks once the weather is up; Rotom-Wash supplies the Electric coverage the room otherwise lacks and Levitate keeps it out of Ground range; Fur Coat halves physical damage against Dewgong. Could this team be cooler? It is now the room the gym was missing. The authored version had no engine at all - four unrelated Water types plus a Tapu Fini that duplicated Champion Wallace's own legend, which is exactly the kind of collision that makes a later fight feel less special. Rebuilding Daphne as the snow room gives Sootopolis its eleventh distinct strategy, hands Freeze-Dry to the one trainer for whom being a Water gym's worst matchup is the joke, and lets Walrein's Blizzard finally be accurate somewhere. Cryogonal is the rare, genuinely strange body the room deserves: a floating ice crystal with enormous Special Defence and no Ground weakness. Overrepresented? Cryogonal, Walrein and Dewgong appear only here in the gym; Lapras's other owners are the route trainer Everett's Perish Song set and Micah's Mega far into the post-game; Rotom-Wash recurs with Keira after the League. Tapu Fini is returned to Champion Wallace alone. Levels: cap 84, offsets 2/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Replace the weather and the Defence boost, the healing and Blizzard's accuracy all go at once. Fire, Fighting, Rock and Steel attacks cover the Ice core; Samurott and Rotom-Wash are the only members that are not weak to Fighting. Taunt stops Snowscape and Rapid Spin, and Walrein is locked into whatever it picked.

## E0469 TRAINER_JUAN_1 class=leader
strategy: RAIN,SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the eleventh door - Juan takes the same rain his gym has been teaching and uses it for attrition rather than burst, then ends the fight with a Mega. Everything the ten rooms showed the player in isolation arrives here at once. First two useful turns: Politoed's Drizzle is live on entry with a Damp Rock behind it and opens with Encore to lock the player into a support move they have just wasted, or spread Muddy Water; Suicune picks Tailwind, Snarl or immediate pressure according to the board. Turn two must introduce a real attacker if the opening pair is being out-paced - Juan's failure mode is passivity and the plan explicitly forbids it. Alternate deployment: Suicune's control supports Manaphy's Tail Glow, which raises Special Attack by three stages in one turn and, under Hydration, is protected from any status the player tries to interrupt it with while the rain lasts. Reserves & disruption: Altaria is the answer to a weather Juan cannot win. Primal Groudon's Desolate Land makes every Water move fail and no rain can replace it, but Cloud Nine switches every weather effect off while Altaria stands, so Juan's Water attacks work again and hit Primal Groudon four times over; the same Cloud Nine turns a Mega Charizard Y's sun into nothing. Dragon/Flying is immune to Precipice Blades and resists Fire, Grass, Water and Fighting, Flamethrower punishes the Grass types a Water gym invites, Ice Beam answers the Dragons and Ground types, and Will-O-Wisp cripples the physical Electric and Grass answers (a Primal Groudon is Fire and cannot be burned). It costs Juan his own rain while it stands, so it is a reserve, not a lead: Sootopolis has just watched Rayquaza calm the sky, and Juan keeps the same trick in miniature. Choice Specs Swift Swim Kingdra is the burst reserve, doubling its Speed in the rain it did not have to set, and Mega Gyarados is the closer with Intimidate deliberately spent before transformation. Counterplay & audit: the pair AI does not treat Cloud Nine as a plan-changing pivot, so Altaria arrives on a replacement rather than a voluntary switch. Could this team be cooler? The rain plan earned retention, but it had no answer to the one weather the player can bring after Sootopolis: a Primal Groudon blanked four members' main attacks and the player won losing one. Toxapex was the wall that could not help - Ground-weak into Precipice Blades and a repeat of the Seafloor grunt's Toxapex a dozen fights earlier. It is cooler now because the counter to the counter is on the bench, and it is not Annika's Golduck: hers switches the weather off for a whole room built to live without it, while Juan's is a Dragon that turns his own team back on. Overrepresented? Politoed's Drizzle is shared with Shelly by design and with nobody else; Suicune, Manaphy and Mega Gyarados are unique to this fight; Kingdra's other owners are two route trainers at far lower tiers; Altaria's other owners include Wally seven fights later, with a Natural Cure Tailwind set and later a Mega; this one shares only Flamethrower with his.
crack: Take the rain and keep taking it - three members lose their engine - but a Primal's weather stops mattering once Altaria is in, so remove Altaria first: Ice (4x), Rock, Dragon and Fairy. Grass and Electric attacks hit almost the rest of the roster; Taunt stops Encore, Tailwind, Tail Glow and Will-O-Wisp. Haze erases Tail Glow and Dragon Dance together, and priority answers Gyarados after it boosts.

## E0472 TRAINER_ALEXIS class=regular
strategy: REDIRECTION
mega_slots: 4
plan: Concept: a psychic quartet that turns its own terrain into a damage multiplier - Expanding Force becomes a spread attack with half again the power while Psychic Terrain is up, so three of the four are firing a boosted two-target move. First two useful turns: Indeedee-F establishes Psychic Surge on entry and redirects with Follow Me only when it preserves a worthwhile Expanding Force; Hatterene attacks. Turn two checks actual groundedness - the terrain and its priority-blocking effect only apply to grounded targets - and whether the terrain is still the team's own. Alternate deployment: Indeedee-F supports Reuniclus's Magic Guard coverage when Hatterene's typing is unfavourable; Magic Guard means Reuniclus takes no damage from Life Orb, burn, poison, hazards or sandstorm. Reserves & disruption: Hatterene's Magic Bounce reflects Taunt, Thunder Wave, Leech Seed and hazards straight back at the sender, so status is not the answer here. Mega Meowstic's screens and its form's ability transition are separate matters; base Prankster gives its screens priority but never makes Expanding Force a priority attack. Could this team be cooler? The terrain-plus-Expanding-Force stack is a real doubles engine and Magic Bounce is a genuine puzzle for a player who has been solving fights with Taunt, so this earns retention as a route regular. The block also carried a live legality error - Mega Meowstic was given Expanding Force, which it cannot learn - so its fourth slot becomes ordinary Psychic, which is honest and still benefits from the terrain. What would make it cooler is deliberately withheld: a Trick Room would turn it into a third room-team in a stretch that already has three, and the psychic-terrain identity is cleaner without one. Overrepresented? Indeedee-F recurs only with Wally's post-game rematch; Hatterene, Reuniclus and Mega Meowstic appear once each; Alexis is the only Psychic Terrain owner before the League. Levels: cap 90, offsets 0/1/1/2.
crack: Replace the terrain and three attacks lose a third of their power. Dark and Steel attacks answer the whole team and Magic Bounce does nothing against damage. Wide Guard blanks Expanding Force and Dazzling Gleam; attack around the redirection with spread moves; and Meowstic's screens fall to Brick Break or Defog.

## E0473 TRAINER_MARIELA class=casual
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: 2
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: a collector's parlour team - four famous faces, one of them wearing a Mega stone nobody expects to exist. First two useful turns: Starmie attacks with Natural Cure keeping it clean of status between switches while Chimecho chooses Helping Hand at a real threshold or its own Hyper Voice. Turn two uses the actual post-transformation ability rather than the base one. Alternate deployment: Pikachu's Fake Out buys Alakazam a turn of immediate Psychic pressure; Magic Guard means Alakazam ignores all indirect damage, so hazards and residual chip never break its Focus Sash. Reserves & disruption: the Light Ball doubles Pikachu's Attack and Special Attack for its attacks and does nothing for Fake Out's utility or its support turns; Encore requires observed move history to be worth the action. Overrepresented? Mega Chimecho and Light Ball Pikachu are unique in the campaign; Starmie's other owners are Connie's gym room and Champion Wallace's Mega; Alakazam appears once elsewhere. Levels: cap 90, offsets 0/0/0/1.
crack: Dark, Ghost, Ground and Steel attacks cover the roster; every member here is frail. Break Alakazam's Sash with any two hits, deny Fake Out with your own, and Chimecho's support turns are wasted once its partner is gone.

## E0475 TRAINER_EVERETT class=regular
strategy: PERISH_TRAP
mega_slots: NONE
plan: Concept: the campaign's perish-trap prototype - Wobbuffet's Shadow Tag forbids the player from switching, Lapras starts a three-turn countdown, and both sides watch the clock. First two useful turns: Lapras uses Perish Song when Wobbuffet can hold the trap and both friendly slots still have a legal exit of their own; Wobbuffet Encores a committed move or waits with Counter and Mirror Coat, which return double the physical or special damage it just took. Turn two plays the countdown honestly - the trapper must leave or deliberately trade before it reaches zero, and switching the trapper releases the opponents early. Alternate deployment: Beheeyem and Musharna are the slower control fallback when the trap is answered - Analytic adds 30% to Beheeyem's damage whenever it moves last, which a Trick Room makes reliable. Reserves & disruption: Life Dew heals both allies, Safeguard blocks the status a trapped player reaches for, and Forewarn tells Musharna the strongest move on the opposing side. Wobbuffet is deliberately given no Protect - the script must not be made easy. Could this team be cooler? The perish trap is the rarest strategy in the campaign and appears exactly once, which is the right frequency; a second owner would make it a gimmick rather than an event. The block carried one legality error - Beheeyem cannot learn Helping Hand - so it becomes Energy Ball, which also gives the control half a real answer to the Ground and Water types that otherwise wall it. The honest limitation stays recorded: countdown ordering, simultaneous faints and escape sequencing are native acceptance gates, not assumptions. Overrepresented? Wobbuffet, Beheeyem and Musharna's Forewarn set are unique; Lapras's other owners are Daphne's gym ice room and Micah's post-game Mega. Levels: cap 90, offsets 0/1/1/1.
crack: Ghost types ignore Shadow Tag entirely, and so do Shed Shell and U-turn. Soundproof blocks Perish Song. Kill Wobbuffet with a spread move so Counter and Mirror Coat cannot answer, or simply out-damage the clock - three turns is not long, and Lapras dies to Electric and Grass attacks.
