## E0476 TRAINER_WALLY_VR_1 class=rival
strategy: TAILWIND,MEGA_REVEAL
mega_slots: 2
plan: Concept: the rival arc, part one, and the last rival fight before the League. These are the partners Wally has raised since Petalburg, built this time to beat the Fire-and-Steel team that is the obvious way through a Psychic/Fairy/Grass core. First two useful turns: Ludicolo and Gallade lead, and MEGA_REVEAL evolves Gallade at the start of turn one. Mega Gallade's ability is Inner Focus, not Sharpness, and it applies before anyone moves: an opposing Fake Out cannot flinch it and Intimidate cannot lower its Attack, so the two tools an Incineroar-style Fire lead depends on do nothing to Wally's ace. Ludicolo uses Fake Out on the foe whose attack matters most that turn, usually the spread Fire user or whichever foe threatens Gallade. Meanwhile Gallade's Sacred Sword ignores Defense boosts and hits Fire, Steel, Dark and Rock super effectively. On turn two Ludicolo's Muddy Water hits both foes, and Water is super effective on Fire, Rock and Ground, so a Fire pair takes it on both sides at once. Assault Vest and Water typing let Ludicolo absorb Heat Wave, and Own Tempo ignores Intimidate. Ice Beam covers Ground, Dragon and Flying; Giga Drain covers Water, Rock and Ground and heals Ludicolo. Knock Off takes the item from a Ghost or Psychic target, and Psycho Cut is Gallade's STAB answer to Fighting and Poison. Alternate deployment: Altaria resists Fire, Water, Grass and Electric; it sets Tailwind, uses Roost to stay healthy, Dragon Pulse on Dragons and Flamethrower on Steel and Grass. Magnezone's Magnet Pull traps Steel types, including a Fire/Steel Mega Heatran, so they cannot switch away from Sacred Sword, Muddy Water or Magnezone's own Body Press. Body Press is calculated from Magnezone's Defense and hits Steel, Rock, Dark, Ice and Normal super effectively. Air Balloon is the Mauville Magneton's balloon: it keeps Magnezone off the ground until the first hit pops it. Reserves & disruption: when Gardevoir enters, Trace copies an ability from the opposing side; if it copies Heatran's Flash Fire, Gardevoir becomes immune to Fire. Moonblast covers Dragon, Dark and Fighting; Mystical Fire covers Steel, Grass and Bug and lowers the target's Special Attack; Focus Blast is Gardevoir's answer to Fire/Steel and Rock/Dark. Eviolite Roselia lands Sleep Powder, and Natural Cure clears its own status when it switches out. Never fire Mystical Fire or Flamethrower into Flash Fire, never aim Sleep Powder at a Grass type, and do not keep Magnezone in to trap a Steel type the side cannot damage. Could this team be cooler? Yes. The previous version answered its own counter with nothing. Delcatty spent the fight on Helping Hand and Heal Bell. Gardevoir's Psychic hit almost nothing on a Steel and Dark team. The Mega sat in the last slot behind Ace Pokemon and arrived only after spread Fire had removed the Fire-weak Grass and Steel members, so a Fire-and-Steel team won on attempt one with four standing. Now the Mega leads and ignores the Fake Out and Intimidate a Fire lead relies on. The Fake Out comes from a Water type that punishes the Fire lead with a spread move, and the trapper keeps a Fire/Steel attacker on the field for Gallade's Sacred Sword. The new member is canon-adjacent: Lotad shares the Route 102 grass where Wally caught Ralts, Ludicolo is fished from that same pond, and Lotad swims the river at Verdanturf, Wally's new home. Overrepresented? Ludicolo's most recent owner is Shelly at Seafloor Cavern, twenty-two encounters earlier, and none of its five other trainers uses Assault Vest or Muddy Water. Juan's Altaria is a Cloud Nine attacker; Wally's is a Tailwind support. Hannah's Mega Gallade is a Leaf Blade attacker, and Roselia belongs only to Wally and Daisy. In the post-League rematch Gallade gives the Mega to Altaria, so the two fights mirror each other.
crack: On turn one, Protect whichever foe Sacred Sword is likely to hit. Alternatively, lead a Ghost, Flying or Fairy attacker faster than Mega Gallade's base 110 Speed; Gallade has only 68 base HP and the whole plan runs through it. Fake Out and Intimidate still work on Ludicolo, not Gallade. Ludicolo is weak to Flying, Poison and Bug, and Assault Vest means it cannot Protect. Wide Guard blocks Muddy Water, and Water Absorb, Storm Drain and Dry Skin absorb it. Fire still hits Magnezone and Roselia super effectively. Any hit pops Magnezone's Air Balloon, after which Ground does four times damage; Fighting is also super effective. Taunt stops Tailwind and Roost, and Ice, Rock, Dragon and Fairy attacks hit Altaria super effectively. Steel and Poison attacks answer Gardevoir, and Focus Blast misses 30% of the time. Grass types, Overcoat and Safety Goggles ignore Sleep Powder.

## E0477 TRAINER_EDGAR class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: the first door of Victory Road - a mechanical gauntlet where three separate members set up in three incompatible ways and the player cannot answer all of them. First two useful turns: Trevenant's Will-O-Wisp cripples a physical lead while Sudowoodo's Rock Head makes Head Smash entirely recoil-free, which is the hardest opening hit in this stretch; Natural Cure keeps Trevenant clean across switches. Turn two answers whichever setup the player failed to stop. Alternate deployment: Venomoth's Tinted Lens makes even resisted Bug Buzz and Sludge Bomb land at neutral damage, so a Quiver Dance behind a Focus Sash is a genuine sweep threat; Klinklang's Shift Gear doubles its Speed and raises Attack, and Clear Body refuses every stat drop the player aims at it. Reserves & disruption: Choice Scarf Manectric with Lightning Rod is the emergency speed control and Electric absorber; Mega Hawlucha's Mold Breaker ignores abilities entirely, so Sturdy, Levitate and Water Absorb do not save anything from Close Combat or Brave Bird. Could this team be cooler? The three-setup structure is the right identity for the gauntlet's opening gate, and the Mold Breaker Mega is a genuinely good closer because it invalidates exactly the defensive abilities a player leans on after eleven Water rooms. It earns retention. The improvement it needed was tier: authored at flat offsets it read as a route ace that had wandered into Victory Road, so the whole team rises and Mega Hawlucha rises further. Tinted Lens Venomoth is the member worth defending - it is the only thing in the range that makes resistances irrelevant, and that is a better kind of pressure than raw stats. Overrepresented? Trevenant, Sudowoodo, Venomoth, Klinklang and Mega Hawlucha are unique in the campaign; Manectric's other owner is the post-game ship trainer Micah, whose duplicate Scarf set is replaced in this pass. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Fire attacks answer Trevenant, Venomoth and Klinklang; Ground and Water answer Sudowoodo and Manectric; Flying and Psychic answer Hawlucha. Taunt stops Will-O-Wisp, Quiver Dance and Shift Gear. Break Venomoth's Sash before it dances, and exploit the Choice lock on Manectric.

## E0478 TRAINER_ALBERT class=ace
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: the control gate - no weather, no terrain, just a Prankster that decides the turn order and five very heavy bodies behind it. First two useful turns: Sableye's priority Fake Out, Quash or Encore rearranges the turn before it happens - Quash in particular forces a fast opposing attacker to move last, which is what makes the slow half of this team work. Rhyperior attacks, holding a Weakness Policy behind Solid Rock, which reduces super-effective damage by a quarter and therefore makes surviving the hit that triggers the Policy far likelier. Turn two cashes whichever advantage the control turn bought. Alternate deployment: Weavile's Pressure and Knock Off strip items while Guzzlord's Beast Boost turns each knockout into more Attack; Guzzlord's absurd HP makes it the one thing here that survives a full-power special attack. Reserves & disruption: Ursaluna's Flame Orb Guts converts a burn into a damage bonus and Headlong Rush is the single heaviest Ground attack in the range; Mega Glimmora's Toxic Debris scatters Toxic Spikes whenever it is hit by a physical attack, so attacking it physically poisons the player's own grounded team. Could this team be cooler? The Quash-plus-Solid-Rock-Policy interaction is a real, unusual plan and Mega Glimmora is a genuinely memorable Mega, so the concept holds. The change made is to Conkeldurr, which was an exact duplicate of Mitchell's set seven doors later in the same gauntlet: Ursaluna is rarer, hits harder, fits the same Guts-and-Flame-Orb role, and gives the gauntlet a Ground attacker that Trick Room teams later in the road can be compared against. That turns a repeated slot into the fight's second-scariest body. Overrepresented? Ursaluna, Guzzlord, Rhyperior's Policy set and Mega Glimmora are unique; Weavile's other owner is the post-game ship trainer Thomas with a zero-Attack activation set; Sableye recurs with Vincent after the League, whose duplicate is replaced with Klefki in this pass. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Fighting attacks answer four of the six. Do not attack Glimmora physically. Break Sableye first - without the Prankster control the slow bodies simply move last. Deny the Weakness Policy with neutral damage, and remove the Flame Orb before Ursaluna's Guts activates.

## E0479 TRAINER_HOPE class=ace
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a support-and-snipe team where the entire roster exists to give one small fish a free Nasty Plot. First two useful turns: Forretress sets Stealth Rock behind Sturdy or pivots with Volt Switch while Lilligant lands Sleep Powder or a Leaf Storm from a Focus Sash. Turn two brings the member that punishes whatever the player committed to. Alternate deployment: Salazzle's Corrosion lets it poison Steel and Poison types that believe they are immune, which is a specific answer to the Steel walls this team otherwise cannot break; Aerodactyl's Tailwind and Taunt handle the speed race. Reserves & disruption: Vaporeon's Muddy Water is spread, so it cannot be stolen by its own Storm Drain ace, and Helping Hand is its real job; Mega Tatsugiri's Storm Drain draws every opposing single-target Water attack and converts it into a Special Attack boost, which is the team's signature - a player who leads with Water hands the sweeper a free stage before it even acts. Could this team be cooler? The shape is right and unusual: five support-shaped bodies and one tiny, absurd Mega sushi dragon that ends the game. It earns retention. The block's one real risk is that the Storm Drain bait only works if the player brings Water, so the redundancy matters - Nasty Plot means Tatsugiri can boost itself when nobody obliges. The improvement made is tier and pacing: at flat offsets the support turns were free for the player, so everything rises and the Mega rises further, making the two-turn setup an actual race. Overrepresented? Mega Tatsugiri, Forretress and Salazzle's Corrosion set are unique in the gauntlet; Lilligant recurs with Darcy's sun team at a lower tier with a different ability; Aerodactyl's Taunt set is shared only with Jonathan far earlier; Vaporeon's other owner is Tiffany's absorption room. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Do not use single-target Water attacks. Fire answers Forretress, Lilligant and Salazzle at once; Rock answers Aerodactyl and Tatsugiri; Dragon and Fairy answer the ace. Taunt stops Stealth Rock, Tailwind, Sleep Powder and Nasty Plot, and Safety Goggles ignores the powder entirely.

## E0480 TRAINER_QUINCY class=ace
strategy: REDIRECTION,TAILWIND
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Ace Pokemon
tactic: SUPPRESS WEEZING NONE SLAKING
plan: Concept: the cleverest single idea in Victory Road - a team built entirely around turning off the worst abilities in the game. Slaking has Truant and can only attack every other turn; Weezing's Neutralizing Gas suppresses every ability on the field while it is alive, Truant included, so a Choice Scarf Slaking with 160 base Attack simply attacks every turn instead. The authored SUPPRESS tactic is what tells the AI to keep Weezing alive for exactly that reason. First two useful turns: Weezing enters beside Slaking and stays; Slaking fires Double-Edge or High Horsepower with no downtime. Turn two protects the suppressor - the moment Weezing dies, Slaking loses half its turns. Alternate deployment: Durant's Entrainment is the backup plan and the fight's second joke - it forcibly gives Truant to an opposing Pokemon, locking the player's own sweeper out of every other turn. Butterfree's Compound Eyes makes Sleep Powder effectively accurate, Rage Powder protects the suppressor and Tailwind hands the slow suppressors the speed. Reserves & disruption: Terrakion's Quick Guard blocks the priority aimed at Weezing. The ace is Archeops, the gas's second beneficiary: its Defeatist halves its Attack and Special Attack once it drops below half HP, and under Neutralizing Gas that drawback simply does not exist, so a 140-Attack Rock/Flying bird hits at full power however hurt it is. Its Sitrus Berry is the backup for when the gas is gone - it pulls Archeops back above the Defeatist line once. Rock Slide and Dual Wingbeat avoid the Earthquake that would hit Weezing, which has Neutralizing Gas rather than Levitate. Every slot serves one legible idea: the player beats it by killing a Weezing, and doing so visibly halves Slaking and cripples Archeops at the same time.
crack: Kill Weezing and the fight halves. Psychic and Ground attacks answer it; Fighting answers Slaking; Fire answers Durant and Butterfree. Do not let Durant touch your sweeper with Entrainment. Quick Guard only blocks priority, so remove the suppressor with an ordinary attack. Once the gas is gone, chip Archeops below half and it stops hitting hard; Rock, Steel, Electric, Ice and Water answer it, and Wide Guard blanks its Rock Slide.

## E0481 TRAINER_KATELYNN class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 5
plan: Concept: a furnace - four Fire bodies, one healer and one brawler, all built to trade. First two useful turns: Audino's Regenerator bulk lets it Wish or Helping Hand while Infernape's Fake Out and mixed Naive spread buy the turn; Wish pays out at the end of the following turn to whoever is in the slot, so it must be aimed at a body that will still be there. Turn two commits to the damage the support turn bought. Alternate deployment: Bewear's Fluffy halves contact damage while doubling Fire damage taken, which is a real and specific weakness in a Fire team; Choice Specs Chandelure is the special cannon, and its Ghost typing makes it immune to the Fake Out and Fighting attacks aimed at a Fire team, while Energy Ball is the furnace's answer to the Water, Ground and Rock attackers that come for it. Audino is the slot worth defending - a Regenerator healer in an all-out offensive team is the reason this fight lasts long enough to be dangerous.

## E0482 TRAINER_SAMUEL class=ace
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a web-and-rain-of-Thunder team - Galvantula's Compound Eyes makes its Thunder accurate without weather, and its Sticky Web lowers the Speed of everything the player sends in afterwards. Alternate deployment: Adaptability Porygon-Z's Tri Attack and Mismagius's Levitate-backed special coverage form the mode that needs no setup at all. The double speed-control plan - permanent web plus temporary Tailwind, so removing one still leaves the other - is the team's spine. Mismagius takes the ghost slot with Levitate, giving the team a Ground immunity and a Nasty Plot sweeper that pairs with the web.

## E0484 TRAINER_MICHELLE class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 1
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: the wall. Michelle is Victory Road's attrition gate - a Mega Meganium whose Triage gives Heal Pulse priority, a Chansey behind an Eviolite, and three bodies that simply refuse to die on schedule. First two useful turns: Mega Meganium uses Heal Pulse, which restores half a partner's HP and, through Triage, does so before almost anything on the field can act - so the player must out-damage a full heal every turn, not merely out-damage a Pokemon. Machamp attacks beside it with No Guard making Dynamic Punch a guaranteed hit and a guaranteed confusion. Turn two chooses between another heal and Light Screen depending on which damage type is actually killing. Alternate deployment: Chansey's Seismic Toss deals fixed damage that ignores every defensive stat, and Soft-Boiled keeps it there; Umbreon's Foul Play uses the target's own Attack, so it punishes exactly the physical sweeper a player brings to break walls. Reserves & disruption: Hydreigon is the one genuinely fast special attacker and the answer to a player who has switched to passive play; Probopass's Magnet Pull traps Steel types and Stealth Rock taxes every switch this team is trying to force. Could this team be cooler? The Triage Heal Pulse idea is excellent and rare, and a pure stall gate is the right change of pace in a gauntlet of sweepers, so the concept holds. The change is Tyranitar, which set sand for a team with no sand abusers at all and duplicated Caroline's dedicated sand team five doors later - a mislabelled strategy flag and a collision in one slot. Umbreon replaces it with a body that actually belongs in a stall team, adds a Dark type to a roster that had no answer to Psychic and Ghost attackers, and makes Foul Play the trap that punishes the player's obvious counterplay. The SAND flag is corrected to SETUP. The Mega now works as this room always described it: Mega Meganium keeps Triage. An instrumented read found the AI scoring the Mega board and declining it on four consecutive turns, and it was right to - the form only had Mega Sol, so evolving traded the priority Heal Pulse this entire room is built on for permanent sun that a team with no Fire attacker can use, plus fresh Steel and Poison weaknesses. Mega Meganium now carries Triage in its hidden slot, so the Meganium that knows Triage keeps it through the evolution while the sun sets a player can build still get Mega Sol. Machamp could not have taken the slot instead: Mega Machamp trades No Guard for Power Fists, which turns the guaranteed Dynamic Punch into a coin flip. Overrepresented? Umbreon, Meganium, Machamp, Chansey and Hydreigon are unique in the campaign; Probopass's other owner is Susie's route team; Tyranitar is returned to Caroline alone. Levels: cap 96, offsets 2/1/1/1/1/1.
crack: You cannot out-grind this team - you have to out-burst it. Fire, Ice, Poison and Flying attacks answer Meganium; Fighting answers Chansey and Umbreon; Fairy answers Hydreigon. Taunt is the single best move here: it turns off Heal Pulse, Soft-Boiled, Light Screen and Stealth Rock at once. Do not boost Attack into Foul Play.

## E0485 TRAINER_MITCHELL class=ace
strategy: TRICK_ROOM
mega_slots: 4
plan: Concept: Victory Road's Trick Room gate, and the one that traps you inside it - Gothitelle's Shadow Tag means the reversed order is not something you can simply switch out of. First two useful turns: Shiinotic's Spore puts a legal target to sleep with certainty while Gothitelle sets Trick Room; Effect Spore additionally inflicts a random status on anything that makes contact with Shiinotic, and Strength Sap drains the target's Attack while healing by that amount. Turn two spends the reversed order on damage. Alternate deployment: Guts Conkeldurr and Thick Club Alolan Marowak are the heavy mode - the Thick Club doubles Marowak's Attack, and Lightning Rod makes it the team's Electric absorber and a redirector against single-target Electric attacks. Reserves & disruption: Mega Scovillain's Insomnia keeps it awake beside its own Spore user, and its dual Grass and Fire special attacks cover the Water and Steel types brought against the room; Magic Guard Reuniclus takes no Life Orb recoil at all, which is why it can afford the item at this tier. All five slow members carry zero Speed IVs so the room works for them and not against them. Could this team be cooler? The trap-plus-room pairing is the right identity for this door, and Mega Scovillain is a genuinely strange, memorable ace. The improvement made is the same one the earlier room teams needed: every Brave, Quiet and Relaxed member now has a zero Speed IV, which is the difference between a Trick Room team and a team carrying the move. The Conkeldurr duplication with Albert is resolved on Albert's side rather than here, because Guts under Trick Room is the more natural home for the set. Overrepresented? Shiinotic, Alolan Marowak and Mega Scovillain are unique; Gothitelle recurs with the route ace Conor at a much lower tier, reading as escalation; Reuniclus's other owner is Alexis with an Assault Vest support role. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/2/1/1.
crack: Taunt the setter before Spore resolves, or bring your own Trick Room. Ghost types and Shed Shell escape Shadow Tag. Fire, Flying and Psychic attacks answer the room; do not make contact with Shiinotic; and wait five turns if you can afford to - everything here is slow on purpose.

## E0486 TRAINER_HALLE class=ace
strategy: REDIRECTION,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: a redirection-and-snowball door. Pachirisu holds the field while two very different self-boosters build, and the Mega closer boosts all five of its own stats in a single turn. First two useful turns: Pachirisu's Follow Me pulls single-target attacks off Pinsir, and Nuzzle guarantees paralysis on a target rather than gambling; Pinsir's Mold Breaker means Sturdy, Levitate, Water Absorb and Volt Absorb do nothing to stop its attacks. Turn two uses whichever body the redirection kept alive. Alternate deployment: Naganadel's Beast Boost raises its Special Attack after every knockout, so a Nasty Plot plus one kill is usually the end of the fight; Meowscarada is the immediate physical option - Protean turns it into the type of each move it uses so every attack carries STAB, Flower Trick always lands a critical hit that ignores the player's Defense boosts and Reflect, Knock Off strips items and Low Kick answers the Steel and Dark bodies that wall its Grass attack. Reserves & disruption: Lickilicky's Cloud Nine suppresses every weather effect on the field, which is a specific answer to the sun, sand and snow teams either side of it in the gauntlet, and its single-use Normal Gem makes one Body Slam far larger than it looks. Mega Falinks's No Retreat raises Attack, Defence, Special Attack, Special Defence and Speed by one stage each at the cost of being unable to switch out - an all-in that the player can answer by simply surviving it. Could this team be cooler? It is already an unusually well-varied door: a snowballing Ultra Beast, a shape-shifting magician cat, a redirector and the campaign's only Mega Falinks, with no shared weather or terrain to exploit. It earns retention. The honest criticism is that Pachirisu duplicates Brawly's gym support from very early in the campaign, but that is deliberate escalation rather than repetition - the same tiny squirrel, now protecting something that ends games - and replacing it would cost the fight its one memorable callback. Overrepresented? Naganadel, Lickilicky and Mega Falinks are unique in the campaign, and Meowscarada's only other owner is Naomi's S.S. Tidal cabin team; Pinsir's Mega is owned elsewhere but this base Mold Breaker version is not; Pachirisu's only other owner is Brawly.
crack: Spread damage ignores Follow Me and kills Pachirisu at the same time. Fire and Flying attacks answer Pinsir, and Bug, Fire, Flying, Ice and Fairy attacks answer Meowscarada, whose Protean typing shifts with every move it uses; Ground and Psychic answer Naganadel; Fighting answers Falinks and Lickilicky. Let No Retreat happen and then survive it - Falinks cannot leave, so a single bulky body can wall the closer outright.

## E0487 TRAINER_VITO class=ace
strategy: SUN,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: the sun door - Drought on entry, Solar Beam with no charge turn, a Contrary Grass type that gets stronger every time it uses its strongest move, and a Mega Raichu X to close. First two useful turns: Ninetales establishes sun on entry and either fires an instantly charged Solar Beam or Encores a wasted opposing action; Bisharp attacks, and its Defiant means any stat drop aimed at it - an Intimidate, a Snarl, an Icy Wind - raises its Attack by two stages instead. Turn two uses the sun rather than reinforcing it. Alternate deployment: Lurantis's Contrary turns Leaf Storm's Special Attack drop into a two-stage raise, so it snowballs by attacking, and Superpower makes it physically tougher each time; Synthesis restores two-thirds of its HP under sun instead of half. Reserves & disruption: Choice Band Dodrio with Early Bird shrugs off sleep in a single turn, and Flygon's Yache Berry cushions the Ice attack that is otherwise its death; Mega Raichu X's Fake Out and Knock Off give the closer utility as well as damage. Could this team be cooler? The Contrary Lurantis and Defiant Bisharp pairing is the fight's real identity: both members are rewarded for the things a player normally does to weaken a team, which makes this door punish reflexive play rather than bad play. That earns retention and is why Lurantis stays here rather than with the route ace Sophia, who is rebuilt around a Dancer engine instead. The one duplication left is Bisharp against Dianne five doors later, and that is resolved on Dianne's side by promoting hers to Kingambit, so this remains the campaign's only Defiant Bisharp. Overrepresented? Mega Raichu X, Dodrio, Flygon and Lurantis are unique after this pass; Ninetales's Drought recurs only with Leaf's Kanto team in the post-game and with Maxie's Magma team far earlier, all with different payloads. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Take the sun away and Solar Beam needs a charge turn while Synthesis halves. Do not use Intimidate, Snarl or Icy Wind into Defiant. Fighting and Fire attacks answer Bisharp and Lurantis; Rock and Ice answer Dodrio and Flygon; Ground answers Raichu. Taunt stops Encore, Tailwind and Synthesis.

## E0488 TRAINER_OWEN class=ace
strategy: NONE
mega_slots: 4
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a no-engine door built purely on threat density - six bodies with no shared weather, terrain or room, each of which beats a different thing. First two useful turns: Torterra attacks while Choice Scarf Staraptor picks the target its Intimidate has already weakened; Staraptor also carries Final Gambit, which deals damage equal to its own remaining HP and faints it - a genuine, unexpected trade the player cannot block with resistances. Turn two brings whichever body the opening revealed the player cannot answer. Alternate deployment: Sylveon's Pixilate converts Hyper Voice into a spread Fairy attack, and the Throat Spray it holds raises its Special Attack the moment that sound move connects; Helping Hand makes it a support turn when the spread would not kill. Reserves & disruption: Toxtricity's Punk Rock raises the power of its sound moves by 30% and halves incoming sound damage, so Overdrive is a spread Electric attack with no friendly-fire cost; Mega Lucario Z is the physical closer; Hisuian Lilligant's Victory Dance raises Attack, Defence and Speed at once and Chlorophyll does nothing here, which is the deliberate cost of a fast physical Grass body. Could this team be cooler? The answer is yes and it is now more varied: Tentacruel was this roster's fourth appearance in the audit range and Victreebel duplicated Julie's Mega two doors away, so two slots were doing nothing the player had not seen. Toxtricity brings a sound-based spread attacker that no other trainer in the campaign fields and pairs deliberately with Sylveon's own sound attack; Hisuian Lilligant brings a physical Grass dancer that is genuinely rare and threatens after a single free turn. The fight keeps its identity - no gimmick, six real threats - while stopping the repetition. Overrepresented? Toxtricity, Hisuian Lilligant, Staraptor, Sylveon and Mega Lucario Z are unique in the campaign; Torterra's other owner is Warren's Trick Room team far earlier. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/2/1/1.
crack: Ice and Fire attacks answer Torterra and Lilligant; Electric and Rock answer Staraptor; Steel and Poison answer Sylveon; Ground answers Toxtricity and Lucario. Watch for Final Gambit from a healthy Staraptor. Taunt stops Victory Dance and Helping Hand, and the Choice lock can be baited.

## E0489 TRAINER_CAROLINE class=ace
strategy: SAND
mega_slots: 3
plan: Concept: the sandstorm door, and the only one in the gauntlet - five bodies that either double their Speed in sand, gain power from it, or are simply immune to its chip damage. First two useful turns: Tyranitar's Sand Stream is live on entry and Smooth Rock stretches it to eight turns, so Stoutland and Sandslash are already at doubled Speed before the player's lead acts; Tyranitar attacks or Protects to bank a turn. Turn two spends the Sand Rush window on damage, because the whole plan expires with the weather. Alternate deployment: Mega Garchomp arrives in the third slot rather than the last, which is deliberate - its Sand Veil raises evasion in the storm it is standing in, so it is hardest to remove exactly while the sand is up. Alolan Dugtrio's Sand Force raises its Ground, Rock and Steel attacks by 30% in sand, and Sucker Punch gives the team priority. Reserves & disruption: Archaludon is the weather-independent closer - a Steel/Dragon that the sand never chips, whose Stamina raises its Defense every time it is hit, so the longer the player spends breaking it the harder it gets; Draco Meteor, Flash Cannon and Thunderbolt cover the Dragon, Fairy and Water answers to the rest of the team. It is the answer to a player who solves the sand and then expects the fight to be over. Could this team be cooler? It is the correct sand team for this position and the composition holds - three distinct sand abilities and a sand-immune closer that does not care about the weather is a genuinely good structure, and Sand Veil placed early rather than late is a smart, slightly unfair touch. The duplication with Michelle's mislabelled SAND flag five doors earlier is resolved on her side, so Caroline is now the campaign's only Sand Stream owner outside the Magma arc and the sandstorm reads as this trainer's signature. The remaining fix here was a legality error: Stoutland cannot learn Double-Edge, so it takes Retaliate instead, which doubles in power after an ally has fainted - a better fit for a team that expects to trade. Overrepresented? Stoutland, Alolan Dugtrio and Sandslash are unique in the campaign, and Archaludon's only other owner is Matt far earlier; Mega Garchomp recurs only as Cynthia's custom Garchomp Z variant in the final optional fight, a deliberate mirror; Tyranitar's other owners are the Magma arc far earlier.
crack: Replace the weather and three members lose half their Speed and their evasion boost at once. Water, Grass, Fighting and Ground attacks answer Tyranitar; Fighting answers Stoutland; Ice and Dragon answer Garchomp; Ground and Fighting answer Archaludon before Stamina stacks, and special attacks sidestep its Defense boosts. Wide Guard blanks Rock Slide, and sand chip damage is hurting the player far more than it hurts this team.

## E0490 TRAINER_JULIE class=ace
strategy: SUN,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
plan: Concept: the second sun door, deliberately built the opposite way round to Vito's - his sun powers a Contrary snowballer, hers powers two Chlorophyll sweepers and a Solar Power glass cannon. First two useful turns: Torkoal's Drought is live on entry and Eruption fires at full power only while Torkoal is at full HP, so the player must damage it before it acts; Venusaur is already at doubled Speed from Chlorophyll and opens with Sleep Powder or Giga Drain. Turn two uses the real HP and speed state rather than the opening one. Alternate deployment: Heliolisk's Solar Power raises its Special Attack by half in sun at the cost of HP every turn, and Glare paralyses regardless of the target's type; Lycanroc's Accelerock is the priority that punishes a weakened lead. Reserves & disruption: Chesnaught is the deliberate change of gear - a Grass/Fighting fortress that resists the Water and Rock attacks a player brings for a sun team, where Iron Defence plus Body Press turns that defence directly into damage, Leech Seed drains the Water attacker it walls, Spiky Shield punishes contact, and Bulletproof blocks ball and bomb moves such as Shadow Ball, Sludge Bomb and Focus Blast; Mega Victreebel's Weather Ball becomes a 100-power Fire attack under its own sun. Could this team be cooler? Two sun teams inside sixteen doors is a real question, and the answer is that they are legitimately different fights - Vito's rewards you for not weakening him, Julie's punishes you for not being fast - so both hold. What makes Julie's memorable is the Chesnaught slot: a player who has correctly brought a Water or Rock answer to a sun team then runs into a wall that resists both, gets tougher every turn and converts that toughness into damage. That contrast is worth more than a rarer sixth Fire type would be. The Victreebel duplication is resolved on Owen's side so the Mega is unique. Overrepresented? Heliolisk, Lycanroc and Mega Victreebel are unique in the campaign, and Chesnaught's only other owners are Kai far earlier and Marley's Mega; Torkoal and Venusaur recur at lower tiers with Darcy, Flannery and Marley in different ability roles.
crack: Take the sun and everything slows down at once. Water, Rock and Ground attacks answer the Fire half; Fire, Ice, Flying and Psychic answer the Grass half; Fire, Flying, Psychic and Fairy special attacks answer Chesnaught, and special damage ignores Iron Defence entirely - and sun boosts your Fire. Chip Torkoal before it moves and Eruption collapses. Taunt stops Sleep Powder, Glare, Iron Defence and Leech Seed.

## E0491 TRAINER_FELIX class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 5
plan: Concept: the last non-rival door before the League, and the only one that opens with sleep and closes with a stance change - six threats with no engine except raw quality. First two useful turns: Jynx's Lovely Kiss or Fake Out steals the opening turn while Kommo-o uses Clangorous Soul, paying a third of its HP to raise Attack, Defence, Special Attack, Special Defence and Speed by one stage each in a single turn; its Throat Spray adds a further Special Attack stage the first time a sound move it uses lands. Turn two is the player's last chance to stop a fully set-up Dragon, whose Clanging Scales then hits both targets and whose Flamethrower answers the Fairy and Steel types that would otherwise wall it. Soundproof makes Kommo-o immune to the player's own sound moves, Perish Song included. Alternate deployment: Sceptile and Mega Greninja are the fast mixed-attacking mode, with Protean changing Greninja's type to match each move it uses so its STAB is always live and its resistances are never the ones you prepared for. Reserves & disruption: Eviolite Duraludon is a genuine special wall because it still evolves, and Stalwart means its attacks cannot be redirected by Follow Me, Rage Powder or Storm Drain; Aegislash's Stance Change flips it to Blade Forme when it attacks and back to Shield Forme when it uses King's Shield, and its Weakness Policy means a super-effective hit while it is shielded makes the next attack enormous. Could this team be cooler? This is the correct final gate: one all-stat setup, one type-shifting Mega, one stance-shifting Policy closer, and a Stalwart wall that specifically defeats the redirection strategies the gauntlet has been teaching the player to use. The composition holds. The one thing worth stating plainly is that an undisturbed Clangorous Soul is the single most dangerous turn in Victory Road, and the level spread reflects that the fight is meant to be decided on turn one or two. Overrepresented? Jynx, Duraludon, Aegislash and Mega Greninja are unique in the campaign, and Kommo-o's four other owners all come earlier in the campaign; Sceptile's other owner is Susie's route team with a White Herb Unburden set; Greninja's base form appears once in the Sootopolis gym at a far lower tier.
crack: Stop Clangorous Soul or lose - Taunt, Fake Out or a knockout before it moves, and the HP it pays makes it easier to finish. Deny Lovely Kiss with your own Fake Out, or lead with a Fire, Rock or Steel body that survives Jynx. Fairy attacks hit Kommo-o four times over, and Ice, Flying, Psychic and Dragon twice; Clanging Scales lowers its own Defence every time it fires; Fighting and Ground answer Aegislash while it is in Blade Forme; and do not hit Aegislash super-effectively while it is shielded. Fighting and Ground answer Duraludon despite the Eviolite.

## E0492 TRAINER_DIANNE class=ace
strategy: SNOW,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the blizzard door - automatic snow, an Aurora Veil that halves both kinds of damage, and a closer that smashes through the screens it hid behind. First two useful turns: Abomasnow's Snow Warning is live on entry, so Froslass can immediately use Aurora Veil, which needs snow to work at all and then reduces physical and special damage for five turns; Abomasnow's Blizzard never misses in its own weather. Turn two attacks from behind the veil rather than adding more setup. Alternate deployment: Magmortar and Virizion are the deliberately off-type answers to the Fire, Steel and Fighting attacks a player brings to beat an Ice team; Justified raises Virizion's Attack whenever a Dark attack hits it. Reserves & disruption: Froslass's Destiny Bond takes the attacker down with it, so removing the veil-setter has a real price; Kingambit's Supreme Overlord raises its Attack for every ally that has already fainted, which makes it strongest exactly when the fight is nearly over, and Sucker Punch turns that accumulated power into priority. Mega Barbaracle's Shell Smash under a still-active Aurora Veil is the intended finish. Could this team be cooler? The Aurora Veil plus Destiny Bond pairing is a genuinely nasty trap - killing the screen-setter costs the player a Pokemon - and the snow is properly sourced, so the concept holds. The change is Bisharp, which was an exact duplicate of Vito's five doors earlier: Kingambit is its evolution, it is rarer, and Supreme Overlord makes it a late-fight threat that scales with the gauntlet's own attrition, which is a far better fit for the last ace before the Elite Four than a second copy of a set the player just beat. Overrepresented? Kingambit, Magmortar, Virizion and Mega Barbaracle are unique in the campaign; Abomasnow's other owner Makayla runs a physical route set; Froslass recurs as Glacia's League Mega, which reads as this trainer studying under her. Levels: cap 96, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Replace the weather and Aurora Veil cannot be set at all; Brick Break, Defog and Infiltrator get through it once it is up. Fire, Fighting, Rock and Steel attacks answer the Ice half. Do not kill Froslass with your ace - Destiny Bond takes it with her. Kingambit gets stronger as the fight goes on, so do not save it for last.

## E0493 TRAINER_SIDNEY class=elite
strategy: TAILWIND
mega_slots: 5,6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Dark Aura pressure with protected fast finishers and a poisoned answer to Fairies. First two useful turns: Incineroar's Fake Out and Intimidate open beside Yveltal, whose Tailwind or Life Orb Dark Pulse/Heat Wave starts the pressure; Grimmsnarl's Prankster screens come next. Roles: Alolan Muk replaces Urshifu, which was four-times weak to Fairy. It is Poison/Dark, so Fairy moves hit it only neutrally, and Assault Vest on 105 HP and 100 Special Defense absorbs the special Fairy attackers that reach the League - Mega Gardevoir, Flutter Mane, Tapu Lele, Enamorus. Gunk Shot is the super-effective hit on every Fairy; Knock Off (Dark Aura-boosted) strips a Choice item, Booster Energy or Assault Vest; Clear Smog erases a Calm Mind, Nasty Plot or Quiver Dance on its target; Shadow Sneak picks off a fast Fairy the others have left low. Poison Touch poisons through Knock Off and Shadow Sneak contact. Mega Sharpedo can earn a Speed Boost before Mega Evolution, then trade the ability for Strong Jaw Crunch/Psychic Fangs (Psychic Fangs removes screens); Mega Absol Z uses its 151 base Speed and Magic Bounce to punish direct status targeting. How the pieces combine: Tailwind lets Sharpedo and Absol move before a fast Fairy, Grimmsnarl's screens blunt its first hits, and Muk walls it and hits back super-effectively while Yveltal's aura powers Muk's Knock Off. Dark Aura boosts eligible Dark attacks on both sides. Could it be cooler? The Fairy answer is still a Dark type - a toxic, aura-fed Muk belongs to the League's villain specialist far more than a borrowed Steel wall - and Clear Smog gives Sidney a clean reset against the setup Fairies. Overrepresented? Alolan Muk's other uses are an Aqua Hideout grunt and Archie, both far earlier; Sharpedo is also an Archie showcase, and Absol Z stays Sidney's exclusive named form. Urshifu is released.
crack: Ground is super-effective on Muk and it is slow, so remove it before it acts and Fairy pressure works on the rest again; burns on the physical attackers, screen removal, Trick Room, priority, and denying Sharpedo a safe setup window. Dark types obstruct Grimmsnarl's Prankster Thunder Wave; a Pokemon with no held item blanks Knock Off's extra value.

## E0496 TRAINER_DRAKE class=elite
strategy: SUN,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 1,6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Mixed Dragon air force with a sun phase and a real answer to Fairy types. First two useful turns: Mega Salamence sets Tailwind or fires Aerilate Double-Edge while Koraidon's Orichalcum Pulse raises the sun for Reshiram's Blue Flare and Mega Dragonite's Flamethrower. Roles: Dragalge replaces Zekrom as the Fairy answer. It is Poison/Dragon, so Fairy moves hit it only neutrally, and its 123 base Special Defense behind an Assault Vest takes the special Fairy attackers that reach the League - Mega Gardevoir, Flutter Mane, Tapu Lele, Enamorus. Adaptability Sludge Bomb is a doubled-STAB super-effective hit on every one of them; Draco Meteor is the Adaptability Dragon nuke, Thunderbolt keeps the Electric coverage Zekrom brought, and Focus Blast answers the Steel types that resist both STABs. Mega Salamence trades Earthquake, which hit its grounded partners, for Iron Head: under its own Tailwind a 120-base Mega outspeeds Flutter Mane and Iron Valiant, so a super-effective Steel hit lands before the fast Fairies move. Reshiram, Koraidon and Specs Regidrago are the damage; Mega Dragonite (145 Special Attack, Multiscale, Roost) is the specially offensive closer and the ace. How the pieces combine: Tailwind plus Iron Head races the fast Fairy, Dragalge walls and poisons the slow special one, and the sun doubles Reshiram's and Dragonite's Fire into the Steel types that wall Dragon moves. Could it be cooler? Drake is a sailor, and Dragalge is the sea dragon that hides in kelp - the Fairy answer comes from his own waters rather than a borrowed Steel wall. Overrepresented? Zekrom leaves the League; Dragalge's other uses are an Aqua Hideout grunt and Shelly's Mega, both far from the League, and the Steel/Dragon line is already Caroline's and Felix's on Victory Road. Mega Rayquaza stays with the final Steven exhibition, not a third Mega here.
crack: Psychic and Ground hit Dragalge super-effectively and it is slow, so remove it first and Fairy pressure works on the rest again; Fairy immunity to Dragon moves, Ice pressure, Wide Guard against Dragon Energy, your own weather or Trick Room against Tailwind, and chip that breaks Dragonite's Multiscale. Draco Meteor lowers Special Attack and Roost changes Dragonite's grounding; neither is a free loop. Steel types resist Iron Head and Sludge Bomb but fear the sun Fire.

## E0498 TRAINER_WALLY_VR_2 class=rival
strategy: REDIRECTION,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the rival arc, part two. Wally comes back as Champion-tier and the arc completes its mirror - the Gallade that was his Mega at Victory Road now stands beside him as an ordinary partner while Altaria, the Swablu he caught on Route 114, carries the stone. First two useful turns: Indeedee-F sets Psychic Terrain on entry and redirects with Follow Me to keep Gallade's Sharpness-boosted Psycho Cut or Sacred Sword alive; Psychic Terrain also blocks all priority moves aimed at his grounded members, which is what makes the setup viable at this level. Gallade attacks. Turn two picks the special half rather than repeating the redirect. Alternate deployment: Tapu Lele is the raw damage mode - its Psychic Surge re-establishes the terrain if it has been replaced, and under it Psychic and Psyshock hit half again as hard. Gardevoir's Trace copies the most valuable ability on the opposing side the moment it enters. Reserves & disruption: Magearna's Soul-Heart raises its Special Attack every time any Pokemon on the field faints, so a long fight makes it progressively lethal, and Fleur Cannon is enormous at the cost of a two-stage drop each use. Mega Altaria's Pixilate converts Return into a Fairy-type attack with a power bonus, and at maximum friendship Return is already at full power - a Dragon Dance behind that is the closer, and its Dragon/Fairy typing means the Steel answer the player saved for Magearna is the only thing that also answers this. Note that Psychic Terrain blocks the team's own priority as well, so Gallade has no safe priority option while it is up. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and the rebuild is what makes the two Wally fights a rival arc rather than two unrelated bosses. The authored roster was Dragapult, Heatran and Incineroar behind a Mega Magearna - a strong team with no connection to Wally at all, and one that borrowed Steven's and the League's identities. The new version keeps a single coherent Psychic and Fairy identity across both fights, promotes his own Altaria to the Mega slot using the campaign's one unused Altarianite, and makes the memorable moment structural: the player recognises every Pokemon, and the one that was a support in the first fight is now the thing that kills them. Overrepresented? Mega Altaria, Tapu Lele and Magearna appear nowhere else in the campaign; Indeedee-F's other owner is the route trainer Alexis at a far lower tier; Gardevoir and Gallade are Wally's alone. Levels: cap 100, offsets 1/2/2/2/2/3.
crack: Steel and Poison attacks answer four of the six; Fire answers Magearna and Gallade; Ghost and Flying answer the Psychic half. Replace the terrain and your priority works again. Spread damage ignores Follow Me. Let Magearna use Fleur Cannon twice and it has halved its own Special Attack; Mega Altaria is bulky but must dance before it threatens, and Steel and Poison attacks answer it outright.

## E0499 TRAINER_VINCENT class=ace
strategy: SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a post-game control specialist - a Prankster that dictates the turn order, a Regenerator pivot that never stays broken, and three closers that each punish a different mistake. First two useful turns: Klefki's Prankster gives Thunder Wave, Light Screen and Foul Play priority, so the speed control lands before anything else happens, and its Steel/Fairy typing walls the Dragon and Dark attacks the rest of the team invites; Mienshao attacks and U-turns out, restoring a third of its HP through Regenerator each time it leaves. Turn two pivots only when the replacement genuinely improves the board. Alternate deployment: Poison Heal Gliscor is the independent Swords Dance mode - the Toxic Orb heals it every turn instead of damaging it, so it is immune to further status and gains HP while setting up. Reserves & disruption: Xurkitree's Beast Boost and Electroweb combine speed control with a Special Attack that grows after each knockout; Choice Scarf Typhlosion's Eruption is calculated from its current HP at the moment it fires, so it must come in clean. Mega Eelektross is the physical closer, and its Eelevate ability is Levitate and Beast Boost at once: it floats over the Ground attacks that are the usual answer to Electric types, and every knockout it lands raises its highest stat, so it and Xurkitree form a snowballing pair. Supercell Slam, Close Combat and Fire Punch cover the Ground, Dragon, Steel and Grass bodies that resist Electric. Could this team be cooler? The control-plus-three-closers structure is right for an optional post-game ace, and the honest weakness was the lead: Sableye was an exact duplicate of Albert's Victory Road set, so the fight opened with a puzzle the player had already solved. Klefki keeps the Prankster identity while adding a typing and a screen the old lead did not have, and Foul Play still punishes the physical sweeper a player brings for Gliscor. The rest earns retention - Eruption on a Choice Scarf is a genuine, unforgiving read for both sides. Overrepresented? Klefki, Mienshao, Gliscor, Xurkitree and Typhlosion are unique in the campaign; Mega Eelektross's only other owner is an Aqua Hideout grunt far earlier, with a different set.
crack: Fire and Ground attacks answer Klefki; Fairy and Flying answer Mienshao; Ice and Water answer Gliscor; Ground answers Xurkitree, but Mega Eelektross floats and has no weakness left: out-damage it with strong neutral hits before a knockout feeds Eelevate, wall it with Grass, Dragon or Electric bulk that resists its main attack, and Protect into Supercell Slam, which crashes for damage when it misses or is blocked. Taunt stops the Prankster support and Swords Dance together. Chip Typhlosion once and Eruption stops mattering, and Magic Guard or a Steel type ignores the Toxic Orb plan.

## E0500 TRAINER_KEIRA class=ace
strategy: SAND,REDIRECTION,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: a sandstorm that exists to protect a single reactive nuke. Rhyperior holds a Weakness Policy, and everything else on the team decides whether the player is allowed to trigger it. First two useful turns: Gigalith's Sand Stream is live on entry with a Smooth Rock behind it, so Sandslash is already at doubled Speed; Sandslash attacks. Turn two either spends the sand window or transitions to support - but only when that protects a body worth protecting. Alternate deployment: Clefable's Follow Me pulls a single-target attack away from Rhyperior, which is a genuine decision rather than a free action, because a super-effective hit landing on Rhyperior would raise both its attacking stats by two stages through the Policy - the redirection can correctly prevent the team's own payoff, and both benefits must never be counted at once. Solid Rock separately reduces super-effective damage against it by a quarter, and the sandstorm raises a Rock type's Special Defence by half, so the Water and Grass attacks the player brings for a sand team - four times effective on Rhyperior - are exactly the hits that fire the Policy without finishing it. High Horsepower, Rock Slide and Ice Punch then cover the Steel, Fire, Flying and Grass bodies; Rhyperior avoids Earthquake so it never hits its own partners. Reserves & disruption: Whimsicott's Prankster Tailwind and Encore are the backup speed plan for when the sand is replaced, not automatic redundancy; Rotom-Wash covers Water and Electric and takes sand chip every turn, which is a real cost; its Levitate and Hydro Pump answer the Ground and Fire attackers aimed at the rocks. Mega Clefable is the closer. Could this team be cooler? The plan's internal tension - a redirector that can accidentally disarm its own ace - is unusually sophisticated and is exactly the kind of decision the AI audit should care about, so the concept earns retention unchanged. The honest note is that this is the second sand team in the post-game and the third overall; it is kept because its sand is a shield for a reactive item rather than a Sand Rush speed engine like Caroline's, which makes it a different fight despite the shared weather. Overrepresented? Gigalith, Whimsicott and Mega Clefable are unique, and Rhyperior's other owners are the Magma arc and Albert's Victory Road team; Sandslash's other owner is Caroline in Victory Road; Rotom-Wash's other owner is Daphne's gym ice room.
crack: Hit Rhyperior with neutral damage only, or with one super-effective hit large enough to remove it through Solid Rock and the sand, or do not hit it at all until the Policy holder is the last thing standing. Water, Grass, Fighting and Steel attacks answer Gigalith; Fighting, Ground, Ice and Steel hit Rhyperior twice over and still fire the Policy, so either remove it in one blow or leave it alone; Steel and Poison answer Clefable. Taunt stops Tailwind, Encore and Follow Me. Replacing the weather removes Sandslash's speed and the team's chip damage together.

## E0501 TRAINER_LEROY class=ace
strategy: TRICK_ROOM
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the post-game Trick Room - six slow bodies that are happiest moving last, set up by a legendary that never needs to move first. First two useful turns: Cresselia sets Trick Room behind a Mental Herb, or Helping Hands a Skeledirge attack when the speeds already favour the team; Mega Scrafty's Fake Out protects the room turn before the transformation, and Intimidate on entry softens the physical lead. Turn two spends the reversed order rather than cycling Moonlight. Roles: Skeledirge is the special cannon - every Torch Song raises its Special Attack, so it grows stronger through the room's five turns, and Unaware means the player's own boosts, Calm Minds and Dragon Dances included, do nothing to protect them from it or to help them break it; Earth Power hits the Fire and Rock types that resist its Fire, and Shadow Ball covers the Ghost and Psychic bodies. Copperajah is the physical closer: Sheer Force strips the flinch from Rock Slide for a 30 percent power bonus and no Life Orb recoil, Heavy Slam from a 650-kilogram elephant flattens light targets, and High Horsepower answers the Fire and Steel types that would wall it. Reserves & disruption: Mawile is base Intimidate and deliberately not a second Mega, so its slot is a second Intimidate body and a Sucker Punch, not a hidden ace; Slurpuff's Sweet Veil makes the whole team immune to sleep, which is the specific answer to the Spore and Sleep Powder leads the post-game is full of. Every slow member carries a zero Speed IV so the room works in the team's favour. Cresselia is the fight's one legendary and its whole job is the room.
crack: Taunt the setter, or be slower yourself. Dark, Ghost and Bug attacks answer Cresselia; Water, Ground and Rock answer Skeledirge, and Dark and Ghost hit it too - chip it before Torch Song stacks; Fire and Ground answer Copperajah and Mawile, and Fighting hits Copperajah too. Sweet Veil stops sleep but nothing else - paralysis, burn and confusion all still land. Wait out the five turns if you can survive them.

## E0504 TRAINER_COLTON class=ace
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 6
ai: Conservative
plan: Concept: a cruise-liner cat burglar's team - Prankster control, two pivots and a tiny Fairy that quietly becomes unkillable. First two useful turns: Liepard's Prankster gives Fake Out, Taunt, Encore and Foul Play priority, so it takes the first turn and decides what the player is allowed to do with theirs; Fezandipiti attacks or sets Tailwind, and its Toxic Chain has a chance to badly poison on every hit. Turn two converts the control into damage rather than adding another passive support. Alternate deployment: Mesprit's Helping Hand and Thunder Wave behind a Covert Cloak - which blocks all secondary effects and Fake Out aimed at it - supports Mega Floette's Calm Mind. Reserves & disruption: Alolan Persian's Fur Coat halves physical damage and Foul Play uses the attacker's own Attack, so the pair of them punish physical setup twice over; Incineroar is the Intimidate pivot with Parting Shot lowering both of a target's attacking stats as it leaves. Eternal Floette's Flower Veil protects the team's Grass and Fairy members from stat drops, and Wish needs a recipient that will survive to receive it. Could this team be cooler? The composition is a good, thematically tight "thieves on a ship" roster and the four-way priority-control opening is genuinely oppressive, so it earns retention. Eternal Floette deserves specific defence: it is one of the rarest forms in the series, it is not simply a stat upgrade, and pairing its Calm Mind with Flower Veil and Wish makes it a win condition the player has to break through rather than out-damage. The change is tier - the block's stale note about a discarded level-100 floor is replaced by honest live-cap offsets. Overrepresented? Liepard, Fezandipiti, Alolan Persian, Mesprit and Mega Floette are unique in the campaign; Incineroar's other owners are the Elite Four's Dark specialist and Wally's earlier authored draft, which this pass removes. Levels: cap 100, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2.
crack: Fighting, Bug and Fairy attacks answer the Dark half; Steel, Poison and Ground answer Fezandipiti and Floette. Taunt beats their Taunt if you move first, and Prankster status fails against Dark types entirely - a Dark lead of your own turns the whole opening off. Break Floette before three Calm Minds.

## E0510 TRAINER_WALLACE_DOUBLES_LEGENDS class=elite
strategy: RAIN,TAILWIND,SETUP,REDIRECTION,ALLY_COMBO,PRESSURE
mega_slots: NONE
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the Champion off duty in Diancie's chamber, no type restriction and no reason to hold back - Kyogre is already Primal when it walks in, so the sea is Primordial from turn zero and the only question is who survives inside it. The rebuild is written against the four optimized parties that went 4-0 on the previous roster (Wide Guard + AV Tapu Bulu, Trick Room with Booster Iron Hands, Shadow Tag Perish Song, Choice Band Urshifu priority offense), and every member is here to close one of those doors. Leads: Kyogre and Lucario. First two useful turns: Kyogre holds Covert Cloak instead of the Blue Orb (it is authored in its Primal form, so Primordial Sea is still on the field before anything moves), which means Fake Out cannot flinch it - the flinch that stole turn one in three of four games is gone. Lucario's Inner Focus makes the same true of the redirector, so turn one is Follow Me at +2 priority pulling AV Tapu Bulu's Wood Hammer (resisted, 144 max into 357 HP) and Booster Iron Hands' Wild Charge (228 max) off Kyogre and onto Rocky Helmet, while a full-HP Water Spout fires: 150 power, heavy rain, from 453 Special Attack behind 252/252 physical bulk, it OHKOs Booster Iron Hands (478-562 into 512) and Mega Gengar (364-428 into 324) and takes 74% from Cresselia - the Trick Room and Perish Song leads die or are crippled before their gimmick lands. Turn two is Zapdos or Zamazenta arriving as the second lane: Zapdos Tailwind if the field is fast, and from then on Thunder never misses on anything that resisted the water and Hurricane never misses on the Grass, Fighting and Bug answers a rain-hater brings. Alternate deployment - the Wide Guard door: Feint at +2 priority (Lucario, and Marshadow's Technician Feint as the second copy) lifts Wide Guard for the side before Water Spout resolves in the same turn, and the pair AI simulates exactly that combination; independent of any adapting, Ice Beam and Thunder on Kyogre, Thunder/Hurricane on Zapdos, Body Press/Heavy Slam on Zamazenta and Melmetal's three punches are single-target lanes that a Wide Guard slot never touches, so the fight has two whole win conditions that do not care about Wide Guard at all. The Trick Room door: Melmetal (0 Speed IVs, 67 Speed) moves first under the player's own Trick Room, and Iron Fist Double Iron Bash moving first is two 30% flinches a turn; Zamazenta and Kyogre do not need to outspeed anyone to do their jobs. The Urshifu door: Zapdos resists Close Combat, is neutral to Surging Strikes and holds Rocky Helmet with Static, so three contact hits cost Choice Band Urshifu half its HP and roll paralysis three times, while rain Thunder OHKOs it (528 into 342) and Kyogre resists the Water STAB; Lucario redirects one Surging Strikes into a second Helmet. Reserves & disruption: Zamazenta-Crowned is the second win condition and deliberately the opposite shape of the first - physical, single-target, speed-independent, Intimidate-proof and Fake Out-proof (Covert Cloak): Dauntless Shield gives +1 Defence on entry, Iron Defense takes it to +3, and Body Press then hits from an effective 1080 Attack with STAB - 4x on Chien-Pao and Kingambit, 2x on Ting-Lu and Melmetal - while Heavy Slam at 120 power covers the Fairies that resist it; while the sea stands it is weak only to Fighting and Ground, and at +1 it takes 55% from Booster Great Tusk's Headlong Rush and 69% from Band Close Combat. Melmetal (Assault Vest, Iron Fist) is the slow anchor: Double Iron Bash, Thunder Punch (Urshifu 2x), Ice Punch (Tusk, Rillaboom, Bulu, Raging Bolt, Landorus 4x) and Body Press for the Dark/Ice and Dark/Steel bodies. Marshadow is the shadow in the storm: a Ghost, so Shadow Tag cannot hold it and Fake Out cannot touch it; Spectral Thief steals the Calm Minds, Swords Dances and Bulk Ups the player sets up behind Wide Guard or Trick Room, Close Combat is the third Fighting lane, Technician Shadow Sneak and Feint are priority, and Assault Vest keeps it a body rather than a Life Orb sketch. Counterplay & audit: every member has 252 HP and a real defensive stat; Ice hits only Zapdos; Electric has one good target (Kyogre) and Zapdos is neutral; Fire fails for the whole time Kyogre stands, which is why three Steel bodies can be here at once; the ability set is audited (Primordial Sea on the Primal species, Inner Focus on the redirector, Static + Helmet on the contact sponge, Iron Fist, Dauntless Shield, Technician) and RAIN/TAILWIND/SETUP/REDIRECTION/ALLY_COMBO are each executable by a named member (Primordial Sea, Zapdos Tailwind, Iron Defense, Follow Me, Feint + Water Spout). Could this team be cooler? Yes, and it is cooler now for a concrete reason: the previous roster was three Forces of Nature that died to any single Ice attack and a Mega that died to one Close Combat, and its whole plan lived in spread moves one Wide Guard turned off. This team keeps the Champion's type-free indulgence and the storm identity - Kyogre is the sea itself, Zapdos is the thunder the sea makes perfect, Jirachi's Hoenn was considered and rejected because it cannot learn Follow Me here, and the three Steel bodies are the fireproof wall the sea builds - and every seam the receipt named now has a member standing in it rather than a sentence in the dossier. Overrepresented? Kyogre is shared with Champion Wallace's League team by design, same trainer, same partner, now in its true form from the first frame. Zapdos, Lucario, Melmetal, Zamazenta and Marshadow each appear once elsewhere in different jobs and never together. Levels: cap 100, offsets 6/6/6/6/6/6 - the engine allows opponents above 100 and the post-game showpiece uses the full +6 on every slot. This is designed to be the hardest fight in the game, above the Elite Four, the Champion and Cynthia: a weather that cannot be taken, a lead pair neither of which can be flinched, a redirector that eats the two hits that killed Kyogre last time, two win conditions with opposite answers, and a bench that wins under the player's own Trick Room.
crack: Ground is the crack and it is not free: Lucario, Melmetal and Zamazenta are all weak to it, but Zapdos is immune, Kyogre's Water Spout hits Great Tusk and Ting-Lu for double, Melmetal's Ice Punch and Zamazenta's Body Press answer the Ground types on contact, and Headlong Rush into +1 Zamazenta is a 55% hit that costs the user its own defences. Fire returns the moment Kyogre is gone and three Steel bodies become weak to it - so the real move is to remove Kyogre first, through Follow Me, through Covert Cloak, through 252/252 bulk and through Protect, with a Grass or Electric attack that Lucario is not redirecting. Ice hits Zapdos alone. Taunt, Haze and Clear Smog stop Iron Defense; a Ghost walls Body Press and Close Combat until Kyogre, Zapdos or Shadow Sneak deals with it; Perish Song still counts on Kyogre and Zapdos, but the singer eats a full-HP Water Spout before the count reaches two. Trick Room still inverts Kyogre, Zapdos and Zamazenta - it has to survive Melmetal moving first. Wide Guard blanks Water Spout and Hurricane until Feint lifts it, and the real version of that door is a Wide Guard held on a board where BOTH actives are Normal-immune: Feint is a Normal attack, so a Ghost target takes nothing and the lift never runs, and clearing the side also works through a non-Ghost partner, so one Ghost is not enough - it takes two. Wide Guard is exempt from consecutive-use failure, so such a board can hold it up forever, and the answer is Marshadow: Spectral Thief and Technician Shadow Sneak are single-target Ghost moves a Wide Guard slot never touches and that hit those bodies for double. The four single-target lanes and the Body Press wall do not need it lifted at all.

## E0511 TRAINER_LEAF_ALTERING_CAVE class=rival
strategy: SUN,REDIRECTION
mega_slots: 6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the Kanto challenger in Altering Cave - six Pokemon from a different region's memory, led by sun and ended by the strongest Psychic attacker in the series. First two useful turns: Ninetales establishes Drought on entry, so Solar Beam needs no charge turn and Encore can lock a wasted opposing action; Kangaskhan opens with Fake Out or immediate Scrappy pressure, which lets its Normal attacks hit Ghost types that would otherwise be immune. Turn two pivots to whichever support and threat pair the opening revealed. Alternate deployment: Chlorophyll Tangrowth is already at doubled Speed in its own sun and uses Rage Powder to hold the field for Mega Mewtwo Y's special offence, or Sleep Powder to remove a threat outright. Reserves & disruption: Gengar supplies Icy Wind speed control and Sludge Bomb, and Snorlax is the immovable object - Thick Fat halves incoming Fire and Ice damage, Recycle restores its consumed Leftovers, and its HP is large enough to absorb the burst the player was saving for Mewtwo. Rage Powder has ordinary target restrictions and cannot protect against spread attacks. Could this team be cooler? Yes, and one slot changed for both theme and duplication. Zeraora is not a Kanto Pokemon and it duplicated Vincent's Mega two doors earlier, so it was doing double damage to the fight's identity. Snorlax replaces it with the most recognisable Kanto body in the series, gives the roster the physical wall it completely lacked, and adds a genuinely memorable interaction in Recycle - a Snorlax that keeps eating its own Leftovers is a wall the player has to actually kill rather than outlast. The team now reads as exactly what it is: a Kanto champion's party, unchanged in spirit, ending on Mega Mewtwo Y. Overrepresented? Snorlax, Kangaskhan's base Scrappy set, Tangrowth and Mega Mewtwo Y are unique in the campaign; Ninetales's Drought recurs with Vito and the Magma arc at lower tiers; Gengar's other owners are the route ace Mollie and Phoebe's League Mega, all with different items and roles. Levels: cap 100, offsets 1/1/1/1/1/2, scaled to the live cap rather than the discarded fixed level-100 floor.
crack: Take the sun and Solar Beam needs a charge turn while Tangrowth loses half its Speed. Rock and Water attacks answer Ninetales; Fighting answers Kangaskhan and Snorlax; Fire, Ice and Flying answer Tangrowth; Dark and Ghost answer Mewtwo. Safety Goggles ignores both powders, spread damage ignores Rage Powder, and Mega Mewtwo Y is extraordinarily frail on the physical side.

## E0512 TRAINER_STEVEN class=rival
strategy: SETUP,TAILWIND
mega_slots: 3,6
ai: Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the Steel champion's exhibition team - a steel spine and three of the heaviest legends in the series, and the one fight where the former Champion brings two stones. First two useful turns: Skarmory chooses Tailwind only when Rayquaza's attack or a safe Dragon Dance actually benefits, and Body Press otherwise, converting its own enormous Defence into damage behind Sturdy; Rayquaza attacks, with Air Lock suppressing every weather effect on the field for as long as it is out - which quietly turns off a player's rain, sun or sand team without spending a move. Turn two attacks through the speed window or brings the appropriate heavy reserve. Alternate deployment: Mega Mewtwo X is the exhibition's second stone and the reason this fight is not a Steel wall - Psychic/Fighting off a 190 Attack stat, Psycho Cut and Drain Punch as its two single-target lanes, Ice Beam for the Ground and Dragon bodies a Steel team otherwise struggles with, and Drain Punch giving it back the health that Life Orb rosters pay for. Steven is licensed for two Megas, so Metagross does not have to wait for Mewtwo to fall. Reserves & disruption: Dialga is the special coverage lane with Pressure doubling the PP cost of every move aimed at it. The Mega designation belongs to Metagross alone - Rayquaza's move-based eligibility must never be allowed to create a second transformation for the same trainer, and the team is built assuming Rayquaza stays untransformed. Steven's fixed allied party in the Mossdeep multi battle is a separate roster and must never be replaced with this exhibition team. Could this team be cooler? The composition is right and stays: Air Lock as free weather suppression, Storm Drain protecting a Steel giant from its worst matchup, and a Mega Metagross closer is a legible, characterful Steven team, and the restraint of one Mega among two legends is what keeps it from being a list. What the block needed was the amendment made explicit rather than pending - Metagross is now the designated Mega in both prose and slot - plus post-game level offsets so the exhibition is a real fight. Overrepresented? Skarmory, Rayquaza, Cradily, Melmetal and Dialga are unique in the campaign; Mega Metagross recurs in the earlier Magma and multi-battle blocks, which is correct - it is Steven's signature and the player has seen it fighting alongside them before meeting it across the field. Levels: cap 100, offsets 1/2/1/2/2/3. Optional exhibition only; never a story gate.
crack: Fire, Fighting and Ground attacks answer the Steel core; Ice, Dragon and Fairy answer Rayquaza and Dialga; Dark and Ghost attacks answer Mega Mewtwo X, and Psychic and Fighting are both resisted by the Ghost bodies it cannot touch. Air Lock means your own weather plan is off while Rayquaza is out, so remove it first. Skarmory's Sturdy guarantees it one turn, and Metagross is fast but not bulky enough to survive a super-effective hit.

## E0513 TRAINER_CYNTHIA_1 class=leader
strategy: REDIRECTION,SETUP
mega_slots: 6
ai: Double Ace Pokemon
plan: Concept: the last fight in the campaign. Cynthia brings her own team - the Sinnoh partners the player will recognise on sight - with one future-paradox legend and a custom Mega Garchomp to finish. First two useful turns: Togekiss uses Follow Me to pull a single-target attack away from Spiritomb or Lucario, or Air Slash, whose flinch chance Serene Grace doubles to 60% - the highest-value single click on the board; Milotic attacks or recovers, and Competitive doubles its Special Attack the moment any stat of its is lowered, so an Intimidate or a Snarl aimed at her lead hands her the fight. Turn two brings a real attacker rather than repeating the support. Alternate deployment: Togekiss supports Miraidon's special pressure after the deliberate Electric Terrain transition - Hadron Engine sets the terrain on entry and raises Miraidon's own Special Attack by a third while it is up, and Electro Drift does extra damage against anything it is super effective against. Reserves & disruption: Spiritomb has no type weaknesses at all and its Infiltrator ignores screens and Substitutes; mixed Choice Scarf Lucario is the surprise speed tier, capable of both Close Combat and Aura Sphere; Mega Garchomp Z is a custom form whose stats and ability must be read from source rather than imported from the ordinary Mega. Note that Electric Terrain prevents grounded Pokemon from falling asleep, which is exactly why this team carries no sleep move - the terrain phase and the support phase do not fight each other. Could this team be cooler? It is cooler now for one specific reason. The authored version paired Roserade's Sleep Powder with Miraidon's Electric Terrain, which makes that Sleep Powder fail against every grounded target on the field - the team's own ace disabled its own lead, and the block could only flag the conflict rather than fix it. Replacing Roserade with Milotic resolves it completely, restores one of Cynthia's most recognisable partners, and swaps a fragile sleep gamble for a Competitive trap that punishes the Intimidate and Snarl leads a player brings to a physical-looking team. The result is a final boss whose six members are all hers and whose two phases no longer contradict. Overrepresented? Spiritomb, Lucario, Miraidon and Mega Garchomp Z are unique in the campaign; Togekiss is Cynthia's alone now that Wally is rebuilt around his own partners; Milotic's other owner is Champion Wallace's Mega, which is the right contrast - the Champion's Milotic is a Mega wall, hers is a Competitive attacker; Garchomp's other owner is Caroline's sand team in Victory Road. Levels: cap 100, offsets 2/2/3/3/3/4 - the highest-level opponents in the game, and deliberately so.
crack: Do not lower Milotic's stats. Spread damage ignores Follow Me and kills Togekiss, which is the whole support plan. Replace the terrain and Miraidon loses a third of its damage; Ground attacks answer it outright and are immune to nothing it has. Fairy and Ice answer Garchomp, Fighting and Fairy answer Lucario, and Spiritomb has no weaknesses but also very little Speed - Trick Room, Taunt and sustained pressure beat it rather than type advantage.
